## [ProcReverser: Multi-Agent System Automates Black-Box Microarchitectural Reverse Engineering for Spectre Vulnerability Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20827) ⭐️ 8.0/10

ProcReverser is a multi-agent system that automates black-box microarchitectural reverse engineering to identify processor properties relevant to Spectre-class vulnerabilities. It achieves Pass@1 and Pass@5 scores of 81.3% and 93.8% on the XuanTie C910, and 83.0% and 100.0% on the gem5 RISC-V O3 CPU model, and also characterizes properties on the SiFive P550. This work demonstrates that LLM-based multi-agent systems can automate a traditionally manual and expert-intensive security analysis process, potentially making processor vulnerability assessment faster and more scalable. It could significantly aid hardware security researchers and defense evaluation by reducing the effort required to characterize microarchitectural behaviors linked to speculative execution attacks. ProcReverser uses execution feedback to iteratively improve experimental plans and test programs, and identifies microarchitectural properties from measurements. The evaluation covers a comprehensive suite of property identification tasks focused on speculative execution, branch prediction, memory dependence, and cache behavior, but the approach currently relies on the availability of execution feedback and may require adaptation for processors with limited observability.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Spectre-class vulnerabilities are a family of side-channel attacks that exploit speculative execution and branch prediction in modern processors, allowing attackers to leak sensitive data. Microarchitectural reverse engineering is the process of inferring internal processor design details—such as cache hierarchy, branch predictor behavior, and speculation mechanisms—from black-box measurements, which is crucial for understanding and mitigating such vulnerabilities. However, this process traditionally requires substantial manual effort to design experiments and analyze results for each processor. ProcReverser automates this using a multi-agent system, where multiple AI agents collaborate to plan experiments, generate test programs, and interpret measurements.

## [SAR vehicle benchmarks rely on background shortcuts, study finds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21827) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper shows that deep networks on SAR vehicle benchmarks like MSTAR and ATRNet-STAR achieve high accuracy even when the vehicle is removed, indicating reliance on background shortcuts. Specifically, with the central 96x96 pixels zeroed out, MSTAR accuracy remains 73.3% (chance 10%), and on ATRNet-STAR's 40-class SOC split, accuracy is 63.3% (chance 2.5%) with the vehicle removed. This work exposes a critical flaw in widely used SAR ATR benchmarks, where high scores may reflect background memorization rather than true vehicle recognition. It urges the community to report background-only and target-only controls alongside accuracy, impacting model evaluation and robustness in remote sensing. Under the official scene-change split, background stops helping and starts hurting: accuracy is 22.8% on full chips but 30.8% when only the vehicle is shown. The SAR foundation model SARATR-X relies on background more than ConvNeXt (+4 to +6 points with vehicle removed) and does not solve scene change; accuracy is mainly set by the gap to the nearest training depression angle (57% at 15°, 39% at 30°, 26% at 45°), and adding more training angles does not help.

**Background**: SAR (Synthetic Aperture Radar) uses radar waves to image the ground, and automatic target recognition (ATR) aims to identify vehicles from these images. MSTAR is a classic benchmark with about 10 vehicle classes collected under controlled conditions, while ATRNet-STAR is a newer, larger dataset with 40 classes across multiple scenes. Shortcut learning occurs when models exploit spurious correlations, such as background clutter, instead of learning the actual target.

## [DNeo introduces MoFA to improve feature encoding in parallel draft models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21925) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper proposes Mixture-of-Features Attention (MoFA), a lightweight feature-encoding approach that addresses lossy projections, memorylessness, and uniform layer sampling in parallel draft models for speculative-diffusion decoding. The resulting architecture, DNeo, achieves 1.3x faster generation speed than the state-of-the-art DFlash-2 without impacting drafter footprint or generation quality. This work advances speculative decoding, a key technique for accelerating LLM inference, by improving the quality of draft tokens and thus the acceptance rate. Faster inference with no quality loss can directly benefit deployment of large language models in latency-sensitive applications. DNeo incorporates MoFA with a working memory that encodes previously rejected proposals, plus 2-tap causal convolutions, prefix-only cross-attention, and non-uniform target layer selection. The paper identifies a 'residual mixing' effect that makes standard encoding projections destructive to the target feature space.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Speculative decoding speeds up LLM inference by using a small draft model to propose multiple tokens, which a larger target model then verifies in parallel. Parallel draft architectures like DFlash and DSpark generate multiple draft tokens in one forward pass and condition on features from the target model via feature encoding. However, standard feature encoding has limitations that reduce draft quality.

## [Commitment-Verdict Gap Explains Harmful Overthinking in Reasoning Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21946) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces a commitment-verdict gap framework showing that final-correctness probes alone cannot decide when a reasoning model should stop, and measures this on DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-1.5B over MATH-500. At 379 points where the model holds a correct answer, continuing to think loses 3.6 accuracy points on average (95% CI [2.2, 5.2]). This work provides a theoretical lens for the overthinking problem, showing that declining accuracy with longer budgets often comes from which chains remain rather than from chains getting worse. It suggests that stopping-rule research should measure commitment and verdict separately, which could improve inference efficiency for reasoning LLMs. The commitment-verdict gap equals the expected remaining change in answer quality and the accuracy gained by stopping; a second identity splits accuracy-versus-budget curves into within-chain answer change and change in which chains remain. Across six reasoning models from three families on MATH-500 and OlympiadBench with budgets up to 16,384 tokens, an oracle stopping rule gains at most +1.8 and +2.5 points at 1.5B and 7B.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Reasoning models like DeepSeek-R1 generate long chains of thought, and prior work has observed that they can sometimes reach a correct answer and then talk themselves out of it, a phenomenon called overthinking. Final-correctness probes estimate the probability that the final answer is correct, but this paper argues that such probes cannot capture whether stopping now would preserve a currently correct answer. MATH-500 is a 500-problem subset of the MATH benchmark of competition-level mathematics, commonly used to evaluate reasoning models.

## [DiscoveryAgent: A Scientist-Centric Framework for AI-Driven Research](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21950) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DiscoveryAgent is a domain-agnostic, scientist-centric AI agent system that combines autonomous investigation with human hypothesis steering and checkpoint-based audit agents. It achieved the best variable-star classifier among evaluated baselines and, with scientist steering, a protein variant-effect predictor surpassed by only one of 106 ProteinGym baselines. This framework addresses the central challenge of balancing autonomous AI investigation with scientists' ability to inspect and redirect ongoing work, potentially expanding scientists' empirical reach while centering their authority over research direction. It demonstrates a scalable form of oversight for agentic research through audit agents that autonomously identify errors at higher rates than previous AI scientist agents. Scientists collaborating with audit agents identified 14x more valid errors versus manual review, and the system was demonstrated in three case studies across physical science, social science, and life science, including a multimodal photometric-redshift estimator achieving 0.971 AUROC for low-redshift classification. The checkpointing system enables audit agents to trace claims and reproduce analyses, though the framework's performance may depend on the quality of human steering and the domain-specific implementation.

## [KV-LTM: Persistent Per-Session KV Caches as Long-Term Memory for Personal Assistants](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21998) ⭐️ 7.0/10

KV-LTM proposes a memory tier that stores each conversation session's raw text along with its KV cache under a fixed reader model, answering queries by restoring cached sessions and appending the question to avoid recomputation. The paper specifies the design, analytically sizes storage (0.34–3.6 TB per user-year for 30K tokens/day) and defines an evaluation protocol that emulates cache reuse without requiring a persistent-cache implementation. This work addresses a key inefficiency in LLM-based personal assistants: the need to re-prefill long histories for each query, which is costly and can lose information. By trading storage for avoided compute and preserving write-time fidelity, KV-LTM could enable more accurate and efficient long-term memory, impacting assistant developers and inference infrastructure. Storage per token ranges from approximately 31K to 328K bytes across pinned BF16 full-attention (GQA) and latent-cached MLA configurations, while the KDA+MLA hybrid uses about 8.1K bytes/token plus a fixed per-session recurrent term. The design degrades gracefully to text and is complementary to retrieval and indexing, with per-query cost modeled as regime-conditional transfer, attention, decode, aggregation, and compute.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: KV cache is a standard technique in LLM inference that stores key and value tensors from previous tokens to avoid recomputing attention, speeding up generation. LongMemEval is a benchmark for evaluating long-term memory in chat assistants, and GQA (Grouped-Query Attention) is an attention variant that reduces KV cache size by sharing key/value heads across query groups.

## [Formal Bridge Between Counterfactual and Abductive Explanations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22010) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper formally characterizes closest-world counterfactual explanations as a disjunctive normal form over all minimally distant counterexample instances and introduces conjunctive counterfactual explanations (CFXps), a subclass restricted to conjunctions of feature literals that guarantee prediction change. It establishes structural results linking contrastive explanations (CXps), abductive explanations (AXps), and CFXps, showing that every CXp induces an AXp for its witness instance and that every CXp together with a witness AXp induces a CFXp whose support is determined by that AXp. This work bridges two important subfields of explainable AI—counterfactual explanations and formal XAI—by providing a unified theoretical framework that connects previously separate notions. The structural results enable a modular approach for computing CFXps using existing AXp/CXp machinery, potentially making counterfactual explanations more efficient and guaranteeing prediction change beyond a single instance. Experiments on benchmarks using tree ensembles and neural networks show that CFXp extraction is practically efficient and generally requires no more feature changes than popular point-based counterfactual explainers. The theoretical results yield a modular approach for computing CFXps using existing AXp/CXp machinery, leveraging the minimal hitting-set duality.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Counterfactual explanations describe the smallest change to the world that can be made to obtain a desirable outcome, often formalized under closest-world semantics. Formal XAI typically relies on feature-based abductive explanations (AXps) and contrastive explanations (CXps), which admit efficient computation and a minimal hitting-set duality. This paper connects these two perspectives by introducing conjunctive counterfactual explanations (CFXps) and establishing structural relationships among CXps, AXps, and CFXps.

SkillBuffBench is a new benchmark and paired evaluation protocol that treats Skill access as a three-level factor (no Skill, a curated Skill, and automatic invocation from an installed library), crossed with memory on/off, four agent harnesses, and multiple models from Anthropic, Google, OpenAI, and Chinese open-weight providers. The paper describes the benchmark design, evaluation protocol, and initial tasks in materials science and biology, with evaluation still in progress and results to be added later. Agent Skills are now shipped by every major agent harness, but evidence for their benefit comes mostly from idealized settings where the right Skill is hand-picked and the agent starts fresh. SkillBuffBench addresses this gap by measuring Skill contribution under realistic deployment conditions, which could inform how developers design, select, and evaluate Skills in coding agents. Every run is marked by a deterministic verifier, uncertainty is reported with a paired task-level bootstrap, and cost, agent rounds, and wall-clock time are reported alongside the score rather than folded into it. The task suite currently covers materials science and biology and will be extended on a rolling basis.

## [More Reasoning Effort Boosts LLM Shopping Agents' Confidence, Not Correctness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22033) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper benchmarks ten frontier LLMs on three product-choice benchmarks built from WebShop, Amazon's SQID, and Wayfair's WANDS, finding that increasing reasoning effort does not statistically improve purchase decision quality but does significantly raise stated confidence. The authors also show that most models are poorly calibrated at every effort level and that errors are strongly shared across models. This counterintuitive result has direct implications for agent design and trust calibration: developers cannot assume that more test-time compute yields better shopping decisions, and users may be misled by inflated confidence. It underscores the need for explicit calibration and error-diversity checks when deploying LLM agents in e-commerce. The study measures calibration by comparing reported confidence percentages with true correctness rates and finds most models miscalibrated at all effort levels. It also reveals correlated errors: when one model fails a task, several others tend to fail the same task, and on tasks where multiple models err, many pick the same wrong product.

**Background**: LLM-based shopping agents are increasingly deployed to process multimodal product listings and recommend purchases. WebShop is a simulated e-commerce environment for benchmarking agents, while SQID and WANDS are product-search relevance datasets from Amazon and Wayfair respectively. Calibration refers to how well a model's stated confidence matches its actual accuracy, a key property for trustworthy autonomous systems.

## [MedGuardBench: A Patient-Oriented Benchmark for Personalized Guardrails](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22069) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced MedGuardBench, a drug-disjoint benchmark of 5,001 scenarios across 87 medications for patient-conditioned medication guarding, and proposed personalized reasoning guardrails with three components: Personalized Reasoning Distillation, Context Unrolling, and a rule-based reward (guided GRPO). This work addresses a critical gap in AI safety by showing that standard guardrails ignore patient-specific context and rely on surface cues, which could lead to unsafe medical advice; the proposed personalized approach improves accuracy and reduces false alarms, impacting healthcare AI deployment. The benchmark reveals that standard guardrails rely on wording and profile presence rather than patient facts; Personalized Reasoning Distillation links risks to patient fields, Context Unrolling makes models check each field, and guided GRPO improves unsafe recall on some backbones but increases false alarms on others. Profile removal and swapping confirm gains come from patient information, not fine-tuning alone.

**Background**: AI guardrails are safety mechanisms that monitor and filter model outputs to prevent harm, but current systems typically judge content in isolation without considering individual user context. In healthcare, where the same medication can be safe for one patient and dangerous for another due to allergies or interactions, personalized guardrails are essential. MedGuardBench evaluates whether guardrails can condition on patient profiles to make accurate safety verdicts.

## [Five-Level Reversibility Classes Proposed for AI Agent Tool Actions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22075) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.22075) introduces Reversibility Classes, a declared five-level vocabulary with argument-sensitive rules and conservative defaults, backed by a pinned implementation and registry covering 536 tools across 20 MCP servers. In 2,158 recorded runs, the implemented session guard gated the associated action in all 29 attributable attack-positive runs, versus 18 for a read/write proxy, while adding nine approval points across 1,209 benign runs. Current agent permission models grant tool access without assessing whether a mistake can be undone, so this taxonomy addresses a real gap in agent safety design. By making recovery assumptions explicit and testable, it could influence how MCP-based agents gate risky actions such as file writes, database changes, and public posts. All 738 rule-case checks conformed to declared expectations, but native annotation mapping differed from draft registry labels for 118 of 481 comparable tools, and the single AI-assisted reviewer approved 148 ratings with only 75 matching the registry, which the authors note is not independent agreement. Controlled experiments on files, Git, SQLite, and three browsers show recovery depends on captured prior state, later changes, editor storage, and recovery route, and expose a Git merge-state exception; the paper does not establish registry-wide accuracy or reduced production harm.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Tool-using AI agents can modify files, databases, access rights, and public information, but existing permission systems typically only check whether an agent may call a tool, not whether the resulting change is reversible. The Model Context Protocol (MCP) is a standardized interface through which AI applications connect to external tools and data sources via MCP servers, making tool-level safety controls increasingly important. Reversibility classification is an emerging idea in agent security: read-only actions need no rollback, while irreversible actions such as sending an email or running a destructive migration warrant stricter gating.

## [EvoDisco: Evolutionary Strategy Discovery for LLM Agent Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22102) ⭐️ 7.0/10

EvoDisco introduces a learnable policy that selects and instantiates one of four semantic operators—Abstract, Refine, Bridge, and Reframe—to evolve LLM agent memory, replacing fixed operator schedules. The policy is trained via a bi-level fitness signal combining retrieval-quality improvement and strategic efficacy, and it outperforms MemP and MemRL on ALFWorld, WebShop, and LifelongAgentBench. This work shifts agent memory evolution from static, hand-designed update rules to a learned, adaptive strategy, which could enable LLM agents to manage long-term experience more effectively in dynamic environments. The gains on ALFWorld (+8.8 online / +12.9 frozen-transfer over MemP) suggest that learning when and how to transform memory is as important as what is stored. The bi-level fitness consists of an inner signal scoring each transformation by retrieval-quality improvement regularized by memory complexity, and an outer signal scoring strategic efficacy over a window of past decisions using historical operator-utility regret, utility-prediction error, and selection diversity. Ablations confirm that both fitness levels and the operator library contribute to the gains, though the method is evaluated under a one-pass online plus frozen-transfer protocol and remains an early arXiv preprint.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: LLM agents often accumulate experience in a memory store, and recent methods update this memory online to improve future reasoning. However, both the operators that transform memory (e.g., compressing or linking entries) and the rules that select them are typically fixed by design. EvoDisco treats memory evolution as an evolutionary strategy discovery problem, where the agent learns a policy to choose and instantiate semantic operators based on the current memory state and recent task history.

## [MathEye refines LLM-generated Lean proofs via angelic execution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22103) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MathEye introduces angelic proof checking, an optimistic execution model that replaces failing proof fragments with placeholders to isolate unresolved proof obligations, then uses MaxSAT optimization over a proof dependence graph to select a minimal set of essential obligations for human resolution. Experiments on 30 benchmarks show MathEye solves 93.3% of problems while reducing user interactions by 55% compared to addressing every placeholder directly. This work bridges LLM-based proof generation and formal verification by salvaging imperfect proof scripts, potentially making LLMs more useful for mathematicians working with Lean. It reduces human effort in interactive theorem proving, which could accelerate formalization of mathematics and increase trust in AI-generated proofs. The angelic proof checking replaces failing fragments with placeholders while preserving the overall argument structure, and the residual analysis formulates obligation selection as a MaxSAT problem to minimize human effort. The approach is evaluated on 30 real-world benchmarks in analysis and algebra, but relies on some human interaction to resolve essential obligations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Lean is a proof assistant and functional programming language based on dependent type theory, used for formalizing mathematics and verifying proofs. LLMs can generate Lean proof scripts that capture high-level structure but often fail on complex theorems due to missing side conditions or misapplied lemmas. Angelic verification, inspired by earlier work in static assertion checking, allows optimistic reasoning by treating unknown values as angelic (i.e., existentially quantified) to avoid false alarms. Proof obligations are conditions that must be discharged to complete a proof, and in tactic-based proving, they are progressively eliminated.

## [RegimeCouncil: Paired-Evidence Routing for Memory Conflict Governance in LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22113) ⭐️ 7.0/10

RegimeCouncil introduces a lightweight paired-evidence router that dynamically arbitrates between local and global memory suppression scopes in LLM agents to handle stale facts under delayed feedback. The paper proves that neither fixed policy is sufficient and shows that RegimeCouncil outperforms the better fixed-scope policy by 5.2-7.3 points on primary disagreement episodes across LongMemEval, TempLAMA, and TAQA benchmarks. This work addresses a critical but underexplored problem in LLM agent memory management: how to govern conflicts when stored facts become stale and feedback is delayed. The proposed online routing approach could improve the reliability and personalization of long-term memory in agents without requiring LLM retraining, impacting applications like conversational assistants and personalized agents. RegimeCouncil uses a Local Advisor that tracks outcomes on the queried fact alone and a Global Advisor that pools recent outcomes across all stored facts, with a Meta Governor that defaults to the Local Advisor and switches to the Global Advisor only when global pooling accumulates a strictly positive recent utility margin. Ablations show that removing any of its three modules reduces accuracy by 2-7 points, and the method is evaluated with Qwen3-30B-A3B and GPT-4o across 30 held-out settings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: LLM agents often rely on long-term memory to personalize responses, but stored facts can become outdated as the world changes. When an agent acts on stale memory, it may only learn of the error through delayed feedback, raising the question of how far that negative feedback should propagate—whether to suppress memory use only for the same fact (local) or across all facts (global). Existing systems typically fix one scope at design time, but this paper formulates the online choice as memory conflict governance and proves that fixed policies are insufficient.

## [Pupavision: A $45-75 Agentic AI Instrument for Monitoring Drosophila Pupariation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22136) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers present Pupavision, a low-cost (~$45-75) physical AI instrument built on a single-board Linux computer and a day/night camera that runs a frontier coding agent's agentic harness directly on the device to autonomously monitor Drosophila pupariation, capture images, and self-repair errors. In a 26-day pilot in a fly incubator (~3,580 frames), the agent assisted image capture and checked the experiment daily, while a deterministic capture service recorded one image every ten minutes and continued during an 11-day agent logout and a 51-hour outage. This work demonstrates a practical blueprint for do-it-yourself agentic instruments that put frontier intelligence on tiny, inexpensive hardware, potentially democratizing automated biology experiments for labs with limited resources. It also highlights a layered design pattern where deterministic code ensures reliable data capture underneath the AI agent, addressing trust and robustness concerns in agentic systems. The agent runs its harness on the single-board computer but the model thinks in the cloud, and it writes code to repair errors and pings the researcher's mobile phone if recording stops. The pilot included an 11-day period when the agent was logged out and a 51-hour outage that motivated the layered design; future work will validate lifecycle tracking accuracy against expert-labeled data and add real-time computer vision.

## [Readie uses learned snapshot sets to cut AI agent sandbox startup latency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22171) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.22171) proposes Readie, a code sandbox system for AI agents that replaces a single fat snapshot with a learned set of compact, pre-warmed startup snapshots. Evaluated on 15K public and LLM-synthesized scripts, Readie reduces startup latency by 3–22× and end-to-end runtime by 1.3–3.3× compared to existing approaches at the same storage budget. Sandbox startup latency is a real bottleneck for AI agents that iteratively generate and execute code, and Readie's learned snapshot-set approach offers a practical way to keep execution fast without inflating storage. This could benefit agent infrastructure providers and researchers building low-latency code execution systems. Readie formulates snapshot construction as an optimization problem and solves it with an approximation algorithm to learn a set of small snapshots that jointly cover most agent-generated workloads. Each snapshot initializes quickly and covers a fraction of code, trading a single fat snapshot for a learned collection that balances compactness and coverage.

Existing sandboxes suffer from slow startup because it is hard to build a compact, pre-warmed snapshot that covers nearly all required artifacts (e.g., packages, datasets) across diverse agent-generated code. Readie's insight is that while a single snapshot struggles to be both compact and broadly covering, a set of snapshots can achieve both.

## [Attribution-based pruning of reasoning LLMs is fragile, study finds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22234) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.22234) demonstrates that attribution-guided pruning for reasoning language models is unreliable because scoring final-answer tokens saturates (NLL of 10^-4–10^-3), and using this target for head pruning causes accuracy drops of 27.6 and 32.2 points compared to scoring reasoning tokens on two models tested with five seeds. The paper also finds that causal importance is not localizable to single neurons and that contrastively isolated 'reasoning-specific' importance is not separable from generic importance. This work challenges a widely assumed premise in attribution-guided pruning of reasoning models, showing that common practices can severely degrade accuracy while perplexity remains misleadingly stable. It has direct implications for model compression research and practitioners seeking to prune chain-of-thought models without destroying their reasoning abilities. The study identifies three failure modes: answer-token scoring saturates and misguides pruning; single-neuron effects are at the measurement floor and cancel out (group ablation effect disagrees in sign with summed member effects in 90% of groups), with reproducible importance emerging only at groups of ~100 neurons; and protecting contrastively isolated reasoning-specific importance is worse than protecting raw importance. A pre-registered hypothesis that distillation-induced concentration predicts pruning cost was not supported, and the authors recommend scoring reasoning tokens, attributing at coarse granularity, and evaluating with accuracy rather than perplexity.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Attribution-guided pruning scores model components by their causal contribution and protects the important ones, and it is increasingly used to compress reasoning language models that produce chain-of-thought (CoT) reasoning. Chain-of-thought prompting elicits step-by-step reasoning in LLMs, improving performance on complex tasks. Negative log-likelihood (NLL) is a common measure of model confidence, but in CoT models the final answer often appears within the reasoning, leading to very low NLL for answer tokens.

A new arXiv paper (2611.22245) introduces a benchmark and evidence-flow profiler for studying multi-agent memory inconsistency using controlled factual conflicts in Harvey LAB document-review tasks. Pooled across three models with comparable generation spending, requiring delegation increases retrieval of both conflicting claims from 50.2% to 68.7% relative to single-agent execution, yet conflict detection falls from 16.2% to 13.2%. This finding is counterintuitive and important for multi-agent design: delegation improves evidence coverage but impairs the lead agent's ability to detect contradictions, meaning systems may retrieve more conflicting information yet fail to flag it. Workers report both claims for only 35.2% of retrieved pairs, and pooled matched comparisons show higher detection than single-agent execution when one worker reads both claims but lower detection when the claims are split across workers. ## [Fidelity Regret Quantifies Simulator Misspecification in Genetic Circuit Design](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22263) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces fidelity regret, a metric measuring how often a cheap simulator selects designs that perform poorly under higher-fidelity evaluation, and finds positive regret in 66 of 72 tested library-organism cells across a 21,362-design subspace. It also shows that spending 34% of the budget on re-scoring top cheap-simulator designs reduces mean excess regret to 0.016, compared with 0.305 for cheap-only selection. This work reveals a fundamental gap in verifier-based optimization workflows: satisfying a formal verifier does not guarantee good physical performance when the simulator used for ranking is misspecified. The proposed metric and budget-aware mitigation strategy are transferable to any domain that optimizes designs through simulators or digital twins, including reinforcement learning from verifiable feedback (RLVF) and synthetic biology. The discrepancy depends strongly on the reference axis: for the yeast library SC1C1G1T1, mean regret is 0.113 for cytometry quality but 0.801 for growth burden, and every evaluated library-circuit-size pair has positive burden regret. Even an oracle whitelist retaining at least 25% of the design space leaves positive regret in 30 of 52 cells, and design-space optimization with frozen language models reaches high-regret regions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: In genetic circuit design, a topological verifier can confirm that a program is a valid circuit computing the target function, but ranking its physical performance requires a simulator. RLVF (reinforcement learning from verifiable feedback) assumes the verifier evaluates the quantity of interest, yet cheap simulators may misrank designs relative to physical measurements. Fidelity regret measures the reference simple regret incurred when a cheap simulator selects the design, providing a way to detect this gap before optimization.

## [DACE: Diversity-Driven Adversarial Co-Evolution for Robust LLM Safety Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22278) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DACE introduces a diversity-driven adversarial co-evolution framework that jointly addresses attacker strategy collapse and defender adversarial forgetting in LLM safety alignment. It couples a two-dimensional strategy space with a normalized marginal coverage gain reward for the attacker, and a Bayesian adversarial replay pool with Thompson sampling for the defender. This work tackles two compounding pathologies that undermine existing co-evolutionary safety alignment pipelines, potentially leading to more robust LLM defenses against evolving adversarial attacks while preserving general capabilities. It could impact researchers and practitioners developing safety alignment methods for large language models. DACE's attacker uses a two-dimensional strategy space (risk category × attack style) with a bounded, non-vanishing exploration signal, while the defender maintains a Bayesian replay pool with Beta-Bernoulli threat posteriors refreshed by time decay and sampled via Thompson sampling. Experiments show improved robustness to out-of-distribution attacks and broader attacker strategy coverage across safety, automated-attacker, and general-capability benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Adversarial training for LLM safety often involves a defender model learning to resist attacks from an attacker model that evolves over time. In co-evolutionary frameworks, the attacker may overfit to a narrow set of successful strategies (attack strategy collapse), while the defender may forget how to counter earlier attacks as the attack distribution shifts (adversarial forgetting). DACE proposes a joint remedy using diversity rewards and Bayesian replay to mitigate both issues.

<a id="item-85"></a>
## [ConfGuard: Physics-Grounded Reference Monitor Secures LLM Predictive-Maintenance Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22288) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ConfGuard introduces a physics-grounded reference monitor that uses conformal prediction to validate LLM-agent predictive-maintenance decisions against degradation physics, defending against injected text and sensor perturbations. On all 10,196 C-MAPSS test windows, it reduces a covert attacker's reach on healthy assets from 0.529 to 0.046 and eliminates suppression of failing assets (0.151 to 0), while escalating only 0.027 of clean windows against a 0.05 target. This addresses a critical gap in LLM-agent security for industrial predictive maintenance: existing defenses based on data provenance and tool privilege cannot assess whether prognostic quantities like remaining useful life are physically plausible. The approach could significantly improve the safety and reliability of autonomous maintenance systems in industrial IoT, where compromised decisions can lead to costly failures or unsafe operations. The conformal monitor tests each prediction against degradation physics—capped remaining life never rises and falls at most one cycle per cycle—and calibrates its threshold over whole asset trajectories to let operators set the expected escalation rate. Behind a Qwen3-14B planner, the composed monitors hold urgency-class injection to 0.00 and the universal sensor attack to 0.12, versus 0.29 and 0.54 for a defended prompt; an adaptive attacker defeats a Mahalanobis detector on 0.504 of windows and adversarial training on 0.485.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Conformal prediction is a distribution-free uncertainty quantification technique that produces statistically valid prediction intervals for any point predictor by assuming exchangeability of data. A reference monitor is a security mechanism that mediates all access requests to enforce a policy, with properties of being non-bypassable, evaluable, always invoked, and tamper-proof. Remaining useful life (RUL) estimation is a key task in predictive maintenance, where degradation physics imposes monotonic constraints on how RUL should evolve over time.

## [SpecSearch: Hardware-Aware Search for Efficient Speculative Decoding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22296) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SpecSearch is a hardware-aware search engine that jointly optimizes decoding strategy, draft length, and precision for speculative decoding, using an analytical cost model and a learned predictor to select high-throughput configurations across GPUs. It achieves an average of 2.01× the throughput of autoregressive decoding and 1.36× the throughput of standard speculative decoding across NVIDIA RTX 5090, H100, L40, and AMD MI300X GPUs when served by vLLM. Speculative decoding's optimal configuration is highly sensitive to workload and GPU, as shown by DFlash achieving 3.50× speedup at low concurrency but only 0.76× at high concurrency on RTX 5090. SpecSearch automates configuration selection without requiring deployment-GPU calibration, making efficient inference more accessible across diverse hardware and workloads. The analytical cost model captures memory and compute constraints to narrow the search space, and a Jev-based predictor using a frozen Qwen3-4B with LoRA adapters and a linear correction layer learns to select configurations on held-out GPUs. The predictor achieves 1.30× throughput improvement over standard speculative decoding and retains 97.36% of the throughput of full calibration.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Speculative decoding accelerates LLM inference by using a smaller draft model to propose tokens that a larger target model verifies in parallel, but its speedup depends on factors like draft length, precision, and concurrency. DFlash is a state-of-the-art speculative drafter that uses a lightweight block diffusion model for parallel drafting. vLLM is a popular open-source LLM serving engine that supports speculative decoding.

## [Streaming On-Policy Distillation Decouples Three Clocks for Stage-Wise Supervision](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22298) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces streaming on-policy distillation (OPD) by decoupling response generation, supervision delivery, and parameter updates into three independent clocks, allowing stage-wise control over supervision budgets, position selection, staleness handling, and update scheduling. In GSM8K experiments distilling Qwen3-4B into Qwen3-0.6B with VERL, supervised-budget matching reduced the early accuracy gap between the best test accuracies over seven common candidates from 4.55 to 2.42 points. This work offers a more flexible and efficient framework for on-policy distillation by allowing supervision to be consumed in a streaming fashion, which could reduce training costs and improve early-stage learning. It has implications for efficient LLM training, particularly in resource-constrained settings where distillation from larger to smaller models is common. The method retains the full causal prefix at each delivery and computes loss only on new positions, with a consumption protocol organizing supervision budgets and staleness. Synchronous continuation from the same checkpoint reached 73.39% best test accuracy versus 74.30% synchronous and 71.95% budget-matched, while an unpaired extended record reached 76.12%; freshness filtering and disagreement-overlap selection improved local peaks but did not establish sustained advantages.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: On-policy distillation (OPD) is a training paradigm where a student model learns from a teacher's supervision on responses that the student itself generates, combining the reliability of on-policy training with the efficiency of dense reward signals. Traditionally, response generation, supervision delivery, and parameter updates occur at the same granularity, but this paper argues they can be decoupled. VERL is an open-source reinforcement learning framework for LLMs, and Qwen3-4B and Qwen3-0.6B are models from the Qwen3 family, with the smaller model as the student.

## [Unifying Framework for Self-Improving Agent Harnesses](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22299) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This arXiv paper presents a unifying architectural and design framework for self-improving agent harnesses, pairing four editable runtime components (context and state, agent control, action interfaces, and verification and recovery) with a four-stage loop that diagnoses failures, proposes code patches, validates candidates, and deploys updates. Experiments on SWE-bench Lite show that gated modular evolution outperforms prompt reflection and full-harness rewrites across models and repositories at a fraction of the token cost. The harness, not just the model weights, determines whether a foundation model succeeds as an autonomous agent, so a framework for safely rewriting the harness itself addresses a key bottleneck in agent performance. This work could shift the field from prompt tuning toward automated runtime engineering, affecting developers building long-running coding and tool-use agents. The paper argues that full-harness rewrites overfit or break across modules, while modular updates with validation gates generalize across models and bound token cost. It also establishes defenses against prompt bloat, benchmark gaming, and evaluator tampering, and shows optimizers can discover reusable mechanisms such as pre-stop verifiers, loop breakers, and persistent memory.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: An agent harness (also called scaffolding) is the software infrastructure surrounding a large language model that manages tool use, memory, state persistence, execution environments, and feedback loops, making an agent out of a stateless model. As tasks grow multi-step and long-running, the harness becomes critical, and recent work has begun exploring how harnesses can improve themselves by inspecting execution traces and rewriting their own code.

## [LPC-MCTS Learns Pseudo-Counts to Fix Count Fragmentation in LLM Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22301) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper identifies count fragmentation in Monte Carlo tree search (MCTS) for LLM reasoning, where standard UCT assigns independent visit counts to textual branches, causing semantically equivalent reasoning strategies to repeatedly receive exploration bonuses. It proposes Learned Pseudo-Count MCTS (LPC-MCTS), which adds a lightweight Coin-Flipping Network head to the policy's hidden representations to estimate representation coverage and share exploration evidence across related branches. This work shows that a simple change to what MCTS counts can substantially improve exploration efficiency and reasoning performance, with gains of up to 4.96 percentage points on math benchmarks. It offers a practical enhancement for researchers using MCTS to boost LLM reasoning without requiring a paradigm shift. LPC-MCTS only modifies the exploration statistic in UCT, leaving the rest of the algorithm unchanged. Evaluated on 5 mathematical-reasoning and 7 search benchmarks, it consistently outperforms strong MCTS baselines; replacing standard counts at inference improves mean@16 accuracy by 3.79 points on Qwen2.5-7B and 2.71 points on Qwen3-4B, while applying LPC-MCTS during both training and inference yields gains of 4.96 and 4.37 points, respectively.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Monte Carlo tree search (MCTS) is a heuristic search algorithm that builds a search tree by iteratively simulating outcomes, and its UCT variant balances exploration and exploitation using visit counts. In LLM reasoning, MCTS treats different textual formulations of the same reasoning step as separate branches, each with its own visit count, which can waste exploration effort. Pseudo-counts, originally developed for count-based exploration in reinforcement learning, generalize visit counts to high-dimensional or non-tabular states by estimating state density.

## [IfcGym and IfcEdit: Verifiable Checkers as Rewards for Dependency-Aware IFC Editing Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22307) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces IfcGym, a CodeAct environment with four verifiable checkers (validity, intent, dependency, preservation) that jointly define strict success for IFC editing, and IfcEdit, a training pipeline that distills verified teacher episodes into a Qwen3.5-9B student via SFT and GRPO. The final student achieves 86.78% strict success on IfcEditBench (1,990 tasks on 13 held-out real projects), up from 79.15% after SFT, and a 27B model reaches 91.76%, exceeding its teacher. This work demonstrates that reinforcement learning with verifiable checker-based rewards can effectively train LLM agents for specialized domains like BIM/IFC editing, where correctness depends on propagating changes to dependent elements while preserving unrelated ones. It provides a benchmark and training recipe that could advance automated building model editing and inspire similar verifiable-reward approaches in other structured-data domains. The four checkers verify validity relative to the input model, intent, dependency, and preservation against a task specification, and their conjunction is strict success; rewarding all four yields 4.83 points more strict success than rewarding intent and validity alone. On public benchmarks BIM-Edit and BIBIMBAP, the final student scores 55.19 and 54.50, and it outperforms all prompted models except the teacher under matched tool access.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Industry Foundation Classes (IFC) is an open international standard (ISO 16739) for exchanging building information modeling (BIM) data among construction software. Editing an IFC model requires propagating changes to dependent elements while preserving unrelated entities, a task that existing LLM agents handle via prompting without learning from edit outcomes. CodeAct is an agent framework where LLMs interact with environments by executing code, and GRPO (Group Relative Policy Optimization) is a reinforcement learning algorithm that fine-tunes LLMs using group-relative rewards without a separate reward model.

## [Trie-Head and Shared-Context Attention Speed Up Wide-Beam Generative Retrieval in SGLang](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22309) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Trie-Head, a model-native trie decoding interface that stores the identifier trie as validated GPU tensors, and shared-context attention that reuses prompt key/value blocks across beams, both implemented in SGLang. On the Qualification Retrieval Platform workload at beam width 200, the combined system improves closed-loop throughput by up to 5.57x over a Python custom logit processor baseline and up to 2.63x over BF16 FlashRec. This work addresses real serving inefficiencies in generative retrieval—vocabulary-wide score tensors that are mostly discarded and repeated attention over the same prompt—without requiring model retraining. It offers practical throughput gains for production IR systems using trie-constrained beam search, which could lower latency and cost for large-scale retrieval deployments. Trie-Head avoids Python constraint callbacks and vocabulary-wide candidate buffers by scoring compact codebook candidates and streaming the full-vocabulary normalizer, preserving the original model's valid-token scores in real arithmetic. Shared-context attention reuses prompt key/value blocks across beams while preserving their private decode tails, and the evaluation covers candidate sourcing for LinkedIn Hiring Assistant and qualification retrieval.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Generative retrieval (GR) uses a language model to predict catalog entries as short semantic identifiers (SIDs) rather than matching documents via traditional inverted indexes. Trie-constrained beam search ensures these identifiers remain valid by restricting generation to paths in a trie, but wide beams (e.g., 200) create serving bottlenecks: large score tensors that are mostly discarded and redundant attention computation over the same prompt for each beam. SGLang is an open-source LLM serving framework designed for high-throughput inference, and custom logit processors (CLPs) are commonly used to enforce constraints but incur CPU-GPU coordination overhead.

## [New Framework Evaluates AI Agents for Investment Research](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22338) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.22338) proposes a domain-specific evaluation framework for AI agents performing investment research tasks, distinguishing outcome quality from execution quality and using task-specific criteria, deterministic validators, and independent LLM judges. It also introduces an evaluation-validity mechanism that checks the integrity of evaluation inputs, reference information, supporting evidence, and grading outputs, with expert review to resolve deficiencies and disagreements. This work addresses a key challenge in AI agent evaluation: the final output quality may not reflect the execution process quality, and uniform criteria are inadequate for diverse investment-research tasks. The framework could improve reliability of agent assessments in finance, impacting both AI researchers and financial practitioners who rely on AI agents for analysis. The framework combines deterministic validators (fixed logic for stable pass/fail results) with independent LLM judges (rubric-based reasoning that may vary across runs) and includes an evaluation-validity mechanism to detect integrity issues in inputs and grading. Numerical results show it can distinguish performance differences across dimensions while identifying potential reliability issues in the evaluation process; implementation and materials will be released on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: AI agents are systems that use large language models to interact with external tools and information sources to complete tasks. Evaluating such agents is difficult because their outputs are non-deterministic and the quality of the final result may not reflect the quality of the steps taken. In investment research, tasks vary widely in their need for numerical accuracy, information retrieval, and evidence, making uniform evaluation criteria inadequate. LLM-as-a-judge is a common evaluation method where a language model scores outputs based on a rubric, but it can be inconsistent, so deterministic validators are often used for stable checks.

## [AutoE2E: Open-Source End-to-End Driving Model for L2++ ADAS and L4 Robotaxis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22374) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The Autoware Foundation's Robotaxi Working Group released AutoE2E v1.0, an open-source end-to-end driving model that uses a temporal bird's-eye-view (BEV) architecture combining multi-camera perception, HD-map information, and route conditioning to predict future vehicle motion. The release includes model checkpoints, training and evaluation code, and DataModelConsole, a dashboard for visualizing experiments; on the KITScenes validation benchmark, the fine-tuned checkpoint achieves an average displacement error (ADE) of 0.745 m and a final displacement error (FDE) of 2.020 m at a three-second prediction horizon. This provides the autonomous-driving community with an inspectable, open baseline for end-to-end driving research, which is valuable for both L2++ ADAS and L4 robotaxi development. By releasing checkpoints, code, and visualization tools, it lowers the barrier for collaborative experimentation and benchmarking in a field where open, reproducible models are still scarce. The evaluated model has approximately 79.9 million parameters, of which only about 1.45 million are trainable in the evaluated configuration, and it uses a pretrained camera-BEV backbone with temporal feature fusion and a lightweight recurrent trajectory planner. The quantitative evaluation is open-loop only, and the model has not yet demonstrated validated closed-loop autonomous driving; preliminary real-vehicle research was conducted with TUM's EDGAR platform and collaboration with KIT.

## [SYMAR: Symbolic Value Functions Speed Up Probabilistic Timed Automata Verification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22404) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SYMAR introduces a symbolic value function approach for verifying probabilistic timed automata (PTAs), avoiding the region splitting and graph reconstruction used by PRISM's most capable PTA method. On PRISM benchmarks, SYMAR is up to 108x faster, solves an instance that times out with the existing method, and is markedly less sensitive to the deadline constant. This work addresses a known inefficiency in PRISM's PTA verification, potentially enabling more scalable analysis of autonomous systems with probabilistic and real-time characteristics. It could benefit researchers in formal verification and developers of safety-critical systems such as distributed agreement protocols and leader election. SYMAR represents each symbolic state with a function mapping sub-regions of clock values to probability bounds, so refinement only adds new sub-regions without splitting existing states or rebuilding transitions. A heuristic forward search guided by bounds from a single unrefined PRISM run confines effort to property-relevant fragments, and every stored interval provably contains the true probability.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Probabilistic timed automata (PTAs) model systems that exhibit probabilistic, nondeterministic, and real-time behavior, and are used in domains like communication protocols and autonomous systems. Verification typically works over symbolic states pairing a discrete location with a region of clock values, and PRISM is a widely used probabilistic model checker that supports PTA verification via methods like digital clocks and stochastic games. The challenge is that the optimal behavior often varies within a clock region, requiring refinement by splitting regions until uniform, which can be inefficient.

## [JevRSI: Recursive Self-Improvement for Cost-Effective Decision Support](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22432) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers from HKUST propose JevRSI, a System-1/System-2 collaboration framework where a fast System-1 model selects actions while an asynchronous LLM-based System-2 recursively builds and improves a modular harness of procedures, state memory, and world models. Experiments on three datasets show JevRSI matches or surpasses LLM agents in decision quality while preserving low-latency execution and amortizing System-2 costs as the harness evolves. This work addresses the latency and cost bottleneck of repeatedly invoking LLM-based reasoning, showing that recursive harness improvement can turn expensive System-2 reasoning into reusable, transferable decision support. It offers a practical path toward deploying fast, adaptive agents in real-time decision settings without sacrificing reasoning quality. The harness integrates procedures, state memory, and world models to provide decision contexts spanning past, present, and future, with utility-driven retrieval selecting modules based on contextual relevance and prior task utility. Environmental feedback guides targeted revisions, and System-2 development interventions decrease as the harness evolves, though the abstract is truncated and full experimental details are not yet available.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: System-1/System-2 thinking, inspired by Kahneman's dual-process theory, distinguishes fast, intuitive decision-making from slow, deliberate reasoning. In AI, fast models enable low-latency actions but struggle with complex tasks, while LLM-based reasoning provides strong capabilities at high computational cost. Recursive self-improvement refers to an AI system iteratively improving its own components, and here it is applied to a modular harness that supports decision-making rather than the model weights themselves.

Traditional fuzzing for IoT often relies on simulated environments that assume ideal human decisions, missing the complexity of real user behavior. Large language models have recently been used to simulate human-like decision-making in various domains, offering a way to make testing more realistic.

## [RehearsalBench tests AI's ability to forecast organizational change outcomes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22480) ⭐️ 7.0/10

RehearsalBench is a new benchmark that evaluates whether AI models can diagnose an organization's operations, choose an intervention, and accurately forecast its consequences in a simulated bank contact center. Ten models made 280 attempts across 14 organizations, with the best model, GPT-6 Astra, improving on doing nothing by 6.8% of the no-change cost and reducing forecast error by 77% compared to a baseline that always predicts no effect. This benchmark addresses an underexplored but practically important problem: helping organizations use AI to predict the outcomes of operational changes before implementing them. It could lead to better decision-support tools for business operations, affecting managers and AI researchers working on forecasting and simulation. Models see only manager-level information and submit plans that are played out across 200 seeded futures and three plausible organizational versions, with decisions scored in dollars and forecasts scored on effect size relative to doing nothing. While all models correctly predicted the direction of effects, they systematically overstated the size of a voice agent's effect on repeat contacts about five-fold, and no model ever recommended holding off when doing nothing was the right choice.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Organizations often ask AI systems to forecast the impact of changes such as automating work, resizing teams, or deploying voice agents. RehearsalBench simulates a bank contact center fitted to real call-level records, requiring models to make decisions and forecasts under uncertainty. The benchmark also tests external validity by having models forecast 33 real organizational changes with known outcomes, showing agreement with real-world rankings.

## [DARWIN: A Geospatial Foundation Model for Marine Intelligence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22496) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DARWIN is a geospatial foundation model for marine intelligence that adapts a masked-autoencoder backbone with missingness-aware encoding, expanded spatial and temporal masking, and Fourier-style positional embeddings, pretrained on 26 years of global Copernicus reanalysis and GEBCO bathymetry. On chlorophyll-a prediction, it achieves the lowest monthly relative error in every month of 2022, outperforming no-encoder baselines and a random-init encoder, and at 10% of supervised data it surpasses the random-init model trained on 100% of the data. This work extends foundation models from terrestrial to marine domains, addressing distinct challenges like missingness, multi-modal variables, and non-stationary dynamics, and provides a scalable backbone for downstream applications such as anomaly detection, species distribution modelling, and environmental monitoring. It represents an important development in Earth observation and AI for marine science, with potential impact on climate and environmental monitoring. DARWIN's pretrained backbone captures coherent oceanographic structure rather than memorizing input statistics, as shown by embedding analysis and qualitative reconstructions. The model's data efficiency is notable: with only 10% of supervised training data, it outperforms a random-init model trained on 100% of the data.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Masked autoencoders (MAEs) are self-supervised learning models that mask random portions of input data and train the model to reconstruct them, enabling learning of meaningful representations without labels. Copernicus reanalysis provides a comprehensive 4D historical record of the Earth's climate by combining observations with models, while GEBCO bathymetry is a global reference map of seafloor topography. Foundation models are large-scale models pretrained on broad data that can be adapted to many downstream tasks.

## [Two-Stage Discrete Diffusion World Model Achieves 79x Speedup and Data Efficiency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22497) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes a two-stage discrete diffusion-based world model that replaces next-token prediction with iterative masked predictions via a lightweight refinement transformer, achieving a 79x inference speedup with only a modest quality trade-off compared to an autoregressive baseline. It also demonstrates that high-quality, consistent gameplay can be generated using only about 28 hours of gameplay data. Real-time inference and data efficiency are critical barriers preventing world models from being used in interactive play and creative ideation, and this work shows both can be substantially improved. Lowering the data requirement to roughly one day of gameplay could make custom training of world models practical for smaller studios and creative fields. The approach builds on an autoregressive world model (Kanervisto et al., 2025) capable of generating consistent sequences over minutes, and replaces it with a two-stage discrete diffusion process plus a lightweight refinement transformer for iterative masked prediction. The 79x speedup comes with a modest quality trade-off, and the data-efficiency claim is based on a dataset of approximately 28 hours of gameplay.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: World models are learned generative models of an environment that predict future states conditioned on actions, enabling agents to plan and generate imagined experience. Discrete diffusion models are a class of generative models that operate over discrete state spaces (such as tokens) rather than continuous ones, using forward and reverse jump processes. Masked-prediction transformers are trained by masking input tokens and predicting them from bidirectional context, and here they are used iteratively to refine generated gameplay frames.

## [LMLineage attributes LLM agent outputs to inputs for context governance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22511) ⭐️ 7.0/10

LMLineage is an out-of-band system that attributes each output sentence of an LLM agent session to supporting input spans with calibrated confidence, without white-box access or extra inference passes. It uses this lineage to detect when previously used documents have drifted and selectively compacts only those, preserving user instructions verbatim. This work addresses two key challenges for LLM agents: interpretability of multi-source reasoning and efficient context management. By providing sentence-level attribution and targeted compaction, it could improve agent reliability, reduce context bloat, and enable incident tracing without costly model introspection. Evaluated on 71 gold annotated sentences from 10 Chinese web-QA cases and 90 turns across three generator models, the system achieves per-turn attribution in 1 second on a single GPU, remains stable up to 40 turns, and shows 0% false compaction with recall bounded by attribution accuracy. Window-level embedding similarity outperforms whole-document or lexical matching, and a greedy-window fast path is faster and more accurate than exhaustive line-matching.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: LLM agents often process long, multi-source contexts and can lose track of which inputs influenced their outputs, leading to context drift and bloated prompts. Context compaction techniques summarize or truncate context to fit within limits, but typically do so indiscriminately. Attribution methods aim to trace outputs back to inputs, but often require model internals or additional inference.

## [Stale Agent Skills Hurt Performance; Selective Maintenance Fixes It](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22525) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.22525) empirically shows that reusing agent skills after environment changes can reduce task performance by over 10 percentage points or increase execution cost by nearly 40% compared to using no skill. The authors propose selective maintenance, which replays checks from successful executions without additional model calls to verify, repair, and validate skill state, improving accuracy by up to 67pp and reducing execution steps by up to 37%. As AI agents increasingly rely on reusable skills to amortize inference-time reasoning and environment interaction, this work highlights a critical maintenance challenge that can silently degrade deployed agents. The proposed selective maintenance method offers a practical way to preserve skill value as environments evolve, which is essential for long-term agent reliability and cost efficiency. The effects depend on which environment-specific details become outdated and the agent's ability to compensate using current observations; task success can remain unchanged while execution savings decline. Selective maintenance operates without any description of the change, rebuilding skill state when local repair is insufficient, and was evaluated across four task settings and two models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Agent skills are reusable packages of instructions, scripts, and resources that AI agents load on demand to guide decisions and actions, retaining past experience in a skill state to reduce inference-time reasoning and environment interaction. When the environment changes, parts of this state can become outdated while others remain useful, creating a need for maintenance mechanisms that can detect and repair stale content without costly model calls.

## [MillBill agent uses open LLM and geometric feedback for machining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22565) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MillBill is a new agent that separates machining decisions from geometric execution: a locally hosted, mid-sized open model (Qwen3.8-27B) proposes operations and cutting parameters for indexed three-axis machining, while a geometric harness generates toolpaths, simulates material removal, and returns feedback for plan refinement. It outperforms frontier-model approaches that construct their own geometry and toolpath workflows on surface-area tolerance metrics. This work shows that a mid-sized open model paired with a geometric verification harness can beat larger frontier models on a practical CAD-to-machining task, suggesting that separating reasoning from execution is a promising architecture for AI in manufacturing. It could lower the barrier to automating CAM programming and improve reliability in toolpath generation. The agent uses boundary-representation (B-rep) parts, stock, and material as input, and setup planning accounts for what the available cutters can physically reach rather than visibility alone. Evaluation is based on the fraction of target surface area within tolerance after simulated machining, and code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Boundary representation (B-rep) is a common method for representing 3D shapes in CAD by defining the limits of the volume. Indexed three-axis machining uses a three-axis machine that can be repositioned at fixed angles to access multiple faces, avoiding the need for continuous five-axis motion. Toolpath generation is a key CAM step that translates machining operations into cutter movements, and language models have recently been explored to automate this by writing geometry and toolpath code directly.

<a id="item-103"></a>
## [PACT: Preserving Correct Plans While Repairing Erroneous Actions in GUI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22591) ⭐️ 7.0/10

PACT introduces a training framework that combines reference-aligned plan protection with plan-aligned action repair to address the plan-action execution gap in GUI agents. It masks negative policy-gradient contributions over verified plan tokens and uses a preference surrogate with stop-gradient pair weights to favor reference actions over erroneous ones, raising AndroidWorld task success from 60.34% to 68.97% (+8.63 pp) and OSWorld success from 40.92% to 44.17% (+3.25 pp). This work tackles a core credit-assignment problem in outcome-supervised reinforcement learning for LLM-based agents: a single negative advantage broadcast across all tokens can penalize correct reasoning along with faulty execution. By improving plan-action consistency, PACT could make GUI agents more reliable for multi-step mobile and desktop automation, where one execution error can derail an entire workflow. PACT uses annotated reference actions to identify execution gaps, masks negative policy-gradient contributions across the token span of the verified plan, and keeps the generated reasoning unchanged as context while applying a preference surrogate with stop-gradient pair weights. It does not alter the original scalar action reward, and it also improves rule-based plan-action consistency on AndroidControl High from 83.71% to 89.74% (+6.03 pp).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: GUI agents are LLM-based systems that interact with graphical user interfaces by perceiving screenshots and emitting actions such as clicks and typing. In outcome-supervised reinforcement learning, a single scalar reward for an entire rollout is broadcast to every generated token, creating a credit-assignment problem: the model cannot tell which tokens were responsible for success or failure. PACT addresses this by separating the plan (reasoning) from the action (execution) and protecting verified plan tokens from negative gradients when the subsequent action is wrong.

<a id="item-104"></a>
## [Continual-ARC: A Lifelong Learning Benchmark from ARC-AGI Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14505) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose Continual-ARC, a lifelong learning benchmark built from the three ARC-AGI editions, where the same tasks reappear after dormant gaps with fresh instances and new tasks keep arriving. It scores memory-based and weight-based learners on the same scale by charging a feedback budget for demonstrations, submissions, and retries, then freezing the learner to test memorization and generalization. Existing continual learning benchmarks typically focus on a single domain, serve each instance only once, and assume one learner family, making fair comparison between memory-based and weight-based systems difficult. Continual-ARC addresses these gaps and its initial results suggest weight-based learning generalizes better than harness-based methods, pointing toward future weight-based continual learning that could teach models to drive harnesses in new domains. The benchmark runs an agent harness with frozen weights, with and without persistence, on Qwen3.5-9B and GLM-5.3 Flash, comparing them against two prompting floors, a program-induction method, and Qwen3.5-9B weights trained to imitate GLM-5.3 Flash. Persistence in the harness generally does not improve memorization unless given explicit expert guidance, and most harness-based methods learn less than a simple floor that caches each task's demonstrations, while weight-based learning shows substantial gains on unseen tasks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: ARC-AGI is a benchmark designed to measure progress toward artificial general intelligence using puzzles that are easy for humans but hard for AI, and it has released multiple editions. Continual learning refers to a system's ability to incrementally acquire, update, and retain knowledge across a sequence of tasks while limiting catastrophic forgetting. Agent harnesses are external scaffolding that let a model act, retry, and persist information between instances, while test-time parametric learning updates a model's weights during inference rather than relying solely on external memory.

## [Marimba: A System One Model for Speech Decisions Using Frozen ASR Features](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18330) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Marimba introduces a system-one model that repurposes frozen Qwen3-ASR 0.6B features to perform typed speech decisions such as term presence, turn completion, language identification, and speaker change detection. It achieves high accuracy, with decoder AUROC of 0.80-0.92 on private conversational test sets and 0.92-0.97 on public read speech. This approach demonstrates that a single frozen ASR model can be efficiently adapted for multiple downstream speech tasks without fine-tuning the backbone, potentially simplifying speech application development and reducing computational costs. It could impact developers building real-time conversational agents and speech analytics tools. Marimba uses zero-shot scoring of ASR transcripts for lexical questions, a question-conditioned head for non-lexical questions, and the ASR model's own language scores for language identification. It achieves 0.93 four-way accuracy on Speech Commands and item-level F1 of 0.904 for PII detection on synthetic Brazilian Portuguese speech, but its turn-end readout (AUROC 0.930) underperforms Smart Turn v3.2 (0.982) on Smart Turn's test set.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: System-one models refer to fast, intuitive decision-making systems in contrast to slower, deliberative system-two reasoning. Qwen3-ASR is a compact open-weight speech recognition model from Alibaba, supporting 30 languages and featuring language identification. AUROC (Area Under the Receiver Operating Characteristic curve) is a metric for binary classification performance, where 1.0 is perfect and 0.5 is random. This work explores transfer learning by reusing frozen ASR features for tasks beyond transcription.

## [RAW: Causal Method Measures User-Side Cost of LLM Refusals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21641) ⭐️ 7.0/10

RAW introduces a causal measurement method that leverages LLM stochasticity as a natural experiment to estimate the user-side cost of refusals in deployed models. Applied to 15 LLMs in LMSYS-Chat-1M and WildChat, it finds that for about half of the models, a refusal reduces subsequent re-engagement by 3 to 17 percentage points. This addresses a previously unmeasured production blind spot—the user-side cost of refusals—which is critical for consumer-facing platforms built on LLMs. The method runs on any deployed model from logs alone at about $0.30 per 1,000 conversations, making it accessible to third-party platforms that cannot run A/B tests. The method uses near-identical prompts where the same model sometimes refuses and sometimes does not, identifying the causal effect via user-clustered inference. The authors release labels, moderation scores, prompt embeddings, and pipeline code; one deployment's positive point estimate was traced to persistent users retrying the refused request.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Deployed LLMs refuse a non-trivial fraction of user requests, but the user-side cost of refusal—whether users disengage and by how much—has been unmeasured on production models because A/B testing refusal is ethically and operationally constrained. LMSYS-Chat-1M and WildChat are large-scale datasets of real-world LLM conversations collected from platforms like Chatbot Arena and WildChat, providing logs for analysis. RAW uses the inherent stochasticity of LLMs as a natural experiment: at near-identical prompts, the same model sometimes refuses and sometimes does not, and this variation identifies the causal effect of refusal on user re-engagement.

## [Testing the overfitted brain hypothesis on an LLM agent with text memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21648) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.21648) tests Erik Hoel's overfitted brain hypothesis by corrupting daily conversation transcripts before nightly memory rewrites in an LLM agent (Claude Opus 5.5) over ten simulated days built from LongMemEval. Across 30 runs, corruption did not improve the agent's ability to apply earlier learning to new tasks, and replacing details in 40% of turns lowered recall from 76% to 60%. This is a creative empirical test of a cognitive science hypothesis on an AI system, bridging neuroscience, memory consolidation, and LLM agents. The negative results suggest that pretrained models can distinguish corrupted text from real events, meaning a fair test of the hypothesis requires a learner that cannot make this separation as easily. The agent's memory was capped by a size limit rising from 19,500 to 117,000 tokens over ten nights, based on human free-recall data, forcing forgetting. Random-text corruption (replacing turns with Moby-Dick passages) had no consistent effect on recall (0 ± 2 points at 40%), because the model recognized it as corrupted and excluded it, while replaced details were stored as fact even when a correct value existed elsewhere.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: The overfitted brain hypothesis (Hoel, 2021) proposes that dreams distort replayed experiences to prevent the brain from overfitting to recent events, thereby aiding generalization. LongMemEval is a benchmark for testing long-term memory in chat assistants, covering abilities like information extraction and multi-session reasoning. Memory consolidation in AI agents refers to write-side processes that merge, update, or abstract new information to keep the memory store accurate and compact.
