## [ICA Lens: Non-Gaussianity Reveals Interpretable Directions in Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11722) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces ICA Lens, a workflow that discovers interpretable directions in LLM residual-stream activations by directly searching for non-Gaussian projection distributions, rather than training sparse autoencoders (SAEs). Across GPT-2 Small, Gemma 2 2B, and Qwen 3.5 9B Base, the recovered directions capture coherent lexical, syntactic, and semantic patterns and are competitive with SAEs on interpretability, sparse reconstruction, concept prediction, and causal interventions, while fitting in minutes per layer and using 7–43 times less memory. This work offers a principled, efficient alternative to SAE-based feature discovery for mechanistic interpretability, showing that non-Gaussianity is a direct unsupervised signal for finding interpretable structure. It could significantly reduce the computational and memory costs of interpreting large language models, making feature discovery more accessible. The method exploits the fact that projection distributions along SAE decoder directions are substantially more non-Gaussian than random directions, with most contexts forming a concentrated background and recurring patterns occupying one tail. Non-Gaussianity is positively associated with human-label confidence and automated interpretability scores, and the approach fits in minutes per layer using 7–43 times less memory than SAEs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Sparse autoencoders (SAEs) are a popular tool in mechanistic interpretability for decomposing polysemantic language model activations into sparse, monosemantic features, but they require training an additional dictionary and can be computationally expensive. Independent component analysis (ICA) is a classical technique that separates multivariate signals into independent components by exploiting non-Gaussianity, since non-Gaussianity is a proxy for statistical independence. Residual-stream activations are the state vectors propagated via skip connections between transformer layers, which accumulate and integrate features across the network.

## [FoundCause: Foundation Model for Causal Discovery with Latent Confounders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.17516) ⭐️ 7.0/10

FoundCause is a new amortized causal discovery foundation model that, in a single forward pass, maps observational datasets to causal graphs while explicitly predicting latent confounders. Trained only on synthetic structural causal models, it uses a permutation-equivariant transformer and outperforms 11 classical and 7 amortized baselines on 14 real-world and semi-synthetic datasets, leading in average AUROC, F1, skeleton F1, and normalized SHD. This work addresses the challenging problem of causal discovery from purely observational data with latent confounders, which is common in real-world settings like epidemiology and economics. By amortizing inference into a single forward pass and explicitly outputting latent confounding, it could enable faster, more scalable causal analysis without costly search or interventions. The permutation-equivariant transformer alternates attention over samples and variables, guided by pairwise dependence and asymmetry statistics, and uses a masked input representation to handle missing data. The edge decoder separates edge existence from direction, and learnable latent tokens predict which variable pairs share a hidden common cause; on 14 datasets, FoundCause achieves an SHD/d of 0.875 versus 0.956 for the best baseline and an F1 of 0.531 versus 0.516.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Causal discovery aims to infer cause-effect relationships from data, but most methods assume no unobserved confounding, which is often unrealistic. Latent confounders—unobserved common causes of two or more observed variables—can induce spurious dependencies that are hard to distinguish from directed causal relations. Amortized causal discovery trains a model on many simulated causal graphs so that inference on new data requires only a single forward pass, avoiding per-dataset search. FoundCause extends this paradigm by explicitly predicting latent confounding as a separate output, a first for i.i.d. observational data.

## [Preference-based trajectory evaluation cuts ties in agentic benchmarks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.17541) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2606.17541v2) proposes preference-based trajectory evaluation, which compares pairs of agent trajectories using temporal preferences over time-to-return profiles instead of collapsing them into binary success values. Across diverse agentic and interactive benchmarks, success-based metrics produced tied comparisons on roughly 75% of instances, while trajectory-aware preferences reduced ties to roughly 35%, improving discriminative power, ranking stability, and data efficiency. This work addresses a real statistical inefficiency in offline evaluation of agentic systems: binary success metrics discard partial progress and create widespread ties, reducing effective sample size and weakening system comparisons. The findings suggest that benchmark saturation may partly stem from evaluation measure design rather than poor data collection or problem difficulty, which could influence how AI evaluation practices are designed. The method compares trajectories directly through temporal preferences over progress and time-to-return profiles, and the abstract reports empirical results across diverse agentic and interactive benchmarks. The paper is a replace-cross submission on arXiv and has been accepted as a NeurIPS 2026 poster.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Agentic systems are AI agents that take sequences of actions to accomplish tasks, and offline evaluation means assessing them on pre-collected trajectories rather than in live interaction. Traditional evaluation often reduces a whole trajectory to a single binary terminal success value, which loses information about partial progress. Preference learning is a technique for learning rankings from pairwise comparisons, and here it is applied to trajectory evaluation to produce finer-grained distinctions.

## [Space-Time Convex Sets Framework Enables Scalable Multi-Robot Motion Planning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00444) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces a framework based on graphs of space-time convex sets (ST-GCSs) for continuous spatiotemporal planning and decoupled multi-robot coordination, including an Exact Convex Decomposition (ECD) method and a best-first solver. It integrates ST-GCS planning and ECD into prioritized multi-robot planning with windowed coordination, coordinating up to 100 robots within a minute in simulation and demonstrating real-robot applicability. This work addresses the scalability challenge in multi-robot motion planning by combining convex optimization with decoupled coordination, potentially enabling efficient coordination of large robot teams in warehouses, factories, and other dynamic environments. The approach offers competitive solution quality against optimization-based and sampling-based planners while scaling to 100 robots. The ST-GCS represents collision-free space-time as convex sets, jointly optimizing region sequence and piecewise-linear trajectory geometry and timing under velocity limits. ECD removes swept occupancies of moving obstacles and previously planned robots while maintaining convex decomposition, and the best-first solver uses temporal reachability for dominance pruning and admissible heuristics with separate or joint search-key evaluation.

**Background**: Graphs of Convex Sets (GCS) is a motion planning technique that represents free space as a graph of convex regions, enabling joint optimization of discrete path choices and continuous trajectories via convex optimization. Decoupled multi-robot motion planning avoids the high-dimensional joint configuration space by planning for each robot sequentially while treating other robots as moving obstacles, but requires repeated single-robot planning where route choice and timing are coupled. Convex decomposition breaks complex non-convex free space into convex pieces, and swept occupancy refers to the volume swept by a moving obstacle over time.

## [ImageNet Contains Natural Backdoor-like Signals, Study Finds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05516) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2607.05516v3) identifies 'statistical adversaries'—naturally occurring, label-correlated signals in ImageNet that act like backdoor triggers without any malicious poisoning. Using statistical controls to remove random correlations, the authors show these signals predictably alter model predictions and transfer across different architectures. This suggests that dataset structure itself can create exploitable adversarial surfaces, meaning vulnerabilities may stem from data distribution rather than individual model quirks. It implies dataset audits should treat spurious correlations not only as bias or interpretability issues but also as latent security risks for vision models. The authors analyze ImageNet for patterns strongly linked to specific labels and apply statistical controls to filter out random correlations. The resulting statistical adversaries are more targeted than generic corruptions and transfer across model architectures, indicating dataset-driven rather than model-specific vulnerabilities.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Backdoor attacks typically involve injecting a hidden trigger into training data so a model learns to misbehave when the trigger appears. Adversarial examples are inputs crafted to fool models, and their transferability across models is a known phenomenon. ImageNet is a large-scale image dataset widely used for benchmarking computer vision models, and its single-label structure can introduce spurious correlations.

## [Activation Steering Workflow and CastVectors for Role-Conditioned LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00023) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces an activation-steering screening workflow for role-conditioned language-model agents, which defines a role profile, extracts a role-specific direction, sweeps four steering coefficients, and evaluates role-profile alignment to pass or flag each candidate configuration. It also presents CastVectors, which achieve a mean judged overall score of 63.2 versus 41.1 for an assistant-axis directional control from prior persona-vector work across the tested grid on OLMo-3-7B-Instruct. This work provides a concrete, checkable method for ensuring that LLM agents behave consistently with assigned roles before they are deployed in social simulations, which is important for the reliability and safety of simulation-based research. The finding that most roles improve with stronger steering but 38 roles decline across all six measured dimensions highlights the need for per-role coefficient selection rather than uniform high-strength settings. The evaluation uses a mixed 275-role inventory with 228 role-agnostic questions, GPT-4.1-mini prompted role references, and GPT-4.1-mini judges on OLMo-3-7B-Instruct. CastVectors also preserve high lexical diversity, whereas the assistant-axis control drops sharply at larger coefficients; code and data are released on GitHub and Hugging Face.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Activation steering is an inference-time technique that modifies a model's internal activations to control its outputs, often by adding a direction vector extracted from the model. Persona vectors are linear directions in activation space that encode high-level behavioral traits such as evil, sycophantic, or humorous, and have been used to monitor and control model character. Social simulations built from LLM agents require each agent to consistently adopt a assigned role, making role-conditioned behavior a key challenge.

<a id="item-212"></a>
## [Simulator Collapse: Single LLM Simulators Hurt MARL Generalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12253) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.12253v3) shows that multi-agent RL agents trained against a single frozen LLM user simulator suffer from 'simulator collapse' — the simulator is mode-collapsed, so the policy overfits to its narrow behavior and generalizes poorly to unseen simulators and real users. The authors propose inference-time Verbalized Sampling and training-time Co-Training (including a population variant), reporting held-out success gains of up to 9% and 14% respectively, plus similar gains with real users, and release the open-source SCOPE framework. This identifies a concrete, previously underappreciated failure mode in LLM-simulated human-AI interaction training and offers two practical mitigations, which matters for anyone building RL agents meant to serve real users. It argues that diverse simulators are a requirement rather than a nice-to-have, potentially reshaping how simulator-based MARL pipelines are designed. Verbalized Sampling samples each simulator reply from a verbalized response distribution at inference time, while Co-Training co-evolves the simulator with the policy like self-play, and a population variant samples the simulator per rollout from a pool of recent checkpoints. Results are validated on Persuasion for Good, tau^2-bench, and CooperBench, and both methods preserve the policy diversity that collapses under single-simulator RL; the work is an arXiv preprint without peer review.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: In multi-agent reinforcement learning for human-AI interaction, researchers often avoid costly human studies by training the agent against an LLM that role-plays the user. Mode collapse is a known LLM failure in which outputs lose diversity and cluster around a few high-probability responses, and prior work has explored decoding-time fixes such as Verbalized Sampling to restore variety. This paper connects the two: a mode-collapsed simulator gives the RL policy a narrow target to exploit, producing strategies that do not transfer to other simulators or people.

## [Query Timing Reverses Positional Biases in LLMs Versus Humans](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12387) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.12387v2) investigates whether the timing of belief updating—during evidence presentation versus only at the end—produces the same positional biases in LLMs as it does in humans, and finds a divergence: LLMs show opposite positional biases to humans, and these biases are more pronounced in newer models than in their predecessors. This finding challenges the common assumption that LLM biases mirror human cognitive biases, and it suggests that evaluation methodologies relying on human-like position effects may be misleading, especially as newer and supposedly more capable models exhibit stronger biases. The study focuses on primacy and recency effects and manipulates when belief updating occurs, but the abstract does not specify model families, task types, or effect sizes, so the generality of the result across architectures and benchmarks remains to be established.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Positional bias refers to the tendency of LLMs to weight information based on where it appears in a prompt rather than its content, with primacy bias favoring early information and recency bias favoring later information. In cognitive science, similar serial position effects are well documented in human judgment, and recent work shows that whether a person updates beliefs during evidence presentation or only at the end changes which effect appears. This paper tests whether that timing manipulation produces the same pattern in LLMs.

## [Sampling Luck Masquerades as Allocation Gain: Auditing Test-Time Budget Allocation for Neural Combinatorial Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13087) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper audits test-time budget allocation for neural combinatorial optimization (NCO) and finds that apparent 2.2–2.6% oracle allocation gains on uniform TSP-100 disappear out of sample (0.457, 0.015, -0.512 percent for POMO, AM, SymNCO). It also shows that under distribution shift, a corrected allocation method yields a real 11.5% (AM) and 12.0% (SymNCO) improvement in best-of-k at equal evaluation budget. This work exposes a common evaluation bias in NCO research: in-sample oracle allocation can produce false published gains, potentially invalidating prior claims and misleading the community. It provides a correction procedure and reporting checklist that could improve methodological rigor and reproducibility in the field. The bias is calibrated against an instance-wise null where true gain is zero by construction, and it does not shrink with more samples or instances over the tested ranges. The real gain under distribution shift exceeds a frozen distribution-label baseline by 4.2 points [1.9, 7.7], and an exploratory policy charging a 20-sample probe retains 3.4% (AM) and 4.6% (SymNCO).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Neural combinatorial optimization (NCO) uses neural networks to solve combinatorial optimization problems such as routing and scheduling. Constructive NCO solvers like POMO, AM, and SymNCO generate multiple solutions per instance and report the best one (best-of-k), typically using a fixed sample count for all instances. Test-time budget allocation asks whether distributing a fixed total sample budget non-uniformly across instances could improve performance, but prior work had not measured this or audited the measurement itself.

## [Cough-based TB models fail to generalize across datasets, study finds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.25846) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A systematic cross-dataset evaluation of classical machine learning and deep learning cough-based tuberculosis classifiers across three independent datasets found that despite moderate within-dataset performance (ROC-AUC up to 0.76 ± 0.06), external performance frequently fell below 0.6. The study shows that audio representations are organized by recording device and dataset rather than TB status, and that a simple clinical-variable baseline generalizes more consistently (ROC-AUC 0.65–0.71). This negative result is a methodological caution for the ML-for-health community: high within-dataset performance is not sufficient evidence of clinical validity, and external validation is essential before cough-based TB screening models can be deployed. It suggests that many reported gains in acoustic disease detection may reflect acquisition artifacts rather than disease signal. The authors observed that predicted TB probability tracks country-level prevalence in the CODA dataset, and that device mismatch degrades transfer while device-diverse training improves it. These findings indicate that acquisition-specific variability in acoustics is a stronger driver of poor generalizability than disease-related acoustic differences.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Cough acoustics are considered a promising non-invasive approach for tuberculosis screening, and many studies have applied machine learning or deep learning to classify cough sounds. ROC-AUC is a common metric for binary classification that measures how well a model distinguishes between classes, with 0.5 indicating random guessing and 1.0 perfect performance. Generalization refers to a model's ability to perform well on new, unseen data, and cross-dataset evaluation tests this by training on one dataset and testing on another.

## [Efficient Auto-Interpretability Pipeline for Boltz-1 Sparse Autoencoders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27754) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces a unified pipeline for auto-interpreting sparse autoencoders (SAEs) trained on the Boltz-1 Pairformer trunk, combining cross-seed dictionary stability, intruder detection, and falsifiable biological descriptions. It reports that stability prioritization finds interpretable latents with about 4.4x fewer latent evaluations and 5.2x lower LLM cost while recovering over half of them. This work advances mechanistic interpretability for AI-for-science models by providing a practical, cost-efficient method to identify useful latents in protein structure models like Boltz-1, potentially accelerating scientific discovery. It also highlights a bias in LLM judges and shows that a simple amino-acid identity filter can outperform stability thresholding, raising important questions about evaluation methods. The pipeline separately assesses latent coherence, describability, and predictive power, converting candidate biological descriptions into falsifiable in silico predictions. However, the LLM judge is heavily biased toward consistent residue identity, and a simple filter selecting for high amino-acid identity precision outperforms stability thresholding without needing extra SAE seeds, though it remains uncertain whether this reflects a genuinely useful heuristic or a limitation of the judge.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Sparse autoencoders (SAEs) are a technique in mechanistic interpretability that decompose model activations into sparse, monosemantic features, helping researchers understand what models have learned. Boltz-1 is an open-source model that achieves AlphaFold3-level accuracy in predicting 3D structures of biomolecular complexes, and its Pairformer trunk is a key component for processing sequence and structure information. Auto-interpretability aims to automatically generate explanations for SAE latents, often using LLMs, but ensuring these explanations are useful and predictive remains challenging.

## [Quantum Signal Processing Model Achieves Exact Kernel Statistics and Convergence Guarantees](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28828) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2608.28828v2) establishes quantum signal processing (QSP) as a solvable model of representation learning, computing the exact mean and variance of its quantum neural tangent kernel at arbitrary depth and proving a sparse-data convergence guarantee for the full nonlinear gradient flow without freezing the kernel. This provides a controlled theoretical framework for learned quantum data geometry with provable training dynamics beyond the frozen-kernel limit, which could advance the theoretical understanding of quantum machine learning and guide the design of trainable quantum models. The analysis reveals an input-dependent angular geometry whose diagonal remains non-self-averaging even when the underlying unitary approaches Haar randomness, and a finite-depth speed limit holds for every data set and trajectory; at higher data density, numerical results show coupled evolution beyond both scalar and frozen-kernel descriptions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Representation learning occurs when training changes the features that define similarity between data, whereas a frozen-kernel model only reweights a fixed geometry. The neural tangent kernel (NTK) is a theoretical tool that describes gradient-based training dynamics in the infinite-width limit, and its quantum analogue, the quantum neural tangent kernel (QNTK), extends this to quantum neural networks. Quantum signal processing (QSP) is a framework for approximating unitary functions by alternating simple unitaries and signal-processing steps, and here it serves as a solvable model for studying representation learning.

## [Safeguard Success Rates Don't Guarantee LLM Deployment Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.00519) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.00519v2) introduces a framework that translates reported safeguard attack-blocking rates into bounds on the minimum and maximum harmful assistance an attacker can still obtain under a specified threat model. Applying it to 85 safeguard evaluations from 52 papers, the authors find that only five argue every attack path passes through the check and only four provide evidence on what remains after a block. The finding that most published evaluations do not establish deployment safety is actionable for AI safety researchers and practitioners, who often treat high attack-blocking rates as sufficient evidence. It suggests deployment decisions should require measurements of residual harmful assistance, not just refusal or attack success rates. For safeguards that block attacks through a check, the maximum attacker gain depends on three properties: whether attack paths pass through the check (coverage), the check's miss rate against every attacker in the threat model, and how much assistance remains after a block. A concrete example shows NeMo Guardrails' default streaming configuration blocked 94% of password leaks in testing, yet the client received the password in every leak, blocked or not.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: LLM services typically deploy safeguards such as input/output filters or guardrail systems, and evaluate them using attack-blocking rates, refusal rates, or attack success rates on test suites. These metrics describe how a control performed on the tested requests, but they do not measure how much harmful assistance an attacker can still extract from the deployed system. The paper argues that deployment safety requires reasoning about the full attack path, including bypasses and post-block leakage, rather than a single aggregate rate.

## [Bayesian Optimization Cuts Scaling Law Construction Costs by 10–100×](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05016) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.05016v2) proposes a Bayesian optimization framework for constructing neural scaling laws, using progressively expanding compute budgets and surrogate-fantasized evaluations to avoid exhaustive grid training. The authors report that their method can closely match scaling law fits obtained from a full dense grid while achieving computational savings of up to 10–100×. Deriving scaling laws currently requires training an exhaustive grid over hyperparameters, token budgets, and parameter counts, which is prohibitively expensive for large foundation models. This work could make scaling law construction far more accessible, enabling researchers with limited compute to derive reliable scaling laws and potentially democratizing a key tool for large-model design. The framework formulates data collection as a Bayesian optimization problem and introduces metrics for comparing scaling law fitting methods under constrained compute budgets. Progressively expanding the compute budget during acquisition, mirroring the compute-ordered evaluation of configurations in practice, substantially improves recovery efficiency, and surrogate-fantasized evaluations recover the broader experimental grid without training every configuration.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Neural scaling laws describe how model loss decreases as a power law with model size, dataset size, and compute, and they guide decisions about how to allocate resources when training large language models. Traditionally, deriving these laws requires training many configurations across a grid of hyperparameters and scales, then fitting a curve to the best-loss frontier. Bayesian optimization is a sample-efficient technique that uses a surrogate model to decide which expensive experiments to run next, making it well suited to reducing the number of training runs needed.

## [EDCT Benchmark Exposes Faithfulness Gaps in Vision-Language Model Explanations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.17953) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Explanation-Driven Counterfactual Testing (EDCT), an intervention-based protocol that extracts visual concepts cited in a VLM's natural language explanation, applies verified minimal edits to those concepts, and checks whether the model's answer and explanation remain consistent with the edited image. Using this protocol, the authors build EDCT-Bench, a benchmark spanning OK-VQA, DriveLM, and 3DSRBench, and find that evaluated VLMs frequently produce responses inconsistent with verified visual changes. This work addresses an underexplored but critical problem in AI safety and interpretability: VLM explanations can sound plausible while being unfaithful to the visual evidence they cite. By providing a systematic, intervention-based way to measure this gap across knowledge-intensive VQA, safety-critical driving, and 3D spatial reasoning, EDCT-Bench could help researchers and practitioners build more trustworthy multimodal systems. The benchmark spans three complementary domains—knowledge-intensive VQA (OK-VQA), safety-critical driving (DriveLM), and 3D spatial reasoning (3DSRBench)—and the fine-tuning study suggests that EDCT-generated counterfactuals provide high-impact training signals. As an arXiv preprint, the work has not yet undergone peer review, and its immediate impact is currently concentrated within the VLM research niche.

**Background**: Vision-Language Models (VLMs) are AI systems that jointly interpret and generate information from images and text, extending large language models beyond text-only inputs. Many VLMs can produce Natural Language Explanations (NLEs) for their answers, but prior work has questioned whether these explanations faithfully reflect the visual evidence rather than merely sounding plausible. Counterfactual testing is an evaluation approach that changes one attribute of the input while holding the rest constant, and if the output changes meaningfully, it can reveal inconsistencies or biases in model behavior.

## [RPMem: Recurrent Parametric Memory for Cross-Session LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23466) ⭐️ 7.0/10

RPMem introduces a two-stage architecture that compiles each session into a model-independent latent memory via forward computation and recurrent gating, then maps the consolidated memory to backbone-specific LoRA parameters. On the PERMA benchmark with Qwen3-8B, it achieves 85.52%, outperforming the strongest parametric and text-based baselines by 5.32 and 12.98 percentage points, respectively. This work addresses a key limitation of parametric memory in LLM agents—its coupling to a specific backbone—by enabling memory reuse after model replacement, which could make long-running agents more portable and cost-efficient. It also offers a promising alternative to text-based retrieval memory, whose long-horizon performance degrades as histories grow. Evaluation spans three long-term memory benchmarks and five diverse backbones, showing near-constant update cost and memory footprint; ablations confirm the complementary roles of session compilation and cross-session consolidation. The abstract notes that evaluation is truncated in the provided content, and the code is available at the linked GitHub repository.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Long-running LLM agents need memory that persists across sessions. Text-based memory stores past interactions as text and retrieves them at query time, but performance becomes increasingly dependent on retrieval quality and contextual reasoning as histories grow. Parametric memory instead encodes experience directly into model computation, but existing methods struggle with cross-session evolution and are tied to a specific backbone. LoRA is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning technique that freezes pre-trained weights and injects trainable low-rank matrices, which RPMem uses to map its latent memory onto different backbones.

## [FLARE: Full-Lifecycle Dense Supervision for Long-Horizon Coding Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23808) ⭐️ 7.0/10

FLARE introduces a full-lifecycle dense supervision paradigm driven by a lightweight Generative Reward Model (GRM), distilled via an offline causal-aware diagnostic framework called RADAR that uses causal-chain backtracking to produce hindsight-free step-level supervision. The GRM provides real-time risk feedback during inference and serves as process-supervised reranking scores for SFT and dense step-level rewards for RL, with reported gains of 19.13% in SFT and 9.19% in RL. Sparse binary Pass/Fail rewards create a severe credit assignment crisis in long-horizon software engineering agents, wasting failed exploratory trajectories and limiting test-time scaling. FLARE's dense, step-level supervision could improve both inference efficiency and post-training stability for LLM coding agents, potentially shifting how agent training pipelines handle credit assignment. FLARE (N=1) reportedly outperforms Global Rollout (N=5) while consuming 5x fewer tokens, and the Active Scaffold mechanism intercepts high-risk generation steps for localized breakpoint re-execution. The work is an arXiv preprint (2609.23808v2) without community discussion or independent validation yet, so the reported gains should be treated as preliminary.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Long-horizon coding agents solve software engineering tasks through many sequential steps, but typically receive only a single binary reward at the end (pass or fail), making it hard to know which intermediate actions deserve credit. Reward models are used to score agent behavior; generative reward models (GRMs) produce interpretable rationales rather than just scalar scores, and recent work has explored their use in RL. Dense supervision supplies feedback at each step instead of only at the end, which is intended to mitigate sparse-reward problems and policy collapse.

<a id="item-223"></a>
## [Video-HopChain: Multi-Hop Video Reasoning Dataset and Confidence-Gated Exploration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25773) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Video-HopChain, a dataset of 22,550 multi-hop video questions over 13,378 videos, where each question chains three to six yes/no sub-questions whose integer answers sum to a verifiable final reward. It also proposes Confidence-Gated Exploration (CGE), which recovers GRPO groups with zero reward variance by masking the policy's most confident token in a second batch of rollouts, raising the mean score on eight video benchmarks from 55.4 to 59.3. This work addresses an underexplored gap in video-language research by providing multi-hop video reasoning data with verifiable rewards, which is scarce compared to still-image multi-hop datasets. The CGE method offers a compute-efficient fix for a known GRPO limitation, potentially benefiting any RLVR training pipeline that suffers from all-correct or all-incorrect rollout groups. Training proceeds in two stages: Qwen3-VL-8B is first trained with GRPO on a standard video dataset, then on Video-HopChain, which alone lifts the eight-benchmark mean from 55.4 to 57.9 and improves every benchmark. CGE uses 8 rollouts per question, sampling the first 4 normally and, if they are all correct or all incorrect, sampling the last 4 with the most confident token masked inside the reasoning span, excluding masked positions from the loss while all 8 rollouts contribute to the advantage.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) trains models using automatically checkable reward signals, such as exact-match answers, rather than learned reward models. Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) is the RL algorithm popularized by DeepSeek-R1: it samples a group of completions per prompt and computes advantages relative to the group's rewards, eliminating the need for a separate critic. A known weakness is that if all rollouts in a group receive the same reward, the group has zero variance and produces no learning signal. Multi-hop reasoning requires chaining several inference steps, so errors compound across steps, making it a demanding test of vision-language models.

<a id="item-224"></a>
## [Intuition Vectors from Frozen Vision Encoders Steer Diffusion Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32619) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.32619v2) shows that simple vector differences between embeddings from frozen self-supervised vision encoders, termed 'intuition vectors,' capture abstract transformations and distinguish among 794 abstract reasoning tasks with up to 87.2% accuracy. Conditioning a diffusion transformer on these vectors outperforms conditioning on full demonstrations on held-out ARC tasks while using nearly six times less test-time compute and enabling zero-shot inference. This suggests abstract reasoning can emerge from the relational geometry of pretrained visual representations without language-based inference or task-specific training, offering a modular, compute-efficient alternative to LLM-centric reasoning pipelines. It could influence how vision models are used for few-shot puzzle solving and inspire new ways to steer generative models with compact latent signals. The method relies entirely on frozen encoders and latent vector arithmetic, with intuition vectors preserving relational structure across task-invariant visual transformations and guiding output retrieval for unseen inputs. The evaluation covers Bongard problems and ARC tasks, but the abstract does not specify encoder architectures, dataset splits, or absolute performance on Bongard beyond discrimination accuracy.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Bongard problems are classic visual puzzles consisting of two sets of images that must be distinguished by an abstract rule, while the Abstraction and Reasoning Corpus (ARC) is a benchmark of grid-based puzzles with only a few input-output examples, designed to test general fluid intelligence. Self-supervised vision encoders such as those trained with contrastive or masked-image objectives learn rich latent spaces without labels, and prior work has shown that simple arithmetic on these latents can capture semantic directions. Diffusion transformers generate outputs by iteratively denoising in a latent space, and conditioning them on compact vectors is a way to steer generation without retraining the backbone.

## [Encoding Rank Imposes Error Floor on Interaction Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.36208) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.36208v2) introduces a diagnostic that computes the attainable contrast space from an encoder and contrast design without labels or predictor fitting, yielding an empirical squared-error floor for any deterministic decoder. On an siRNA panel, a graph neural network's training-only feature mask merges 165 measured states into 90 classes, reducing the rank of 140 interaction contrasts to 72 and producing a floor of 0.009980 (14.63% of recorded contrast energy). This work separates representability from learned accuracy, showing that encoder design alone can impose an information-theoretic limit on interaction prediction, which is relevant to ML practitioners working on representation learning and graph neural networks. It provides a label-free diagnostic to identify encoding-imposed limits and trace them to specific features, potentially guiding better encoder design. The floor is 14.63% of recorded contrast energy, but its 14.6% share of fitted interaction error adds little because predicted interactions are nearly zero and their MSE (0.068335) slightly exceeds that of predicting zero. Three restored chemistry columns suffice for full rank, and removing the full mask eliminates the floor but refitting improves interaction MSE by only 0.000017; the diagnostic does not by itself predict refitting gains or explain remaining error.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: In machine learning, an encoder maps raw inputs into a representation space, and a decoder uses that representation to predict outputs. Contrasts are differences between conditions that capture interactions, and the rank of the contrast matrix reflects how many independent interaction directions can be represented. If the encoder collapses distinct states into the same class, some contrasts become unlearnable regardless of model capacity, creating an error floor. This paper formalizes that idea by computing the attainable contrast space directly from the encoder design.

<a id="item-226"></a>
## [LeapQuant enables near-lossless 8-bit quantization of linear-attention recurrent states](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.38166) ⭐️ 7.0/10

LeapQuant introduces a training-free per-window quantization scheme that quantizes the recurrent state only once at the end of each token window, while computing outputs within the window from the fixed low-bit state plus high-precision buffered updates. It also preserves the state's largest outliers as high-precision Compensator Tokens and smooths the residual before quantization, achieving accuracy comparable to FP32 with 2.05–3.70× kernel-level and 1.47× end-to-end speedups on NVIDIA B200, RTX PRO 6000, and RTX 5090 GPUs. Linear-attention hybrids such as Gated DeltaNet and Kimi Delta Attention are increasingly used in long-context LLMs, but repeatedly reading and updating their fixed-size recurrent state is a major inference bottleneck. LeapQuant shows this state can be safely compressed to 8 bits without retraining, directly reducing memory and compute costs for long-context serving on current GPUs. The method targets two error sources: rounding-error accumulation, mitigated by quantizing only once per window, and per-quantization error, mitigated by outlier compensation and residual smoothing. Experiments span the Qwen, Kimi, and GLM model families, though the approach is evaluated only on linear-attention hybrid architectures and specific NVIDIA GPU generations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Standard transformer attention stores a key-value cache that grows with context length, making long-context inference expensive. Linear-attention variants like Gated DeltaNet (GDN) and Kimi Delta Attention (KDA) instead compress the entire context into a fixed-size recurrent state that is updated token by token, giving linear rather than quadratic scaling. However, that state must be read and written at every step, so its memory traffic becomes the bottleneck; quantizing it to 8 bits is attractive but naive quantization accumulates rounding errors and struggles with outlier rows and columns in the state.

## [Grokking Explained by Misaligned Low-Loss Regions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.00620) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.00620v2) proposes that grokking occurs when the low-loss regions induced by training and validation partitions are misaligned, and demonstrates a stable anti-grokking counterexample under a symmetry-preserving split where validation performance does not recover. This framework challenges existing correlational explanations of grokking and suggests that once low-loss regions are well aligned, training hyperparameters alone cannot produce grokking, which could reshape theoretical understanding of delayed generalization in deep learning. The analysis is based on mode connectivity and the geometry of low-loss regions, and the anti-grokking counterexample arises in the standard modular-arithmetic setting under a symmetry-preserving train/validation split, contradicting the assumption that grokking always occurs there.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Grokking refers to the sudden emergence of validation-set generalization long after a model has overfit the training set, first observed in small algorithmic tasks trained with transformers. Mode connectivity is a property of neural network loss landscapes where distinct minima can be connected by low-loss paths, and low-loss regions are the basins around these minima. The paper uses these geometric concepts to explain why grokking occurs and when it might not.

## [Power-draw verification weakly constrains hidden AI compute, study finds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.07476) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper derives a closed-form bound on the maximum hidden computation that a power trace cannot exclude, expressed as a fraction β of declared machine capacity. Measurements on NVIDIA A100 GPUs yield β = 1.16 in the worst case, meaning an adversary could hide more computation than the declared capacity, while an adversarial matched-energy schedule hides at least 0.41 of capacity. This work provides a quantitative assessment of whether off-chip power measurements can support verification of international AI compute treaties, suggesting that analogue channels alone are insufficient and that stronger threat-model restrictions are needed. It informs AI governance policy by clarifying the limits of hardware-based verification. The bound β is the largest hidden computation a power trace cannot exclude as a fraction of declared capacity; with additional verifier capabilities, such as re-executing declared work at an observed operating point, β can be reduced to 0.059 in the maximally restricted case. The analysis assumes an adversary actively trying to subvert the audit and uses power traces from A100 GPUs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Compute governance proposals aim to limit frontier AI training by verifying the amount of computation used, often through hardware-level measurements. Power draw is an analogue, off-chip signal that could reveal how much computation occurred, but its effectiveness against adversaries who try to mask their activity is unclear. The NVIDIA A100 is a widely used data-center GPU for AI workloads, with a maximum power draw of 250 W for the PCIe 40 GB model.

## [Natural Code Transformations Confuse AI Agents in Vulnerability Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14350) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.14350v3) reports that AI agents produce dramatically different vulnerability analysis results across real-world code commits that have the same functionality and the same vulnerability. The authors build a corpus of natural, functionality-preserving code transformations mined from real development patterns and show that even a single transformation form can effectively confuse frontier agents such as Claude Mythos 5.2 with Claude Code, reducing vulnerability discovery success. As AI agents become capable of automating vulnerability discovery at scale, they can also be repurposed for large-scale software attacks; this work suggests a practical defensive mechanism that slows attackers and buys time to patch. It also expands the research space of using code transformations as a defense against malicious agents, affecting both software vendors and AI security researchers. The study evaluates transformations across multiple code domains and vulnerability categories, analyzes different agent failure modes, examines whether transformations can be composed for stronger protection, and considers adaptive attackers who try to reverse the transformations. The finding is based on an arXiv preprint and has not yet undergone peer review.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: AI agents powered by large language models are increasingly used to automatically find software vulnerabilities, but the same capabilities can be turned toward attacking systems. Adversarial machine learning studies how small, semantics-preserving changes to inputs can fool models; this paper applies that idea to source code, using transformations that keep program behavior identical while altering surface form. Functionality-preserving transformations are common in real development, such as refactoring or stylistic changes, making them plausible as a defense.

## [Orchestra decouples planning and progress evaluation for long-horizon robot manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18352) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Orchestra, an agent harness that separates task planning and progress evaluation from action generation, enabling robots to execute long-horizon multi-step manipulation instructions. It also presents RMBench-Compose, a new benchmark for evaluating whether robots trained on atomic instructions can execute longer subtask sequences. This work addresses a key challenge in robotics: enabling robots to follow multi-step instructions while tracking progress and adapting to scene changes. The decoupled architecture and new benchmark could advance the development of more capable long-horizon manipulation systems, impacting research in robotics and embodied AI. Orchestra achieves a mean success rate of 68.22% across nine RMBench tasks, outperforming Mem-0 by 26.22 percentage points, and on RMBench-Compose it achieves an Average Success Length (ASL) of 7.35 subtasks, 2.46 times that of HarnessVLA. In four real-world multi-step tasks, it improves mean ASL by 25.0% over Orchestra using the base π0.5 policy.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Long-horizon manipulation requires robots to perform sequences of subtasks over extended periods, maintaining awareness of task progress and adapting to dynamic environments. Traditional approaches often train policies on short atomic instructions, limiting their ability to generalize to longer sequences. Agent harnesses, which provide software infrastructure around models to enable agentic behavior, have emerged as a way to orchestrate planning, memory, and tool use. Progress evaluators, separately trained models that estimate task completion, are increasingly used to decide when to advance between subtasks.

## [World Model + LightGBM Ensemble Tops Retail Sales Forecasting Benchmark](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25170) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper compares LightGBM, pretrained time-series foundation models (TimesFM, Chronos-Bolt, Timestral), the Temporal Fusion Transformer, and a state-carrying world model for retail sales forecasting across four public datasets (M5, six Dominick's categories, Breakfast at the Frat, and Corporacion Favorita). The key finding is that the average of the tuned world model and LightGBM, when summed from the finest grain to every coarser grain, achieves the lowest error in 22 of 27 grain-dataset cases and is never the least accurate on final held-out periods. Retailers need forecasts at multiple grains (item-day, item-week, category), and this study shows that a simple ensemble of a tuned world model and LightGBM can reliably serve all grains, outperforming both standalone foundation models and TFT. This suggests that practitioners should consider hybrid approaches rather than relying solely on large pretrained models, and that hierarchical reconciliation from the finest grain is a viable strategy. The world model is particularly strong on existing items kept out of training (losing at most 0.040 in scaled error vs. up to 0.311 for LightGBM) and on lines with short history (more accurate on six of nine datasets), while LightGBM wins on heavily promoted lines, fast sellers, long-history lines, and price-cut weeks. The authors caution that findings hold only for these datasets and price/promotion levers, and recommend rerunning comparisons on proprietary data.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Retail sales forecasting often involves hierarchical time series, where forecasts at fine grains (e.g., item-store-day) must be aggregated to coarser grains (e.g., category-week) for different business decisions. LightGBM is a fast, high-performance gradient boosting framework for tabular data, while the Temporal Fusion Transformer (TFT) is an attention-based deep learning architecture designed for multi-horizon forecasting with heterogeneous features. Time-series foundation models like TimesFM, Chronos-Bolt, and Timestral are pretrained on large corpora and can be applied zero-shot or fine-tuned to new forecasting tasks.

## [ASE: Activation-Space Exploration Boosts RLVR Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27012) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduce Activation-Space Exploration (ASE), an online method that clusters activation differences between correct and incorrect responses to build a bank of latent exploration vectors, which are then applied to subsequent rollouts to diversify reasoning paths. Under the same rollout budget, ASE improves average accuracy over GRPO by 5.41 and 3.15 percentage points on six visual math and six general visual reasoning benchmarks with Qwen3-VL-2B-Instruct, and by 4.87 percentage points in Mean@8 on six math reasoning benchmarks with Qwen3-1.7B-Base. This work addresses a key inefficiency in RLVR—repeated sampling from the same policy concentrates on similar reasoning paths—by diversifying rollouts in activation space, potentially making RL training more sample-efficient and improving reasoning performance without extra rollout budget. The gains across both visual and textual reasoning tasks suggest the method could be broadly applicable to LLM post-training. ASE builds a structured bank of latent exploration vectors by clustering activation differences between correct and incorrect responses to the same problem, then applies different vectors to subsequent rollouts to explore alternative reasoning paths. The method is evaluated on Qwen3-VL-2B-Instruct and Qwen3-1.7B-Base, with gains also observed on Qwen3-8B-Base, all under the same rollout budget as GRPO.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) trains LLMs by sampling responses, scoring them with an automatic verifier, and updating the policy from rewards, but the policy only learns from trajectories that appear in its rollouts. Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) is a popular RL algorithm that estimates advantages by comparing rewards within a group of sampled responses for the same prompt, eliminating the need for a separate value model. However, repeated sampling from the same policy can concentrate on similar reasoning paths, limiting exploration under a fixed budget. ASE addresses this by diversifying rollouts through activation-space interventions.

## [WorldFlow: Joint Video and 3D Motion Prediction via Large-Scale Pretraining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39278) ⭐️ 7.0/10

WorldFlow introduces a generative world model that jointly predicts future video and query-based 3D trajectories using a unified Mixture-of-Transformers backbone trained with a rectified-flow objective. It is trained on 1.44 million object-level samples and introduces WorldFlowBench, achieving the lowest average displacement error (ADE) and final displacement error (FDE) among learning-based methods, with a 54.2% ADE reduction on the SpatialCog set compared to the strongest baseline. This work bridges video world models and explicit 3D motion prediction, enabling more physically grounded predictions for embodied AI and robotics. By coupling visual evolution with geometric dynamics, it could improve spatial reasoning and action planning in real-world environments. The model parameterizes 3D motion as continuous displacement tokens aligned with the video grid, and the MoT backbone jointly denoises both modalities under a rectified-flow objective. WorldFlowBench comprises 1,678 evaluation samples from 11 data sources, including spatial reasoning tasks, and the model reduces ADE and FDE by 55.9% and 68.5% respectively compared to its flow-only variant.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: World models are generative models that predict future states of an environment, often used in embodied AI to simulate physical dynamics. Video world models typically predict future frames but leave 3D motion implicit, while 3D motion prediction models often rely on flow-only supervision. Mixture-of-Transformers (MoT) is a sparse architecture that integrates multiple transformer modules for multi-modal learning, and rectified flow is a generative modeling technique that learns straight flows between distributions for efficient sampling.

## [See, Think, Use: Diagnosing Perception and Reasoning Failures in VLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01467) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new protocol called See, Think, Use traces each answer of a vision-language model to perception, reasoning, or the use of perception, using two extra questions per item without access to model weights. It employs a localization probe to measure seeing and a text-replacement probe to measure thinking, while use is computed as the rate at which answers follow the model's own perception across counterfactual image pairs. This diagnostic protocol enables developers to identify which stage—perception, reasoning, or use—is failing and direct test-time compute accordingly, potentially improving VLM reliability and efficiency. It addresses a key limitation of standard benchmarks that credit correct answers regardless of whether they stem from genuine visual understanding or memorized priors. The protocol requires only two extra questions per item and no weight access, making it broadly applicable; experiments on two model families and six datasets show that the helpful knob depends on the task family, and that asking the model to localize first repairs failures when its boxes are correct. Allocating compute by measured stages approaches the best uniform policy at lower cost and outperforms random allocation where a knob raises the short stage.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) are AI systems that process both images and text to answer questions or generate descriptions. Standard benchmarks typically score only the final answer, so a correct response might come from true visual perception, from reasoning, or from guessing based on common biases in the training data. Disentangling these sources is important for understanding model capabilities and for targeting improvements.

## [EmoReward: Self-Evolving Reward Calibration for Emotional Video Generation RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06518) ⭐️ 7.0/10

EmoReward introduces a self-evolving training mechanism that distills reliability from reward traces collected during an actual training run to adaptively weight multiple rewards for emotional video generation. It first plans grounding cues from the emotional prompt, scores videos with heterogeneous signal primitives, and uses Reflective Reward Gating and RewardMemory to calibrate fusion weights in subsequent training runs without updating reward models. This work extends GRPO beyond verifiable domains like code and math to subjective, non-verifiable tasks such as emotional video generation, where fixed rewards poorly capture emotional intent and invite reward hacking. By adaptively calibrating reward weights, it offers a scalable alternative to RLHF for aligning generative models with nuanced aesthetic and emotional goals. Experiments on HunyuanVideo 1.5 show improvements across multiple RL algorithms, with comparisons to other agentic reward methods under SAGE-GRPO; ablations and transfer experiments on SD 3.5 and FLUX further support adaptive reward calibration. The method uses instance-level gates for phase, face confidence, conflict, and subtlety, and fixes bounded multipliers for the next run without updating reward models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) is a reinforcement learning algorithm that estimates advantages by comparing rewards within a group of responses sampled for the same prompt, removing the need for a separate value model. It has driven progress in post-training generative models for verifiable domains like code and math, but extending it to subjective qualities such as emotional expression is challenging because there is no uniquely correct target. RLHF, which trains a reward model from human preferences, is difficult to scale and subject to individual aesthetic differences, and fixed multi-reward schemes can lead to reward hacking where models exploit flaws in the reward signal.

## [OverServe Attack Amplifies SaaS Agent Resource Use via Tool-Prompt Co-Design](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14808) ⭐️ 7.0/10

OverServe is a new multi-agent framework that constructs resource amplification attacks against SaaS LLM agents by co-designing MCP status tools and completion-confirmation prompts. Tested on Qwen, Nemotron, DeepSeek, and Sonnet across nine benchmarks, it achieves up to 93.88x amplification of API requests while passing all original task checks. This research exposes a novel attack surface in subscription-based SaaS deployments where model and tool calls consume provider resources without additional customer payments, potentially leading to significant financial losses for providers. It highlights the need for new defenses and billing models that account for autonomous agent behavior. The attack works by having a malicious tenant send a prompt that makes the agent repeatedly check an unfinished condition via MCP status tools, even after the business task is done, and the tool specifications trigger no alerts in evaluated MCP scanners. The framework uses separate tool and prompt reviews to select effective pairs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: The Model Context Protocol (MCP) is an open standard introduced by Anthropic in November 2024 to standardize how LLMs integrate with external tools and data. In SaaS deployments, LLM agents autonomously decide when to stop, and resource amplification attacks aim to exploit this by inducing excessive resource consumption.

## [ReShap: Interpretable RL for High-Dimensional Multi-Action Settings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15507) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ReShap is a policy-distillation framework that extends Shapley-based interpretability to high-dimensional, multi-action reinforcement learning environments by extracting compact image features, performing SHAP attribution, and distilling deep RL policies into interpretable surrogates. It is evaluated on Atari and Box2D using three deep RL algorithms, with a human-subject study showing that decision trees generally outperform linear and logistic regression surrogates. This work addresses a key limitation of prior Shapley-based interpretability methods like SILVER, which were restricted to low-dimensional states and binary actions, making them impractical for real-world visual RL tasks. By enabling interpretability in high-dimensional, multi-action settings, ReShap could improve trust and safety in deep RL deployments such as robotics and game playing. ReShap distills policies into three interpretable surrogate families—decision trees, linear regression, and logistic regression—trained on the full set of (state, policy-action) pairs along the policy's visited distribution. Results show that decision trees generally achieve the best fidelity and task return, while linear regression is weakest in most settings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Shapley values, rooted in cooperative game theory, provide a fair way to attribute a model's prediction to individual features, and SHAP is a popular method for estimating them. Policy distillation transfers knowledge from a complex deep RL policy to a simpler, interpretable model. ReShap combines these ideas to make deep RL policies more transparent, building on the SILVER framework but extending it to high-dimensional image observations and multiple actions.

## [Spec-Driven PBT derives semantic invariants from specifications for agentic testing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15920) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper proposes Spec-Driven PBT, an agent that first expresses invariants in natural language from the specification and only then inspects the implementation to translate them into concrete tests. Across 25 applications from ViBench, it finds a novel set of bugs that Code-driven PBT misses while writing 2.4x fewer property tests. This approach addresses a key challenge in agentic coding, where AI-generated code is produced faster than it can be reviewed and exhibits characteristic failure patterns. By deriving invariants from specifications rather than implementations, it provides complementary bug discovery that targets documented product workflows, which could improve quality assurance for AI-written software. The study found that nearly 60% of the 169 total bugs were found by only one approach, with Spec-Driven PBT bugs tending to violate documented product workflows while Code-driven PBT bugs concentrated on implementation robustness. The abstract is truncated, and no community discussion is available to gauge impact.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Property-based testing (PBT) is a technique where invariant properties of a program are checked against many generated inputs, but deriving these invariants has traditionally been high-effort. Recently, LLM agents have been used to author properties from the implementation alone (Code-driven PBT), removing some of this effort. Agentic coding refers to AI agents producing implementations from natural language specifications, and thorough specifications like product requirements documents are now routine in this practice.

## [EgoX: Cross-Embodiment Robot Learning via Embodiment Dreaming](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16728) ⭐️ 7.0/10

EgoX introduces an egocentric cross-embodiment learning framework that pretrains a Modular Embodiment Dreamer (MED) on mixed human, robot, and simulated demonstrations, then fine-tunes on target-embodiment data. Across 13 task-embodiment pairs, cross-embodiment pretraining raises average normalized task progress from 0.63 to 0.80 over training from scratch, and from 0.53 to 0.77 under out-of-distribution conditions. This work addresses a key challenge in robot learning: effectively leveraging heterogeneous demonstrations from humans, robots, and simulations despite differences in morphology, sensing, and control. The significant gains, especially under out-of-distribution conditions, suggest a path toward more generalist manipulation policies that can transfer across embodiments. MED assigns a dedicated tokenizer per observation modality and a dedicated expert per action modality within each embodiment, while sharing a Transformer trunk across embodiments. A training-only embodiment-dreaming objective predicts latent representations of future visual and proprioceptive states, encouraging the shared trunk to model each embodiment's dynamics.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Cross-embodiment learning aims to train a single policy that works across different robot bodies, but differences in sensors, actuators, and morphology make this difficult. Imitation learning from demonstrations is a common approach, but combining data from humans, real robots, and simulations requires handling mismatched observation and action spaces. EgoX uses an egocentric perspective to align manipulation structure across embodiments while keeping interfaces embodiment-specific.

## [Zero-pair cross-modal alignment fails beyond COCO, LightAlign fixes it](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20000) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.20000) tests zero-pair cross-modal alignment across five datasets and four modalities (natural images, environmental audio, chest radiographs, and 3D CT), finding it succeeds only on COCO and fails on the other four. The authors analyze the failures, showing alignment requires locating a shared subspace and orienting the mapping, and propose LightAlign, which uses a few paired samples with ridge regression to achieve alignment. This work provides a rigorous empirical test of the Platonic Representation Hypothesis and identifies key failure modes of zero-pair alignment, especially in medical imaging where the shared subspace carries the smallest variance fraction. LightAlign offers a practical solution that outperforms end-to-end trained models with only a few pairs, potentially influencing future cross-modal representation learning and medical imaging applications. The paper uses random-matrix theory to predict how many pairs are needed when the shared subspace variance fraction is small. On 3D CT, LightAlign with only 200 pairs outperforms CT-CLIP on CT-RATE (0.754 vs. 0.733 AUROC), and with 100 CT-RATE pairs it generalizes to RAD-ChestCT from a different hospital; on CheXpert it reaches 0.862 mean AUROC with about 5% as many pairs as models trained on MIMIC-CXR.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: The Platonic Representation Hypothesis (PRH) proposes that neural networks trained on different modalities and objectives converge toward a shared representation. Zero-pair alignment aims to align frozen encoders from different modalities without any paired data, leveraging this shared structure. Medical imaging is considered a challenging domain because the shared subspace between modalities tends to capture a small fraction of the variance.

## [Audit of LLM Hiring Systems Reveals Interface and Compliance Risks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20140) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.20140v2) presents an empirical audit of a modular LLM-assisted hiring system, testing operational readiness on four LLMs with 210 resumes across 14 job roles and six decision interfaces, and compliance readiness against unauthorized requests and prompt injections embedded in resumes. The findings show that seemingly minor design choices—such as using scalar scores versus pass/fail judgments—significantly affect how well LLMs distinguish candidate qualifications, and that compliance with permission rules varies across models, which has direct implications for fairness and regulatory compliance in HR tech. Scalar scores generally distinguished qualification changes better than simple pass/fail judgments, while comparative judgments were sensitive to presentation order; explicit permission rules and separating resume content from instructions reduced unauthorized compliance to varying degrees across LLMs, and LLM evaluators often diverged between their decisions and offered justifications.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: LLM-assisted hiring systems use large language models to screen and rank resumes, aiming to automate parts of the recruitment process. Prompt injection is a technique where hidden text in a resume attempts to manipulate the LLM's evaluation, and prior work has shown that such attacks can undermine fairness and trust in AI-driven hiring.

## [Forecast explanations pass checks but overstate lift, study finds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20811) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper defines eight checks for plan-lift explanations in retail forecasting and shows that both a world model and LightGBM can pass them while overstating true lift by 23–42% in high-demand weeks, leading to excess stock orders. This finding is significant because it shows that standard sanity checks for explanations do not guarantee accuracy about the real world, which can directly cost money through overstocking and misplanned promotions. The world model states 1.33–1.40 times the true lift in high-demand weeks, and stock ordered to its lift is off by 0.44–0.48 of the true lift; across 703 configurations, checks passed were not related to accuracy (Spearman 0.02).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Retail forecasting models often decompose a forecast into a baseline (what would sell with no plan) and a lift (the extra sales from price and promotion plans). Merchants use this lift to order stock and plan promotions. LightGBM is a popular open-source gradient-boosting framework for machine learning. The paper introduces eight checks to audit whether such explanations are trustworthy, but finds that passing them does not ensure the lift is accurate.

## [Transcript-Only Frustration Detection Fails on Short Clinical Voice-Agent Calls](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21001) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper evaluates per-turn frustration detection on 150 real patient turns from a deployed clinical voice agent, finding that transcript-only methods perform no better than chance on one-word turns. Across 30 calls (714 turns), audio-capable models flagged only 3-4 of 39 frustrated one-word turns when using the transcript, and a classifier using only word count achieved 57.3% accuracy. The findings suggest that clinical triage systems relying solely on speech-to-text transcripts may miss frustrated patients, especially when patient responses are very short. This has practical implications for the design of autonomous voice agents in healthcare, where detecting frustration is important for patient safety and quality of care. Patient turns in deployed calls are overwhelmingly short, with roughly 59% being one or two words versus 13% in an acted corpus, and a length-matched acted corpus still shows an accuracy gap. Audio helps only for some models and prompts: two Gemini 2.5 models gain 3.0 percentage points when asked to attend to delivery, but under input-specific prompts a newer Gemini model and two smaller open models perform worse with audio (-3.5, -8.6, and -10.6 pp), and a WavLM probe misses every frustrated one-word turn in 24 added calls.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Autonomous voice agents are increasingly used for routine clinical follow-up calls, and these systems typically process only the transcript produced by speech-to-text, discarding vocal cues such as tone and delivery. Frustration detection is a key triage task, but most speech emotion recognition research relies on acted corpora, which may not reflect the short, spontaneous turns found in real patient calls. This study compares real deployed calls with a length-matched acted corpus to test whether transcript-only detection is sufficient.

## [LynnReal-Hand: Hand-Controlled Video World Model for Real-Time Interaction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21479) ⭐️ 7.0/10

LynnReal-Hand introduces a hand-controlled video world model that unifies precise hand-object interaction modeling with efficient streaming generation, using a 100K-clip dataset and region-aware score distillation. It achieves an FVD of 181.67 and a MANO Dice score of 93.02, with a three-step causal model maintaining a MANO Dice score of 92.49, and enables 540p streaming at 18.03 FPS on a single H200 GPU. This work advances interactive video world models by enabling natural hand-based control for object interaction, which could benefit applications in virtual reality, gaming, and embodied AI. The release of datasets, model weights, and code will support further research in hand-controlled video generation. The model uses a causal flow map for few-step generation and introduces Ultra-Fast-VAE, a compact video decoder that preserves high reconstruction fidelity with lower computational cost. Real-time interaction reaches 741ms latency with a peak GPU memory footprint of 34.89GB.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Video world models are generative models that simulate interactive environments, allowing users to explore and manipulate scenes through controls like text or camera movements. Score distillation sampling (SDS) is a technique that uses pretrained diffusion models to optimize generated samples, and causal flow maps enable few-step generation by distilling bidirectional diffusion models into efficient causal generators. Hand-object interaction modeling is challenging due to complex dynamics and the need for real-time computation.

## [KVM x86 patch unconditionally resets MMU context on INIT emulation for CVE-2026-99312](https://www.cve.org/CVERecord?id=CVE-2026-99312) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A Linux kernel patch for KVM on x86 now unconditionally resets the MMU context when emulating an INIT signal, fixing CVE-2026-99312. Previously, if paging was already disabled, the MMU context was not reset, leaving stale state that could lead to a use-after-free. This vulnerability affects nested virtualization environments (L2 guests) and can potentially enable a guest-to-host escape, impacting cloud providers and anyone running untrusted workloads in KVM. It highlights ongoing security challenges in nested virtualization. The bug occurs when an INIT is emulated while L2 is running with CR0.PG=0, because the MMU page role includes guest_mode (L2) and SMM bits; the fix ensures these bits are always cleared. The vulnerability was discussed on Hacker News, with some commenters questioning the severity and noting the original CVE record is now a 404.

hackernews · Kozmik1 · Nov 18, 16:06 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50548004)

**Background**: KVM (Kernel-based Virtual Machine) is a Linux kernel module that allows the kernel to act as a hypervisor. Nested virtualization lets a guest hypervisor (L1) run its own guests (L2), and an INIT signal is a processor reset used in x86 systems. The MMU context tracks address translation state, and failing to reset it on INIT can leave stale mappings that may be exploited.

NEBench R1 introduces a benchmark of 7,546 multiple-choice questions derived from U.S. Nuclear Regulatory Commission (NRC) reactor operator licensing exams across 18 nuclear facilities, each with an official explanation for reasoning evaluation. Six LLMs were tested, achieving overall accuracy between 34.0% and 73.9%, and an LLM-based judge revealed that correct answers often lacked technically sound reasoning. It provides a foundation for measuring the reliability and domain competence of LLMs in nuclear engineering, potentially influencing how such models are validated before deployment. The benchmark classifies questions according to NRC Knowledge and Abilities catalogs and uses an LLM-based judge with a multidimensional rubric to evaluate reasoning quality. Notably, longer reasoning traces were associated with lower accuracy in frontier reasoning models, suggesting reasoning length may reflect question difficulty rather than improved problem-solving, and reasoning-token consumption could serve as a signal for unreliable responses.

## [AgentComposition: Bundle-Aware Resource Selection for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22030) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This arXiv paper introduces AgentComposition, a method that jointly selects a backbone model and a tool-skill bundle for an LLM agent using retrieval-grounded generation and a bundle-aware critic. It builds AgentSelect-Exec with 5,060 executed configurations and recovers the complete reference configuration within ten candidates for 67.73% of queries, nearly twice the strongest baseline. Agent performance depends heavily on the right combination of model and tools, yet most systems select resources independently or rely on fixed catalogs. This work formulates joint resource composition as a learnable problem, which could improve agent reliability and adaptability in real-world deployments. The generator uses constrained beam search over resource descriptions to recombine candidates without retraining, while the critic is trained on LLM teacher judgments and historically successful configurations as execution proxies. The proxy-trained critic raises the top-ranked agent's execution score from 7.58 to 8.37 out of 10, and execution feedback further increases it to 8.78.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: LLM agents often need to choose both a backbone model and a set of tools or skills to accomplish a task. Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) helps ground decisions by retrieving relevant information before generating an answer, and constrained beam search allows controlled generation by enforcing constraints during decoding. This paper combines these ideas to compose agent resources from a catalog, addressing the limitation that token-based generators are tied to their training catalog and independent scoring misses complementarities between resources.
