## [Hybrid ALNS and Branch-and-Bound Solver for Integrated Passenger and Parcel Transport](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22043) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper presents a solver for the SOICT 2026 contest on Integrated Passenger and Parcel Transportation, combining Adaptive Large Neighborhood Search (ALNS) with an exact route-selection engine and branch-and-bound. On 38 official instances, it achieved a total benefit of 487,237, finishing all runs within 277 seconds and using at most 279 MB of memory. This work demonstrates a practical hybrid approach that integrates metaheuristics with exact optimization for a complex vehicle routing problem, potentially inspiring similar hybrid solvers for other selective pickup-and-delivery variants. It also highlights the effectiveness of combining ALNS with branch-and-bound for real-world transportation problems with time windows and heterogeneous vehicles. The solver is a single-file Python program using only the standard library, featuring a constant-time insertion test, greedy insertion, local search, ALNS with simulated annealing, and an exact route-selection engine that enumerates feasible routes and solves a set-packing problem via branch-and-bound with Lagrangian bounds and reduced-cost fixing. The engine is automatically selected based on instance size, and an independent verifier and watchdog ensure valid output.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: The Integrated Passenger and Parcel Transportation problem is a selective pickup-and-delivery routing problem with time windows, open routes, and heterogeneous taxis, where the goal is to maximize profit by serving a subset of requests. Adaptive Large Neighborhood Search (ALNS) is a metaheuristic that iteratively destroys and repairs solutions using adaptive operator selection. Branch-and-bound is an exact algorithm that systematically explores solution space with bounding to prune suboptimal branches.

## [Audit protocol reveals lower harm can mask worse concept corrections](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22084) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper proposes a correction audit protocol that separates harm from useful gain in concept corrections, and shows that worst-case correction training on three breast-ultrasound datasets lowers harm but increases corrected negative log-likelihood (NLL) in all 21 source comparisons pooled over six seeds. This work challenges the common practice of evaluating correction objectives solely by harm and accuracy, showing that lower harm can be misleading unless read together with useful gain, absolute risk, and the starting prediction, which is critical for trustworthy ML in medical imaging. The audit splits each correction into harm and useful gain on identical events, separates the starting prediction from the correction response, and compares objectives with amplitude baselines matched in source harm; on CUB, a monotonic harm penalty lowers path-level harm in all 80 concept sets while raising NLL in all 80, almost entirely before any correction.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: In medical imaging, concept corrections are expert adjustments to model-predicted concepts such as BI-RADS descriptors in breast ultrasound, which are standardized terms for describing findings. Harm measures how much a correction worsens the prediction, while useful gain measures how much it improves it. Negative log-likelihood (NLL) is a common metric for probabilistic predictions, where lower values indicate better-calibrated probabilities. The paper argues that harm alone is insufficient because it can fall for the wrong reasons, such as a model responding less to corrections or starting from a worse uncorrected prediction.

## [LoCaR: Training-Free Localized Image Retrieval with Frozen VLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22131) ⭐️ 6.0/10

LoCaR is a training-free framework for localized text-to-image retrieval that uses SAM2 to generate object-centric region proposals and encodes each region independently with a frozen vision-language encoder. The authors also introduce LOCFG, a Visual Genome-derived benchmark designed to evaluate retrieval of smaller and more challenging referents. Whole-image embeddings often under-represent small objects and local attributes, so this work shows that improving region-level representation itself is the main source of retrieval gains across several vision-language backbones. Because it requires no encoder fine-tuning, the approach is practical for deploying localized retrieval on top of existing frozen models. LoCaR optionally adds paired-support adaptation, a residual mechanism that uses matched text-region exemplars without updating the underlying encoders. The LOCFG benchmark is derived from Visual Genome and focuses on smaller, harder referents, while the main reported gains come from better region representation rather than from the optional adaptation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Text-to-image retrieval typically compares a text query against a single embedding of the whole image, which can miss small objects or local attributes. SAM2 (Segment Anything Model 2) is a foundation model for promptable segmentation in images and videos, and here it is used to propose object-centric regions. Visual Genome is a large dataset of images annotated with objects, attributes, and relationships, making it suitable for building a localized retrieval benchmark. Frozen vision-language encoders are pretrained models used without fine-tuning, which keeps the framework training-free.

## [RecPo reconfigures procedural knowledge for compositional robot skills](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22174) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose RecPo, a framework that represents robot skills task-agnostically through multiple procedure views and composes stored transitions to form executable skill sequences under sparse task specifications. On an extended Franka Kitchen benchmark, RecPo achieves 91.9% ordered completion with 1.0 ms of task inference per episode, and real-robot studies show adaptation via local memory edits without task-specific retraining. This work shifts robot adaptation from learning new behaviors to cost-efficiently reconfiguring existing competence, which could make general-purpose robots more versatile and easier to deploy in new tasks. It is relevant to robotics and AI researchers working on skill composition, compositional generalization, and long-horizon manipulation. RecPo maintains a procedure knowledge base of skill-induced transitions, with each view exposing a reusable factor and preserving local skill connectivity; at deployment, task specifications are interpreted through the same views and resolved as compositional queries over stored relations. The abstract reports strong benchmark results but does not detail the full experimental setup, baselines, or limitations, so technical depth cannot be fully assessed from the abstract alone.

Skill composition is the principle that a small set of reusable skills or modules can be recombined to cover a large space of behaviors, a key ingredient of compositional generalization. A new look-ahead-free benchmark tested LLM trading agents before 598 earnings announcements and after 409 bad-news drops of large US stocks, finding that no agent beat simple betting strategies across five models from four vendors. This study challenges the assumption that LLM agents can extract alpha from news events and suggests that reported gains may stem from memorization of training data rather than genuine predictive skill, urging builders to benchmark against simple baselines and test blind after training cutoffs. ## [CorrectCast: Lightweight Residual Correction for Precipitation Nowcasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22178) ⭐️ 6.0/10

CorrectCast introduces a decoupled residual correction framework that refines frozen precipitation nowcasters by training only a lightweight corrector conditioned on multimodal context (QPE and PWV) and scale-aware modulation. On MRB and CONUS datasets, it improves six base nowcasters (extrapolation, deterministic, and generative) with average CSI gains of 0.0233 and 0.0290, respectively. This approach avoids expensive retraining and architecture-specific modifications, offering a unified correction interface that can be applied post-hoc to diverse nowcasting models. It could lower the barrier for operational meteorology centers to enhance existing forecast systems without replacing them. The corrector operates on predicted precipitation fields and uses historical QPE and PWV observations as additional context, with scale-aware modulation to estimate residuals. Gains are measured by mean CSI, a standard verification metric, but the absolute improvements are modest and evaluated on two specific regions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Precipitation nowcasting predicts rainfall intensity and location from minutes to a few hours ahead, which is critical for flood warnings and public safety. Deep learning models have become dominant but often produce errors in intensity, location, and spatial structure. Retraining these models is costly and requires architecture-specific changes, motivating post-processing approaches like residual correction. QPE provides observed precipitation estimates, while PWV measures atmospheric water vapor that can indicate precipitation potential.

## [Fine-tuned BERT-CRF beats zero-shot LLMs on Japanese clinical NER](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22219) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A controlled study on the iCorpus benchmark (113 entity categories) compared two fine-tuned BERT-CRF baselines with nine open-weight LLMs for fine-grained Japanese clinical NER. Zero-shot LLMs achieved a best relaxed F1 of only 0.337, while BERT-CRF baselines reached 0.836–0.841; however, after QLoRA fine-tuning, all eight LLMs with at least 13B parameters surpassed BERT, reaching relaxed F1 of 0.882–0.914. This provides a clear negative result for zero-shot LLM use in fine-grained clinical NER and shows that task-specific fine-tuning plus sufficient output capacity is necessary for LLMs to outperform established encoder-based models. It offers practical guidance for clinical NLP practitioners deciding between BERT-style models and LLMs, especially in low-resource or non-English medical settings. The evaluation used 433 test sentences from 36 documents and 1,672 training sentences; output-token limits materially affected results, with increasing the limit improving relaxed F1 by 0.052–0.085 across three-seed means. JSON Schema constraints changed relaxed F1 by only -0.014 to +0.008 for seven models, and medical-specialized models showed no consistent advantage over general models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Named entity recognition (NER) is the task of identifying and classifying entities such as diseases, drugs, and symptoms in text. BERT-CRF combines a pretrained BERT encoder with a conditional random field layer to model label dependencies, and has long been a strong baseline for NER. Open-weight LLMs are models whose parameters are publicly released, and zero-shot prompting means asking the model to perform a task without any task-specific training examples. QLoRA is an efficient fine-tuning method that uses 4-bit quantization and low-rank adapters to adapt large models with limited GPU memory.

## [Multimodal LLM system digitizes handwritten cognitive and behavioral assessments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22252) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose a modular multimodal LLM-based system with human-in-the-loop validation to extract fields and scores from scanned cognitive assessments (MMSE, MoCA, Mini-Cog) and the NICHQ Vanderbilt Assessment. The system achieves 88% end-to-end accuracy on cognitive assessments and 97.5% on the NICHQ Vanderbilt, with human oversight boosting cognitive accuracy to 97.5% while reducing manual work by 63.8%. Paper-based assessments are ubiquitous in clinical care, but handwriting and scanning artifacts make them hard to process at scale; this system offers a scalable, cost-efficient way to structure such data for clinical decision support, reducing processing time by 10–20 times and costs by over 12 times compared to manual extraction. It demonstrates a practical application of multimodal LLMs in healthcare, potentially easing administrative burdens and enabling large-scale data analysis. The system uses a two-module pipeline: a form classifier that assigns a scanned page to a form type, followed by a score extractor that outputs fields and scores into a schema. A dual-model validation engages human annotators only when two LLMs disagree; qualitative analysis of the Mini-Cog Clock Drawing task found that 62.3% of extraction errors stemmed from ambiguous drawings where the LLM still provided reasonable readings.

## [Physics-Constrained Rectified Flow Enforces Conservation in Climate Downscaling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22283) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces Physics-Constrained Rectified Flow (PC-RF), a conditional flow-matching downscaler that uses training penalties for wind divergence, negative precipitation, and mass drift, plus a post-sampling projection that exactly removes non-negativity and mass-conservation violations. On ERA5, both PC-RF and its penalty-free baseline achieve zero Non-negativity Violation Rate (NVR) and zero Mass-Conservation Error (MCE) after projection, while the penalties reduce Divergence Error by about 30% on a synthetic test. Generative downscaling models can produce realistic-looking fields that violate basic physical laws, undermining trust in climate projections. PC-RF shows that simple post-processing can guarantee exact enforcement of non-negativity and mass conservation, which are critical for impact studies like flooding and crop stress, and argues that such checks should be standard pass-or-fail tests. The projection alone, without any trained network, drives NVR from 0.236 and MCE from 0.184 to zero on a corrupted synthetic field, but the penalties add no perceptual gain separable from training budget or run-to-run variation, and PC-RF shows no edge over the baseline on SSIM, PSNR, or CRPS at a matched budget. The authors also note a sign error in the divergence operator on ERA5 and that the fine reference is interpolated, so perceptual scores cannot establish downscaling skill.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Statistical downscaling infers high-resolution climate fields from coarse-resolution inputs, and recent work uses generative models like diffusion and flow matching to produce sharp, realistic details. Rectified flow is a flow-matching variant that learns straight transport paths between noise and data, and ERA5 is ECMWF's fifth-generation global atmospheric reanalysis widely used as a reference dataset. Physical constraints such as non-negativity of precipitation and mass conservation are often violated by generative outputs, motivating methods to enforce them.

## [Learning Regions of Attraction for Transverse Feedback Linearization under Controller Singularities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22286) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper proposes a learning-based method to estimate the constrained region of attraction (ROA) for transverse feedback linearization (TFL) controllers, which are undefined on singular sets. It decomposes the state-constrained Zubov value function into a known quadratic cost-to-go and a learned safety value function, trained via fitted value iteration without infinite-horizon trajectory labels, and verifies the inner layer with interval branch and bound. This work addresses a critical safety issue in path-following control: determining where a TFL controller can be safely activated despite singularities. The approach combines learning with formal verification, potentially enabling safer deployment of TFL controllers in robotics applications like unicycles and quadrotors. The method achieves coverage of 72.0%, 83.4%, and 19.1% of the simulated ROA on a unicycle following a circle and a sinusoid, and on a planar quadrotor, respectively, significantly outperforming the quadratic cost-to-go (11.3%) and two PINN solvers (4.0% and 0.0%). The verification uses interval branch and bound for the inner layer and sample testing for the learned outer layer.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Transverse feedback linearization (TFL) is a nonlinear control technique that exactly linearizes the error dynamics for path following, but its controller is undefined on a singular set (e.g., zero speed for a unicycle). The region of attraction (ROA) is the set of initial states from which the closed-loop system converges to the path without violating constraints. Zubov's method characterizes the ROA via a value function that solves a partial differential equation, and learning-based approaches like physics-informed neural networks (PINNs) have been used to approximate it.

## [EviDARL: Dual-Agent RL Framework for Multi-Table QA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22310) ⭐️ 6.0/10

EviDARL introduces an evidence-centric dual-agent framework with alternating reinforcement learning for multi-table question answering, where an Evidence Construction Agent generates verifiable intermediate traces and a Reasoning and Answering Agent performs multi-hop reasoning over that evidence. Experiments on MMQA and TQA-Bench show it substantially outperforms competitive open-model and multi-table reasoning baselines under constrained model and training budgets. Most LLM-based table reasoning research focuses on single-table settings, so this work addresses the real gap of multi-hop reasoning over evidence dispersed across multiple tables. It could benefit enterprise analytics and data-intensive QA applications where answers require joining and aggregating information from several tables. The Evidence Construction Agent generates and executes table-manipulation programs over complete tables to produce question-specific evidence and verifiable intermediate traces, while the Reasoning and Answering Agent performs multi-hop reasoning over this evidence. EviDARL combines agent-specific fine-grained rewards with parameter-efficient alternating reinforcement learning, optimizing each agent in turn while holding the other fixed to enable bidirectional adaptation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Multi-table question answering (QA) requires answering natural language questions by reasoning over multiple related tables, often involving operations like joins, unions, and aggregations, which is more complex than single-table QA. Reinforcement learning from human feedback (RLHF) is commonly used to align LLMs, and parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) methods like LoRA reduce the cost of adapting large models. EviDARL builds on these ideas by using parameter-efficient alternating RL to train two collaborating agents for multi-table QA.

## [Multistage Contextual Optimization for Ride-Hailing Vehicle Repositioning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22330) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper formulates a multistage linear program for vehicle repositioning in ride-hailing that incorporates a macroscopic fundamental diagram (MFD) linking vehicle accumulation to trip completion, and develops a multistage contextual optimization method that trains demand prediction using decision performance with an exact gradient. On New York City ride-hailing data, this approach yields 7.7% to 9.1% more business benefit than the same predictor trained by mean squared error, outperforms baselines from five paradigms, and even beats realized demand fed to the same policy. This work bridges demand prediction and decision-making by training the predictor directly on downstream decision performance, which could lead to more efficient vehicle repositioning and higher platform profits in ride-hailing. It also introduces a parallel block evaluation algorithm that speeds up training by a factor of 1.86 with eight workers, making the approach more practical for large-scale deployment. The exact gradient consists of three parts: parameter changes affecting forecasts and thus decisions, earlier decisions influencing the current decision through the inherited state, and this state also altering forecasts as context. An equivalent adjoint uses reverse vector-Jacobian products to avoid computing full solution and state-parameter Jacobians, and the parallel block evaluation algorithm is proven to recover the serial trajectory within a finite number of passes with bounded gradient error when stopped early.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Ride-hailing platforms reposition idle vehicles to balance spatial supply and demand, a prediction-driven multistage problem where each stage's forecast and decisions affect future states. The macroscopic fundamental diagram (MFD) is a well-known traffic model that relates vehicle accumulation in an urban network to trip completion rate, providing a reproducible curve for traffic control. Contextual optimization is a data-driven framework that learns policies mapping contextual information to decisions, often by minimizing decision regret rather than prediction error.

## [HealthCLBench: Chinese Medical Benchmark for Context Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22362) ⭐️ 6.0/10

HealthCLBench introduces a Chinese medical benchmark comprising 328 controlled case clusters, 1,103 cases, and 4,931 expert-annotated rubric criteria to evaluate how models acquire, integrate, and apply context in health conversations. The benchmark includes full-context and query-only evaluations of seven general-purpose models and Ant Afu, with GPT-6-Astra leading both full-context metrics at 75.03 and 60.42. This benchmark addresses a gap in Chinese-language medical evaluation by systematically combining explicit contextual difficulties, controlled case variations, and member-specific medical criteria for open-ended answers. It enables more nuanced comparison of models' context-learning abilities in safety-critical health domains, potentially guiding improvements in medical LLM reliability. The benchmark's human review of 700 full-context responses showed 90.5% pooled agreement with automated judgments at the rubric-item level, and across eight systems, full-query differences ranged from 11.83 to 31.76 case points and 10.87 to 38.97 geometric cluster points. Response comparisons revealed failures in checking historical information, expressing uncertainty when warranted, and ensuring recommendations respect required conditions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: In-context learning refers to a large language model's ability to learn new tasks or behaviors at inference time from examples in the prompt without updating its parameters. Rubric-based evaluation decomposes open-ended outputs into explicit criteria, enabling structured and interpretable assessment, which is essential in safety-critical domains like healthcare. HealthCLBench builds on these concepts by creating controlled case clusters where context changes while backgrounds remain shared, testing models' ability to adapt to new evidence or goals.

## [Holding Reference Choice Reverses Visual Recovery Rankings in Robot Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22368) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv preprint introduces AuditEmbodied, a paired execution and trajectory evaluation framework that independently varies holding references and waiting duration while keeping recovery executors frozen. In a 960-execution factorial study, changing the holding reference during a 1-second wait reversed the ranking of CAD-feedback and landmark-based recovery methods, with protected completion counts shifting from 6/40 vs. 19/40 under registered holding to 34/40 vs. 22/40 under position-only holding. This finding establishes holding reference as an explicit evaluation condition that can flip the comparative ranking of visual recovery methods, which is critical for robotics researchers who benchmark recovery performance. It suggests that prior evaluations may have overlooked a key variable, potentially leading to misleading conclusions about method effectiveness. Trajectory analysis localizes the main separation to grasp formation, where small observation-time target residuals precede large tracking errors. On fresh seeds, gradual position reanchoring improves CAD recovery's long-wait completion by 42.5 percentage points and reduces mean complete recovery time by 6.06 seconds relative to registered holding.

**Background**: In robotic manipulation, visual recovery methods help robots regain grasp after disturbances or waits. Holding reference refers to how the robot's pose is defined during a wait—either registered (using full pose tracking) or position-only (using only position). This study shows that the choice of holding reference can significantly affect which recovery method performs better, highlighting the need for careful evaluation design.

## [ROSMonitoring Extended to Monitor and Enforce ROS 2 Actions at Runtime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22407) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper extends ROSMonitoring, a runtime verification framework for ROS 2, to support actions by reconstructing their lifecycle and translating violations into protocol-aware enforcement operations such as goal rejection, cancellation, and message suppression. The implementation automatically instruments actions declared in the monitoring configuration and correlates events via goal identifiers. ROS 2 actions are used for long-running tasks like navigation and manipulation, but existing monitoring tools only covered topics and services, leaving a gap in safety assurance for complex robotic behaviors. This work enables runtime enforcement for actions, which could improve the reliability and safety of autonomous systems. The evaluation uses controlled stress tests varying action invocations, workload size, and client-server topology, showing approximately linear growth in monitoring cost with repeated invocations under fixed workload, but additional overhead with larger workloads and more client-server pairs. The current implementation is synchronous and sequential, which limits performance.

## [PACD: Training-Free Decoding Method to Reduce Cross-Modal Hallucinations in Omnimodal LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22456) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces Provenance-Aware Consensus Decoding (PACD), a training-free framework that mitigates cross-modal hallucinations in omnimodal large language models by using two complementary paths: Relevance-Guided Candidate Arbitration for non-consensus cases and Provenance-Based Consensus Recovery for consensus cases. Experiments on four Omni-LLMs and five benchmarks show up to 27.6% relative improvement over standard decoding on cross-modal hallucination benchmarks and an average 7.7% relative improvement over the best baseline on general audio-visual reasoning benchmarks. Cross-modal hallucinations—where one modality improperly influences predictions about another—are a major obstacle to deploying omnimodal models in reliable real-world applications. PACD offers a training-free solution that can be applied to existing models without retraining, potentially improving trustworthiness in audio-visual reasoning tasks. PACD operates at decoding time and does not require additional training or fine-tuning. It uses relevance-guided candidate arbitration to identify task-relevant unimodal evidence and selectively arbitrate between predictions, and provenance-based consensus recovery to evaluate layer-wise modality evidence consistency and strength under confidence constraints to resolve erroneous consensus.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Omnimodal large language models (Omni-LLMs) integrate audio, video, and text to perform multimodal reasoning, but they are prone to cross-modal hallucinations where one modality incorrectly influences another. Existing training-free decoding methods attempt to mitigate this via modality perturbation or relevance-based weighting, but they can suppress correct unimodal predictions and allow erroneous consensus to persist. PACD addresses these limitations by introducing provenance-aware consensus decoding.

## [COTTI: Contrastive Training and Time-Aware Inference for Robust Industrial Recommendation under Promotional Drift](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22597) ⭐️ 6.0/10

COTTI is a new industrial recommendation framework that separates historical representation learning from event-time score adjustment to handle promotional drift. It introduces Cross-Period Contrastive Alignment (CPC), Active Re-weighting (RW), and Adaptive Boundary Alignment (ABA), achieving an offline AUC of 0.8127 on Fliggy data compared to 0.8123 for TEN with ABA. Promotional events cause significant shifts in user behavior and traffic composition, making recommendation models less accurate during sales or holidays. COTTI's approach to decouple representation learning from event-time adjustment could help industrial systems maintain performance during such periods, as evidenced by a 3.14% relative gain in conversion rate in a ten-day online experiment. COTTI achieves a p99 latency of 38.9 ms and throughput of 14,700 requests/s, with ABA adding only 2.2 ms to p99 latency. However, cross-category analysis reveals improved discrimination alongside declines in paid-item recall for novel categories, indicating a trade-off between aggregate prediction and novel-category discovery.

**Background**: Promotional drift refers to the changes in user behavior and data distribution during promotional events, which can degrade the performance of recommendation models trained on historical data. Contrastive learning is a technique that learns representations by pulling similar samples together and pushing dissimilar ones apart, and time-aware inference adjusts predictions based on temporal context. Fliggy is a travel platform by Alibaba, and its dataset is commonly used in recommender system research.

The paper introduces a technique that uses a Dirichlet Process Gaussian Mixture Model (DPGMM) to cluster internal representations across all nodes of each Convnet layer, enabling visualization of learned features. This approach addresses the high dimensionality and abstraction of deep layers, and the authors discuss its utility for transfer learning. A Gaussian Mixture Model (GMM) is a probabilistic model that assumes data comes from a mixture of Gaussian distributions, and the Dirichlet Process (DP) is a prior that allows the number of mixture components to be infinite, so the model can automatically determine the number of clusters. Transfer learning reuses a model trained on one task for a different task, and understanding what features are learned helps decide which layers to transfer.

## [AI-Assisted Proof Tightens Convergence Rate for Stochastic Multi-Gradient Descent](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.18174) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper establishes an improved convergence rate of Õ(T^{-1}) for stochastic multi-gradient descent (SMG) in smooth nonconvex multi-objective optimization, up from the previous Õ(T^{-1/4}) bound by Chen et al. (2024). The proof was initially generated by ChatGPT 5.4 Thinking Extended in response to a homework prompt and then verified and reorganized by the author. This closes a significant theoretical gap in the convergence analysis of stochastic multi-gradient methods, which are widely used in multi-task learning and multi-objective optimization. The AI-assisted proof also highlights the growing role of large language models in mathematical discovery and theoretical research. The improvement exploits the Lipschitz continuity of the Pareto-stationarity (PS) measure defined by the MGDA direction norm, rather than the (1/2)-Hölder continuity used previously, and does not require bounded gradients. For MoCo and MoCo+ variants with exact MGDA updates and constant batch sizes, the analysis yields rates of O(T^{-1/2}) and Õ(T^{-2/3}), matching single-objective momentum rates up to logarithmic factors.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Multi-objective optimization (MOO) seeks to simultaneously optimize several potentially conflicting objectives, and a common approach is to find a Pareto-stationary point where no objective can be improved without worsening another. The multiple-gradient descent algorithm (MGDA) computes a steepest common descent direction from the gradients of the objectives, and its stochastic version (SMG) uses stochastic gradients. Convergence rates measure how quickly the algorithm approaches a Pareto-stationary point as the number of iterations T grows.

## [Systematic review finds fragmented metrics for AI coding assistant productivity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21632) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.21632) presents a staged systematic literature review of AI coding assistants' impact on developer productivity, identifying 187 unique productivity markers across studies, with individual studies typically using only five. The review finds that reported effects are often positive but stronger in perceived and tendency-based findings than in objective or statistically evaluated ones. The lack of comparability across studies makes it difficult to draw cumulative conclusions about AI coding assistants' real impact, which affects tool vendors, engineering leaders, and researchers seeking evidence-based decisions. This review provides a structured basis for making productivity evidence more interpretable and multidimensional. The review uses the SPACE framework as a theory-informed lens to capture human-centered outcomes like satisfaction and well-being, revealing that explicit productivity research is dominated by time-, output-, and artifact-oriented proxies. The abstract is truncated, limiting visibility into the full findings and novelty.

## [Multimodal LLM Explanations Boost Perceived Understanding and Trust in XAI Visualizations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21638) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A pre-registered within-subject experiment (N=128) across seven decision scenarios and five XAI methods found that augmenting XAI visualizations with multimodal LLM-generated textual explanations improved perceived understanding by 0.35 points and perceived trust by 0.26 points on five-point scales (both p < .001). The gains were consistent across all scenarios, did not differ between local and global explanations, and were largest for participants with the least prior XAI exposure. This suggests that multimodal LLMs can serve as an effective interpretive layer for XAI, making explanations more accessible to non-expert audiences and potentially broadening the impact of explainable AI in real-world decision-making. It also highlights a promising direction for improving user trust in AI systems through natural language augmentation. The study measured only perceived understanding and trust, not actual comprehension or behavioral trust, and used a within-subject design that may be subject to demand characteristics. A mediation analysis indicated that the effect on perceived trust was mediated by perceived understanding.

## [Depth-Aware Activation Steering via Attention Residuals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21655) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper combines Attention Residuals (AttnRes) with Jacobian-lens (J-space) concept readout to create a lightweight depth-aware activation steering pipeline that distributes persona vectors across layers instead of injecting at a single layer. On Qwen3-0.6B and Qwen3.5-0.8B, this method yields stronger internal alignment (deeper-layer concept cosine and rank, p<0.0001) and stronger behavioral shifts than single-layer injection. This work advances activation steering by introducing depth-aware propagation and internal verification, addressing a key limitation of existing single-layer methods. It provides a practical, low-overhead approach with a careful evaluation protocol, potentially benefiting researchers and practitioners working on training-free LLM control and interpretability. The effect is sign-specific: reverse vectors invert all six metrics, random directions degrade alignment (p<0.0001), and a short LoRA fine-tune erases the imprint. Injection rewires attention strictly downstream (upstream JS = 0.00000), and the method exhibits an intensity window (effects monotone over 0.3-0.7, then generation collapses) and metric discipline (keyword-rule metrics fail LLM-judge validation, kappa <= 0.09).

The Jacobian-lens (J-space) is a method to read verbalizable concepts from a model's internal representations at each token.

## [AdaFlowLite enables opportunistic inference on asynchronous mobile data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21691) ⭐️ 6.0/10

AdaFlowLite introduces opportunistic inference for asynchronous multi-modal mobile data, allowing inference to begin as soon as partial data arrives. It uses a hierarchical analysis-based normalized matrix to model cross-modality affinity and a multi-modal lightweight Swin Transformer (MMLST) for real-time data imputation without retraining. This approach addresses the trade-off between latency and accuracy in distributed sensing tasks like smart cabins and driving assistance, where sensor data arrives at different times. It could significantly improve real-time performance and energy efficiency on mobile and edge devices. Experiments show AdaFlowLite reduces inference latency by up to 80.4%, improves accuracy by up to 62.1%, and cuts energy consumption by nearly 50% compared to existing methods. The method also enhances LLM performance by preprocessing asynchronous data.

AdaFlowLite builds on the AdaFlow concept of opportunistic inference, which uses modal affinity to balance latency and accuracy.

## [TIBECG: Temporal Information Bottleneck for Lead-Specific Demographic Privacy in ECG Waveform Release](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21694) ⭐️ 6.0/10

TIBECG introduces an architectural temporal information bottleneck that suppresses age and sex inference from individual ECG leads while retaining at least 95% of each lead's clean diagnostic macro-F1. Evaluated on 6,386 recordings across 12 leads and 21 latent lengths, it outperformed the best conventional transformation on all leads by a median 22.08 percentage points in reducing age/sex interval gain. ECG waveforms can leak demographic information beyond the intended diagnostic task, so this work offers a privacy-preserving approach that maintains diagnostic utility, which is important for safe sharing of medical signals in cloud-based or proprietary diagnostic services. It also highlights the need for lead-specific evaluation rather than one-size-fits-all privacy transformations. The processor has 2,302,337 parameters and varies latent length without using age or sex labels in protector fitting; direct downsampling suppresses demographic inference more strongly but at a larger loss of diagnostic utility, and lower latent KL divergence does not reliably rank residual sex leakage at a fixed length. The findings support lead-specific evaluation but not validated consumer deployment or expert-readable reconstruction.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: The information bottleneck method is an information-theoretic technique introduced by Tishby, Pereira, and Bialek that seeks a compressed representation preserving relevant information while discarding irrelevant information. In ECG analysis, demographic factors like age and sex are known to influence waveform characteristics, and deep learning models can inadvertently learn to infer these attributes from ECG data, raising privacy concerns when waveforms are transmitted or shared.

## [ActiGrid Benchmark: Learned DQN Policy Fails to Beat Simple Entropy Stopping for Power System Diagnosis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21695) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces ActiGrid, a benchmark for active evidence acquisition in cyber-physical power system diagnosis, and finds that a Double Deep Q-Network (DQN) policy is statistically indistinguishable from calibrated entropy stopping on the TU Delft digital substation benchmark and Pareto dominated by simpler controls on the MSU/ORNL attack benchmark. Compact subset classifiers also outperform the learned policy while reading fewer telemetry sources on both benchmarks. This negative result challenges the assumption that reinforcement learning adds value to evidence acquisition in safety-critical diagnosis, suggesting that simpler, interpretable stopping rules may suffice and that practitioners should benchmark against strong baselines before deploying complex learned policies. Entropy stopping over subset classifiers achieved the highest mean Macro-F1 of 0.928 on TU Delft while reading only 57% of sources, but on MSU/ORNL no method exceeded 0.45 Macro-F1 and entropy stopping brought no savings over a static three-source subset. The DQN only requests additional sources or stops to diagnose and never operates grid actuators, and the classifier is trained to tolerate missing modalities.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Cyber-physical power systems generate many telemetry sources, and diagnostic models often inspect all of them for every event, even though some events are clear from a single source. Active evidence acquisition aims to sequentially select which sources to read, balancing diagnostic accuracy against the cost of acquiring more evidence. Double Deep Q-Network (DQN) is a reinforcement learning algorithm that mitigates overestimation bias in Q-learning by decoupling action selection from action evaluation, and it is often used for sequential decision-making tasks.

## [Quark Mass Hierarchies Obstructed in Heterotic String Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21751) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper identifies two tree-level mechanisms by which complex structure moduli could generate quark mass hierarchies in smooth heterotic Calabi-Yau compactifications, but shows analytically and numerically that both are obstructed in previously studied tetra-quadric models. Scanning databases of tens of thousands of complete intersection Calabi-Yau line bundle models, the authors find a unique smooth quotient admitting a rank-three up-quark Yukawa matrix and obtain realistic top, charm, up, and bottom quark mass hierarchies after renormalization group evolution. This work provides a negative result that rules out certain perturbative mechanisms in a well-studied class of string compactifications, narrowing the search space for realistic fermion masses in string phenomenology. It also demonstrates a concrete path to realistic quark mass hierarchies in the smooth supergravity regime, which is important for connecting string theory to Standard Model observables. The analysis combines holomorphic superpotential integrals, which can be computed algebraically, with matter field Kähler metrics that require solving a coupled system of partial differential equations for the Ricci-flat metric, Hermitian Yang-Mills connections, and harmonic bundle-valued forms. The unique viable model is a smooth quotient from complete intersection Calabi-Yau line bundle constructions, and the physical couplings are evaluated and run down via renormalization group equations to match observed hierarchies.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Heterotic string theory is a ten-dimensional framework in which extra dimensions are compactified on Calabi-Yau manifolds to yield four-dimensional physics. In these compactifications, the Yukawa couplings that determine fermion masses arise from overlap integrals of wavefunctions in the extra dimensions, and their values depend on the geometry and on bundles (Hermitian Yang-Mills connections) that encode gauge fields. The Standard Model quark masses span over five orders of magnitude, so understanding how such hierarchies emerge from string theory is a long-standing challenge.

## [ARC-2 adds continuous compliance via Delta Management for autonomous systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21811) ⭐️ 6.0/10

ARC-2 introduces Delta Management, a continuous governance framework that measures deviations between a system's current operational state and its certified baseline region, replacing static certification with continuous monitoring. It proposes four extensions: worst-case CRAB evaluation with outlier quarantine, a layered trigger taxonomy, a graduated safe transition protocol, and environmental delta as a baseline validity condition. This work addresses the post-deployment compliance gap left by ARC 1 and aligns with EU AI Act Article 72(2) continuous compliance obligations, potentially influencing how autonomous systems are governed throughout their operational lifecycle. It could affect regulators, developers, and operators of autonomous robots and AI systems in regulated domains. The framework defines compliance relative to a baseline region rather than a fixed point, quarantines isolated outliers to avoid unjustified restrictions, and distinguishes hard safety violations, single-dimension failures, correlated multi-system degradation, and composite risk indicators for proportionate responses. Environmental drift beyond the certified Operational Design Domain triggers recertification or ODD revision instead of automatic authority contraction.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: ARC 1 was a three-layer governance architecture for autonomous systems, comprising model safety validation, cognitive certification (CRAB), and operational authorization (LEA), but it only covered deployment-time certification. Continuous compliance frameworks, such as the open-source Continuous Compliance Framework, automate ongoing checks against standards like NIST and SOC 2, and the EU AI Act requires post-market monitoring for high-risk AI systems. Delta Management extends these ideas to autonomous systems by monitoring deviations from a certified baseline.

## [Cancellation-Free Constrained SquareCB for Safe Controller Selection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21880) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper proposes a cancellation-free constrained SquareCB algorithm that uses a rectified weight on the cost instead of a Lagrange multiplier, achieving regret O(√(KT Reg_Sq(T))/δ) and violation O(√(KT Reg_Sq(T))) under realizability and Slater's condition. In experiments on a safe-control-gym quadrotor and a BOPTEST building, it reduces violation by 6 to 9 times compared to primal-dual and virtual-queue baselines. This work addresses a key limitation of existing reductions to online regression for constrained contextual bandits, where slack in some rounds can offset violations in others, and provides a theoretically sound and practically effective method for safe controller selection. The algorithm requires only a regression oracle and works even when the budget must be met in every context, including adversarially chosen contexts. The bounds depend on the regression oracle's error Reg_Sq(T) and Slater margin δ, and the rectified weight is zero when estimated cost is within budget, otherwise just large enough to bring it back to budget.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Contextual bandits are a framework for sequential decision-making where an agent chooses actions based on context to minimize loss. Constrained contextual bandits add a budget constraint on the expected cost, and common approaches use Lagrange multipliers or virtual queues to enforce it. SquareCB is a regression-based algorithm for contextual bandits that achieves strong regret guarantees by exploring according to the regression oracle's uncertainty.

## [Algebraic framework evaluates data streams with delayed labels and distribution shifts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21914) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper extends an algebraic evaluation framework to data streams by weighting confusion matrix counts over time using exponential fading or sliding windows, achieving O(m^2) update cost per step regardless of stream length. It incorporates delayed and selective labels via an observation mask, where instances are counted with calibrated class probabilities until their true labels arrive, and separates prior shift from concept drift using prior-free coordinates (recall and specificity) with a first-order Jacobian decomposition. This work addresses a critical challenge in streaming evaluation: distinguishing degraded model performance from changes in class prevalence, which is essential for reliable monitoring in online learning systems. The proposed methods enable unbiased pooled and per-class measures with constant update cost, making them practical for real-time applications and potentially improving decision-making in dynamic environments. The framework guarantees that each pooled measure remains a weighted combination of per-class measures, and audited conventions report no value for classes without verified instances. On synthetic and four real datasets, a detector using prior-free coordinates with per-class memory raises no excess false alarms under label shift, unlike Jacobian and error-rate detectors, though correction for delayed labels only partially recovers precision, specificity, and F1. Twenty-five statements are formally verified in Lean 4, and the code is available in the aggmetrics package.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: In data stream learning, models must continuously update as new data arrives, but labels often come with delays or only for a subset of instances, and the underlying data distribution can shift over time (concept drift) or the class balance can change (prior shift). Traditional evaluation metrics assume immediate and complete labels, leading to biased estimates when these assumptions are violated. Exponential fading and sliding windows are common techniques to weight recent data more heavily, while calibration ensures that predicted probabilities reflect true likelihoods. This paper builds on an algebraic framework that derives micro and macro measures from a confusion matrix, extending it to handle the complexities of streaming environments.

## [hETM: Hierarchical Embedded Topic Model for Document and Section Structures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21980) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces the Hierarchical Embedded Topic Model (hETM), which couples the topic distribution of a document and those of its sections through a hierarchical prior in the embedding space. It derives an amortized variational inference procedure for hETM and shows that it achieves the lowest or near-lowest held-out perplexity among flat and hierarchical topic model baselines on structured collections from legal, corporate/government, and online forum domains. Many real-world document collections, such as legal documents, company and government reports, and online discussion forums, have internal section structure that flat topic models ignore. hETM's ability to model this hierarchy could improve topic modeling and downstream tasks like clustering for these domains, offering a more structured alternative to standard embedded topic models. hETM extends embedded topic models by introducing a hierarchical prior in the embedding space and uses amortized variational inference for efficient training. Experiments show it achieves the best clustering performance on most collections, with comparable or higher topic quality, though the paper does not report major breakthroughs beyond these incremental gains.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Embedded topic models integrate word embeddings into the topic generation process, producing coherent topics and robust performance on large vocabularies, but they represent each document by a single topic distribution. Hierarchical topic models aim to capture nested structure, such as documents containing sections with distinct subtopics. Amortized variational inference is a technique that uses a shared neural network to approximate posterior distributions, enabling efficient inference for large datasets. Held-out perplexity is a common evaluation metric for topic models, where lower values indicate better predictive performance on unseen documents.

## [RLDE: Reinforcement Learning Framework for Interpretable Document Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22039) ⭐️ 6.0/10

RLDE formulates long-document revision as a reference-free Markov decision process with a limited action space (NOOP, INSERT, DELETE, REPLACE), using Pointer Networks for token-level span selection and PPO agents to optimize readability, perplexity, and grammar rewards. On the BEA-2019 dataset, it improved Flesch Reading Ease from 49.8 to 59.4 and reduced perplexity from 63.7 to 39.8 while preserving 93% of semantic information. This work offers an interpretable, low-cost alternative to black-box LLM rewriting, showing that RL with explicit edit actions can match or exceed chain-of-thought prompted models like GPT-4 and LLaMA-2 on document revision metrics. It could benefit applications requiring transparent, iterative text refinement, such as academic editing and content moderation. RLDE uses a frozen BART-base model to generate context-aware suggestions and introduces a novel SBERT-based Semantic Preservation Penalty to avoid deletion degeneracy. Human evaluations scored 4.9/5 for meaning preservation and 4.4/5 for edit quality, with statistically significant improvements (p < 0.01) across all metrics.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Pointer Networks are neural architectures that use attention to select elements from the input sequence as output, enabling variable-size output dictionaries. Proximal Policy Optimization (PPO) is a popular reinforcement learning algorithm that penalizes large policy changes between updates for stable training. BART-base is a transformer encoder-decoder model pre-trained by corrupting text and learning to reconstruct it, often used for text generation tasks.

## [Segmentation-Guided Model Directly Estimates Rooftop PV Capacity from Aerial Imagery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22076) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose Direct Head, a segmentation-guided neural architecture that directly regresses rooftop photovoltaic capacity from aerial imagery, avoiding the conventional two-step approach of segmenting PV arrays and multiplying area by a power density estimate. Evaluated on 7,590 French rooftop installations, it reduces mean absolute error by 8.7% and 12.5% compared to a parameter-matched area-density neural model. Accurate installation-level PV capacity inventories are crucial for distribution planners, and this method improves estimation accuracy without relying on complete interconnection records. The approach could enhance grid planning and solar energy integration, benefiting utilities and energy modelers. Direct Head retains U-Net segmentation and soft-mask pooling but directly regresses nonnegative capacity from PV-region features and area descriptors. It was tested under the original split and a department-based holdout, showing symmetric mean absolute percentage error reductions of 14.0% and 20.7%, respectively.

This work proposes bypassing the area-density product by directly regressing capacity from learned features.

## [AUC-Based Ranking Unifies Contrastive and Generative Visual Representation Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22094) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.22094) proposes AUC Generative Reconstruction (AUCGR) and Partial AUC Generative Reconstruction (pAUCGR), which reformulate generative reconstruction as a discriminative ranking problem by ordering matched versus mismatched reconstruction outputs. This embeds discriminative supervision directly into the generative process, avoiding the gradient interference that arises when contrastive and generative losses are naively combined. Multimodal LLMs depend on visual encoders that must simultaneously capture fine-grained perceptual detail and class-discriminative semantics, and the long-standing trade-off between contrastive and generative objectives has forced awkward loss-weight tuning. A plug-and-play objective that unifies both without architectural changes could simplify visual encoder training and improve representation quality for downstream MLLMs. AUCGR optimizes the area under the ROC curve so that matched reconstruction pairs receive higher scores than mismatched ones, while pAUCGR restricts comparisons to reliable feature subspaces to mitigate supervision bias. The authors provide theoretical connections to AUC/pAUC optimization with generalization guarantees and report experiments showing gains in both discriminative accuracy and fine-grained perceptual representation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Contrastive learning trains encoders to pull similar samples together and push dissimilar ones apart, yielding strong class discrimination but suppressing intra-class variation, whereas generative reconstruction (e.g., autoencoding or diffusion-based tokenizers) emphasizes pixel-level fidelity without explicit comparative supervision. Combining these losses in a shared latent space often causes optimization conflicts and instability. AUC is a standard ranking metric measuring the probability that a positive example is scored above a negative one, and this paper leverages that ranking view to fold discrimination into reconstruction.

## [EgoHandWAM probes hand motion's role in egocentric world action modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22151) ⭐️ 6.0/10

EgoHandWAM forecasts 3D wrist and fingertip trajectories and future visual features from current egocentric images and hand states, trained on 751,065 EgoDex windows with a frozen DINOv2 encoder. The action-only model reduces two-second wrist endpoint error from 60.26 mm to 56.48 mm, and adding future-feature supervision yields a 1.32% smooth L1 error reduction over copying the current feature. This work quantifies how much hand motion actually informs egocentric world models, showing that gains are real but modest, which helps set realistic expectations for embodied AI systems that rely on hand trajectories for forecasting. It also provides a large-scale benchmark and careful ablations that can guide future research in egocentric vision and dexterous manipulation. The model still underestimates long reaches, and shuffling hand histories across sessions erases the visual-feature gain, indicating the model relies on session-specific context. In a separate four-task probe, adding recorded future displacements and tracking-validity masks reduces visual-feature error by 3.93%, but masks alone give little improvement and a predicted trajectory adds little; on two simulated RoboTwin tasks, substituting aligned human examples for robot examples gives no consistent success increase across three seeds.

**Background**: Egocentric vision involves first-person camera views, often from head-mounted devices like Apple Vision Pro, and is used to study dexterous manipulation. World action models aim to predict future observations and actions from current sensory input, which is important for robotics and augmented reality. DINOv2 is a self-supervised vision transformer from Meta that produces strong visual features without fine-tuning, and EgoDex is a large-scale dataset of 829 hours of egocentric video with 3D hand and upper-body pose annotations collected via ARKit.

## [Single-chain reasoning lock-in tested across aspectual and pragmatic ambiguity in Qwen3-8B](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22188) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A preregistered study on Qwen3-8B-Q8_0 (16,640 formal units, temperature=0.0) tested the single-chain premature lock-in hypothesis across four experiments, finding that 15 of 16 hypothesis slots retained confirmatory status. The paper introduces Multi-Candidate Verbalization (MCV), which elicits K=5 weighted estimates in one call, and reports large effects such as a 25.00 pp difference between Direct-CoT and MCU on telic high-confidence completion and a -91.25 pp collapse of NLI-CoT on uninterrupted atelic items. This work provides a methodologically rigorous, preregistered framework for probing how LLMs commit to a single interpretation under ambiguity, which is relevant for evaluating reasoning reliability in computational linguistics and LLM evaluation. The dissociation between discrete NLI-CoT and scalar Direct-CoT judgments across models suggests that prompt-and-readout protocols can substantially shape observed reasoning behavior. The study uses sealed formal experiments with Holm control at alpha_fam=0.05, and includes a 1,800-call OpenAI ablation plus a cross-model extension across five external models (4,222 valid units, <2% missing per slice). The authors caution that confirmatory effects concern observable prompt-and-readout protocols, while internal premature lock-in remains a hypothesis; all code, stimuli, and analysis plans are available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: The single-chain premature lock-in hypothesis posits that LLMs may commit early to one reasoning path and fail to revise it when ambiguity arises. Aspectual ambiguity concerns whether an event merely occurred, reached a telic culmination, or satisfied a plan, while pragmatic ambiguity involves multiple standards of evaluation. Multi-Candidate Verbalization (MCV) is a readout method that asks the model to produce multiple weighted candidate interpretations in a single call, contrasting with unweighted (MCU) and self-weighted (MCW) readouts.

The paper introduces the Educational Guardrail System (EGS), a lightweight, model-agnostic runtime framework that complements intrinsic alignment for educational LLMs through query moderation, scenario-aware pedagogical guidance, and response auditing. Experiments across general and education-specific benchmarks show that EGS improves moderation performance, refusal quality, and jailbreak robustness across diverse LLMs. This work addresses the real risk of deploying LLMs to adolescents by moving beyond binary refusal to contextual guidance, which could improve both safety and pedagogical utility in educational AI. It offers a practical, model-agnostic approach that can be adopted across different LLMs, potentially influencing AI safety practices in education. EGS triages inputs by risk level, redirects gray-zone queries with category-specific educational interventions, and audits outputs before delivery. A new arXiv paper (2611.22268) argues that philosophers' attempts to develop uniform decision rules for cases where physicians' judgments diverge from medical AI recommendations are 'fatally flawed' because such cases are context-specific empirical medical matters, not epistemological ones. The author distinguishes useful 'anticipation' (exploring plausible futures under uncertainty) from wasteful 'speculation' (abstracting away uncertainty) in technology ethics. This critique challenges a growing body of philosophical work that seeks crisp, general solutions to human-AI disagreement in medicine, suggesting such solutions may abstract away from determinative contextual factors. It also offers a framework for evaluating when futures thinking in AI ethics is productive versus merely speculative, relevant to policymakers and ethicists. The paper contends that general decision rules can only be stipulated by abstracting away from relevant contextual circumstances, and that efforts to develop crisp philosophical solutions to physician-AI disagreement fall into the 'speculation' category. It calls for technology ethics to begin from and return to the messy, uncertain present.

## [FIRE-Drive: Feasibility-Informed Risk Evaluation for Diffusion Planning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22297) ⭐️ 6.0/10

FIRE-Drive introduces a feasibility-informed risk evaluation framework for diffusion-based end-to-end autonomous driving, combining dynamic feasible anchor initialization, candidate-specific bird's-eye-view risk evidence, and compact high-quality candidate supervision with lightweight refinement. On NAVSIM v1, it achieves a Predictive Driver Model Score (PDMS) of 91.5 with a ResNet34 backbone, and closed-loop evaluation on 220 Bench2Drive routes yields a Driving Score of 71.43 and a Success Rate of 42.73%. This work addresses key bottlenecks in diffusion-based planners—initialization, mode selection, and supervision—by aligning them under a unified objective, which could improve the safety and reliability of end-to-end autonomous driving systems. The reported results on standard benchmarks suggest practical gains for real-world deployment. The method uses dynamic feasible anchors to avoid infeasible proposals from noisy initialization, and a scorer that leverages local BEV risk evidence to distinguish geometrically similar but safety-different trajectories. Ablations confirm the contributions of feasible initialization, auxiliary supervision, and risk-informed selection, though evaluation is limited to NAVSIM v1 and Bench2Drive.

## [AI Conversational Assistant Boosts Human-Centric Building Digital Twin Usability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22350) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers designed, implemented, and evaluated an AI-driven conversational assistant integrated into a human-centric Building Digital Twin System (BDTS) for Indoor Environmental Quality monitoring, deployed at New York University Abu Dhabi. A controlled two-arm between-subjects quasi-experimental study compared the baseline interface with the experimental interface, showing significant reductions in task-completion time and perceived workload. This work demonstrates that conversational AI can make complex building data more accessible to non-specialist stakeholders, potentially improving decision-making and operational efficiency in smart buildings. It contributes an empirically evaluated architecture that separates generative interaction from deterministic environmental assessment, offering a reusable design pattern for human-centric digital twins. The study used wave-based, non-randomized assignment, and while accuracy and usability improved, these improvements did not meet the adjusted significance criterion. The architecture coordinates conversational responses with adaptive visual presentation and auto-suggest functionality, and the evaluation was conducted as a quasi-experiment rather than a fully randomized controlled trial.

## [nnU-Net-Based Framework Reuses Segmentation Features for 3D Medical Image Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22380) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper proposes a self-configuring framework that extends nnU-Net to perform both segmentation and classification in 3D medical imaging by reusing representations from a segmentation model. It investigates two information-sharing strategies: a frozen-backbone branch that augments features with region-of-interest information, predicted foreground volume, and segmentation confidence, and an adaptation branch that partially fine-tunes the final encoder stage with an auxiliary segmentation loss. The framework achieved an Overall score of 0.8056 on the public validation leaderboard and ranked first in the official FLARE 2026 Task 2 final evaluation, with the highest DSC, NSD, and AUROC across all four testing tasks. This work demonstrates that segmentation representations can be efficiently reused for classification without building a separate model, potentially reducing computational cost and development time for multi-task medical imaging pipelines. Its top ranking in the FLARE 2026 challenge and compliance with strict inference limits (under 60 seconds and 4 GB GPU memory per case) suggest practical value for clinical deployment. The framework builds on nnU-Net's self-configuring pipeline and uses a lightweight classifier on encoder features and predicted segmentation probabilities. Five-fold experiments showed that more complex configurations did not consistently improve classification performance, and local inference measurements remained below the challenge limits of 60 seconds per case and 4 GB GPU memory.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: nnU-Net is a self-configuring method for deep learning-based biomedical image segmentation that automatically adapts its pipeline to a given dataset, and it has become a standard baseline in medical imaging challenges. Multi-task learning, which jointly trains segmentation and classification, is an active research area, but reusing a pretrained segmentation model for classification is less explored. The FLARE challenge series focuses on abdominal organ segmentation and related tasks, providing a benchmark for efficient and accurate 3D medical image analysis.

## [RICoT: Recurrent Interleaved Chain-of-Thought for Page Stream Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22382) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose RICoT, a prompting framework that treats page stream segmentation as a sequential process over the entire document stream, using a sliding window with a compact carried-forward record to keep per-call cost bounded. They also introduce FinancePSS-205, a new benchmark of 205 real-world financial page streams, and show that RICoT matches or outperforms pairwise and windowed prompting on three open-weight LLMs. This work reframes page stream segmentation for LLMs as a sequential reading task, moving beyond local boundary classification and potentially enabling more accurate and scalable document splitting in real-world workflows. The public release of FinancePSS-205 addresses the lack of disclosed methods and benchmarks in commercial document-splitting APIs, which could spur further research and reproducibility. RICoT processes pages within a sliding window, committing boundary decisions before moving on, and carries forward a compact record across windows; on a TABME test subset, RICoT with GLM-5 achieves document F1 0.847 and boundary F1 0.915, above published zero-shot results. A 2B-parameter student model trained on a larger teacher's decisions performs comparably with and without page-level reasoning, coming within 0.05 document F1 of the strongest prompted model on FinancePSS-205.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Page Stream Segmentation (PSS) is the task of splitting a continuous stream of scanned pages into distinct documents by detecting boundaries, often using OCR, layout analysis, and visual features. Chain-of-thought (CoT) prompting is a technique that elicits reasoning in large language models by instructing them to generate intermediate reasoning steps, improving performance on complex tasks. RICoT combines these ideas, applying CoT in a recurrent, interleaved manner to handle long page streams efficiently.

## [Five-Layer Framework Audits Benchmark Accuracy on UAV Intent Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22434) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper proposes a five-layer framework—observation, representation, decoding, use, and behaviour—to audit whether benchmark accuracy reflects true model understanding, and instantiates it on the AeroIntent UAV intent recognition benchmark. The audit reveals an unreliable observation layer on 7 of 20 Drone programs, weak feature correlations (e.g., RAFT flow radial bias r=+0.257), and a global out-of-sample correlation of only r=+0.080, far below the minimum detectable effect of |r|=0.599. This work challenges the common assumption that high benchmark accuracy equates to model understanding, offering a structured methodology to separate encoding, decodability, and use. It could influence how benchmark designers and evaluation specialists validate their metrics, particularly in safety-critical domains like UAV intent recognition. The decoding layer sets the minimum detectable effect at |r|=0.599 under a Bonferroni threshold (α=0.00714, power 0.80), and the observed r=+0.080 has power 0.00; injection experiments confirm the MDE condition. The behaviour layer shows accuracy is consistent with appearance cues (0.6552) rather than bounding-box trajectory (0.4080), with a cluster-level paired difference of +0.2471 (p=0.0132).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Benchmark accuracy is often interpreted as evidence that a model has learned the intended construct, but this inference can be flawed if the benchmark's measurements are noisy or if models exploit shortcuts. The AeroIntent benchmark is a simulation-based dataset of 990 synchronized multimodal sequences from 22 trajectory programs across five mission intents, designed for UAV intent recognition. RAFT (Recurrent All-Pairs Field Transforms) is a state-of-the-art deep learning method for optical flow estimation, used here to compute motion features. The minimum detectable effect (MDE) is the smallest true effect an experiment can reliably detect given its sample size and statistical power.

## [LeViT-UNet++ Uses Haar Wavelets for Efficient Medical Image Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22482) ⭐️ 6.0/10

LeViT-UNet++ introduces a Haar Wavelet Multi-Scale Downsampling (HWMD) module that decomposes features into low- and high-frequency components to preserve fine details during downsampling, combined with a depthwise convolution-based decoder. On the Synapse benchmark, it achieves a DSC of 76.57% with only 8.025M parameters and 241.72 FPS, evaluated across four datasets (Synapse, ACDC, LA2018, MSD Heart) in both 2D and 3D settings. Efficient medical image segmentation is critical for real-time clinical applications like surgical navigation and radiation therapy, where computational resources are limited. This work offers a favorable accuracy-efficiency trade-off, potentially enabling deployment on resource-constrained devices without sacrificing segmentation quality. The HWMD module mitigates detail loss by using Haar wavelet decomposition, while the depthwise convolution-based decoder integrates multi-scale global representations from Transformer blocks with local spatial features. The compact model size and fast inference make it suitable for real-time use, though the approach is an incremental variant of existing LeViT-UNet and UNet++ architectures.

**Background**: Medical image segmentation involves delineating anatomical structures in images like CT or MRI, often using encoder-decoder architectures such as U-Net and its variants (e.g., UNet++). Transformers like LeViT provide global context modeling but can be computationally heavy, while downsampling operations (e.g., pooling) may lose fine details. Haar wavelet transform is a technique that decomposes images into frequency sub-bands, preserving both approximation and detail information, and has been used in downsampling modules to reduce information loss.

## [FLEX: Signed Feature Attribution for Multi-Label Classifiers via Fisher LDA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22515) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces FLEX (Fisher LDA Explainer), a global surrogate model that fits a single multi-label Fisher LDA over the full binary label matrix to produce signed feature attributions for each label in a coherent coordinate system. Experiments show that FLEX achieves consistently high fidelity across benchmarks and classifier families, with a monotonically increasing fidelity-complexity Pareto curve. This work addresses the challenge of explaining multi-label classifiers, where correct predictions may arise from diffuse feature combinations rather than a single dominant feature. FLEX provides practitioners with a predictable way to trade off explanation complexity for fidelity, which is valuable for debugging and trusting multi-label models in high-stakes applications. Pooling covariance across labels outperforms per-label alternatives, especially when labels are numerous and positive examples are scarce. The magnitude of each discriminant weight vector bounds its label's separability, flagging labels that may be predicted correctly for the wrong reasons, and FLEX's in-sample fidelity matches or leads Binary Relevance alternatives once a modest feature budget is allowed.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Multi-label classification assigns multiple non-exclusive labels to each instance, and explainable AI (XAI) aims to make such black-box models interpretable. Global surrogate models approximate a black-box model's behavior with an inherently interpretable model, while Fisher Linear Discriminant Analysis (LDA) is a classical dimensionality reduction and classification technique that finds linear combinations of features maximizing class separation. FLEX combines these ideas by fitting a single Fisher LDA to the entire label matrix, producing signed attributions that indicate both the direction and importance of each feature for each label.

## [DGF-Asym adaptively weights peers for robust collective estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22532) ⭐️ 6.0/10

DGF-Asym is a decentralized collective estimation method that extends the DeGroot-Friedkin model with asymmetric peer weighting based on retrospective predictive performance. It enables consensus formation without prior sensor-noise statistics or explicit fault models while keeping inter-robot communication asynchronous, and in simulated collective navigation it produced more stable estimates than three baselines at the cost of longer decision times. This work offers a model-free, asynchronous approach to collective estimation that could improve fault tolerance in multi-robot systems without requiring detailed noise or fault models. It is relevant to swarm robotics and decentralized sensing applications where robots must balance personal observations with unreliable peer information. The method's overall performance depends on the fault ratio, group size, and influence-threshold selection, and validation was limited to simulated collective navigation. The approach trades longer decision times for more stable estimates and more reliable maintenance of the decision criterion compared to baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: The DeGroot-Friedkin model describes how individuals in a network update their opinions and self-weights over a sequence of discussions, and it is widely used to study consensus formation in multi-agent systems. Collective estimation requires robots to combine their own sensor readings with information from peers, which becomes challenging when sensors are noisy or faulty. DGF-Asym builds on this model by adaptively weighting peers based on how well their past predictions performed, without needing to know noise statistics or fault types in advance.

A new arXiv concept paper (2512.15783v5) introduces a measurement standardization framework that compresses expert-AI interactions into eight structured, comparable fields to enable prospective risk detection in deployed AI systems without access to model internals. The paper outlines a staged research program with a statistical protocol including paired bootstrap inference, DeLong's test, a non-inferiority margin of 0.05, and Holm-Bonferroni correction, and demonstrates a minimal application to a published expert-AI corpus. The framework defines a grammar of eight interaction fields and a statistical protocol with paired bootstrap inference, DeLong's test for paired AUCs as a sensitivity check, a pre-specified one-sided non-inferiority margin of 0.05, and Holm-Bonferroni correction. A minimal application to a published expert-AI corpus shows the judge reproduces policy and evidential alignment scores across two runs, but judge reliability at scale remains to be validated in future work.
