A new arXiv paper (2606.16084v5) analyzes 1,952 sperm-whale codas using frozen audio encoders and direct spectral/envelope measurements, showing that click waveforms carry information beyond click timing. The study reveals a two-tier acoustic organization: within codas, click-form composition and spacing improve prediction of whole-coda form, while across coda sequences, the second preceding coda form improves prediction beyond the immediately preceding one. It also demonstrates a novel application of frozen audio encoders to bioacoustics, which could inspire similar machine-learning approaches in animal communication research. The analysis uses frozen audio encoders to identify recurring click and coda forms independently, then validates differences with spectral and envelope measurements. While the second-preceding coda form improves prediction beyond first-order references, stricter controls do not establish a general higher-order rule, and click-form order does not improve whole-coda classification.

## [PhysAlign Benchmark Tests Whether Multimodal Models Assign Diagram Elements Correctly](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33319) ⭐️ 6.0/10

PhysAlign introduces a benchmark of 3,341 human-validated probes across 986 physics problems to evaluate whether multimodal models correctly assign recognized diagram elements to their intended physical roles. It also proposes five complementary metrics, including CAcc, GAcc, and JAcc, to separate recognition failures from correspondence errors. The benchmark reveals a consistent gap between local visual recognition and physical-role grounding: even when content is correctly recognized, the conditional correspondence error rate is 13.8% for GPT-6-Astra and about 50.6% for InternVL3.5-8B. This shows that strong perception alone does not ensure reliable physical interpretation, exposing a grounding bottleneck hidden by answer-level accuracy. The benchmark uses localized probes and controlled variants to isolate correspondence errors from recognition failures, and its five metrics assess diagram content recognition, correct physical correspondences, and problem solving. The large gap across models suggests that role-assignment errors are a distinct failure mode not captured by standard answer accuracy.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Multimodal physics reasoning requires models to interpret diagrams, such as circuit schematics or free-body diagrams, and connect visual elements to physical entities, relations, and conditions. Existing benchmarks like SeePhys and PhysReason evaluate end-to-end problem solving, but answer-level accuracy can mask whether a model truly grounds recognized visual evidence in the correct physical meaning. PhysAlign specifically targets this role-assignment step by disentangling it from basic visual recognition.

## [Staged pipeline screens LLM judges without ground truth for dental coach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24335) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.24335v2) presents a staged pipeline that screens candidate LLM judges and fine-tuned student models before expert review, using explicit decision rules at each stage: judge output audit, similarity with hosted judges, bias checks, and decision robustness. Demonstrated on an AI dental hygiene coach, two fine-tuned small models (Granite-4.1-8B and Qwen3-8B) were scored on seven metrics by five judges, with local judge Qwen3.8-27B found most similar to hosted judges overall. This work addresses a key bottleneck in LLM-as-a-judge evaluation: the lack of ground truth makes judge reliability uncertain, and expert grading is slow and costly. If validated, such staged screening could enable cheaper, more trustworthy automated evaluation in specialized domains like healthcare coaching, though the current study is preliminary and uses synthetic data. Two local judges (Qwen3.8-27B and Gemma-4-31B-it) were not distinguishable from hosted-judge agreement on six of seven metrics by rank correlation, and no judge favored its own model family or training-data source. The student comparison did not separate Granite-4.1-8B and Qwen3-8B under the pipeline's rule, and the lean toward Granite depends partly on truncated Qwen3-8B outputs; the pipeline also surfaced hallucination flags from one hosted judge that no other judge corroborated.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: LLM-as-a-judge is an evaluation method where a large language model scores outputs of another model, offering scalability compared to human evaluation. However, judges can be biased and their reliability is hard to establish without ground-truth labels. Fine-tuning small language models like Granite-4.1-8B and Qwen3-8B is common for domain-specific tasks, but evaluating them requires either expensive expert review or trustworthy automated judges.

## [Grassroots Contracts Proposed to Mitigate AI Risks on Digital Platforms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06374) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.06374v2) proposes 'grassroots contracts'—freely adopted social contracts realized via person-to-person platforms—as a decentralized approach to mitigate risks from AI-controlled global platforms, anonymous AI agents, and unattributed deepfakes. The authors developed, with AI assistance, a methodology to compile these contracts into working smartphone apps, presenting two example contracts: one for a social graph with accountable AI agents and one for a social network with digital provenance. This work offers a novel governance mechanism that shifts control from centralized platforms to user-owned communities, potentially empowering individuals to hold AI agents and content creators accountable. If viable, it could influence how digital platforms address AI risks, offering an alternative to top-down regulation. The contracts introduce accountability for AI agents by making manufacturers responsible for defects and operators for reckless use, similar to cars, and ensure every item carries signatures of its author and forwarders to attribute harmful content. However, the abstract is truncated, the idea is speculative with no empirical validation, and the authors acknowledge much work remains for grassroots apps to compete with incumbent global platforms.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Grassroots contracts draw on Pierre-Joseph Proudhon's 19th-century notion of a social contract that individuals can freely adopt without surrendering sovereignty, and on grassroots platforms—person-to-person systems owned and governed by their participants. The paper addresses AI risks such as global platforms ceding control to AI, anonymous AI agents masquerading as humans, and unattributed deepfakes that people cannot distinguish from genuine content.

## [Information Limits of Replay-Free Recurrent State Migration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19345) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper characterizes the minimum continuous causal memory needed to migrate the state of a recurrent model when the replacement model's directions are unknown, showing that at most kd extra coordinates suffice for a controllable d-state model with scalar input, and that a single rank-one direction can require all kd. This work provides a theoretical foundation for understanding what information must be preserved when swapping recurrent models, which is relevant for model updates, continual learning, and deployment scenarios where replaying history is infeasible. The analysis uses a time-invariant affine linear family and relates the minimum memory to the reachable dimension of a lifted coefficient system minus anchor state information; experiments include a small language model where equal-memory cubic interpolation improves state fidelity but not predictive performance, and with repeated slow poles, eight history coordinates achieve normalized output error 10^-4 across five seeds.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Recurrent neural networks (RNNs) maintain a hidden state that summarizes past inputs, enabling sequence processing. When replacing one RNN with another, the old state may not be directly compatible with the new model, so migration requires retaining additional information about the history. This paper studies the fundamental limits of such state migration without replaying the original input sequence.

<a id="item-317"></a>
## [LULR: Regularizing Treatment Leakage in LLM-based Uplift Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20794) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper proposes LULR, a regularization method that penalizes mutual information between the LLM's shared representation and treatment assignment to prevent shortcut learning in uplift estimation. It uses a variational approximation to the contrastive log-ratio upper bound (CLUB) of mutual information and is validated with proxy experiments and an industrial pricing randomized controlled trial. Treatment leakage can cause LLM-based uplift models to learn spurious correlations that fail in deployment, so LULR offers a targeted fix that could improve the reliability of causal effect estimation in real-world applications like pricing and marketing. The industrial RCT results suggest practical benefits, with higher offline lift and online sales volume compared to baselines. LULR keeps the LLM encoder frozen and penalizes dependence between the causal head's trainable shared representation and treatment while retaining separate outcome heads. On an industrial pricing RCT, mean offline lift@0.3 is 0.258 for LULR versus 0.106 for the same-backbone baseline, and a one-week online A/B test of the full pricing policy shows 5.45% higher sales volume and 1.91% higher GMV, though these gains do not isolate the regularizer's effect.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Uplift estimation aims to predict the incremental effect of a treatment (e.g., a price change) on an outcome, which is crucial for personalized decision-making. Large language models (LLMs) can provide rich semantic features for uplift models, but if the text contains signals that reveal the treatment assignment, models may learn shortcuts that do not generalize. Treatment leakage refers to this phenomenon, where text features are predictive of treatment status and can introduce bias. Mutual information measures dependence between variables, and CLUB is an upper bound used to estimate and penalize such dependence.

## [TAR: Temporal Anchor-Constrained Reasoning for Video Temporal Grounding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.07683) ⭐️ 6.0/10

TAR introduces a temporal anchor (T-anchor) mechanism that acts as a transparent checkpoint within chain-of-thought reasoning, forcing the model to ground intermediate thoughts in visual evidence and progressively refine temporal predictions. It also presents a bootstrapping paradigm that automatically harvests high-quality CoT data using only a standard 7B model, eliminating the need for ultra-large models or separate reward models. This approach reduces hallucinations in video temporal grounding by making the reasoning process faithful and auditable, which could improve trustworthiness and accuracy in vision-language tasks. It also lowers the computational cost of generating high-quality reasoning data, making advanced reasoning more accessible to researchers with limited resources. TAR achieves state-of-the-art performance on video temporal grounding benchmarks and generates progressively refined reasoning traces, but the abstract does not specify the exact benchmarks or the magnitude of improvement. The bootstrapping paradigm uses a 7B model to harvest CoT data, which may still require careful filtering to ensure data quality.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Video Temporal Grounding (VTG) is the task of localizing a specific segment in a video that corresponds to a natural language query. Recent Large Vision-Language Models (LVLMs) use Reinforcement Learning to generate Chains-of-Thought (CoT) for such tasks, but they often rely only on outcome-based supervision, leading to hallucinations where the reasoning is disconnected from the visual content. TAR addresses this by introducing temporal anchors as checkpoints within the CoT to enforce grounding and progressive refinement.

## [ACC-SGD-IE corrects cross-epoch bias in SGD influence estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.26185) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces ACC-SGD-IE, an accumulative correction to the standard multi-epoch SGD-Influence Estimator (SGD-IE) that accounts for interactions between repeated removals of the same sample. It uses the sample's Hessian at subsequent appearances to correct the estimated parameter change, and non-convex analysis shows the correction removes a linear interaction term from the finite-horizon error bound. More accurate influence estimates can improve data selection and cleansing, leading to better model performance and lower misclassification rates after removing harmful examples. This work addresses a known bias in multi-epoch influence estimation, which is important for reliable data attribution in deep learning. Experiments on clean and noisy tabular, text, and vision datasets show that ACC-SGD-IE reduces validation-loss-change RMSE by 10.8% and improves Jaccard@10% by 15.2% on average relative to SGD-IE. On noisy industrial datasets, it reduces aggregate parameter RMS error with a mean relative reduction of 53.4%, and gains translate to lower test misclassification rates after data cleansing on MNIST and CIFAR10.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Influence estimation aims to predict how removing a training example affects a model's predictions, supporting data debugging and cleansing. The SGD-Influence Estimator approximates leave-one-out influence by aggregating gradient-based contributions along the SGD trajectory, but in multi-epoch training it sums one-epoch approximations and ignores interactions between repeated removals of the same sample. This paper corrects that bias using Hessian information at later appearances of the sample.

## [Map-Free Trajectory Prediction via Goal-Conditioned Expert-Routed Transformer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.02368) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces a map-free trajectory prediction framework centered on a Goal-Conditioned Expert-Routed Transformer (GCERT), which uses a lightweight intra-agent front-end to decompose historical motion into a smooth trend and local detail residual via centered moving average, and employs dual-level expert routing (edge-level relation experts and node-level state experts) to model inter-agent interactions without HD maps. Map-free prediction improves deployment flexibility by removing dependence on high-definition maps, which are costly to maintain and often unavailable in real-world scenarios, so this work could make autonomous driving systems more scalable and adaptable to new environments. The model predicts motion goals that update node representations and construct prospective relative-geometry and orientation edge features before the final interaction layer, conditioning attention, relation-specific message generation, and state transformation; it is jointly optimized with trajectory-regression, mode-classification, and goal-prediction objectives, but no code or community discussion is available.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Trajectory prediction is essential for safe planning in autonomous driving, and many methods rely on high-definition (HD) maps that provide road topology and lane connectivity. Map-free approaches remove this dependency but must recover motion cues from short histories and infer interactions without lane-level guidance. The Transformer architecture, which uses attention mechanisms to model relationships in sequence data, is commonly used for this task, and expert routing is a technique that dynamically selects specialized sub-networks for different inputs.

## [OPAL: CPU-Resident Graph Engine for Persistent Memory Across Reasoning Sessions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.16736) ⭐️ 6.0/10

OPAL, a CPU-resident C++17 graph engine, maintains persistent application state across reasoning sessions, achieving a median resident-update latency of 0.083 microseconds on Apple Silicon M2 Pro hardware with up to 5 million retained Atomic Memory Vertices (AMVs). The paper introduces Bounded Local Generator Classes (BLGC) as a mathematical foundation and reports extensive reliability tests, including byte-identical checkpoints and passing Python SDK and Model Context Protocol interface checks. This work separates persistent memory from probabilistic reasoning, allowing applications to keep accepted state available while models are idle and only invoke them when interpretation, planning, or generation is needed. It could benefit AI systems that require durable, low-latency memory across sessions, though its niche systems focus limits immediate impact on mainstream software engineering. The reported 0.083 microsecond P50 covers complete resident-update calls including validation, indexed lookup, and numerical state mutation, while initialization and storage-growth costs are reported separately. Reliability tests verified event replay, conflict rejection, concurrent calls, allocation-failure handling, and fresh-process recovery, with engine-version comparisons producing byte-identical checkpoints.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Persistent memory in AI systems typically refers to storing state across sessions so that an application can resume without recomputing everything. OPAL is a graph engine that stores records and relationships as vertices in memory, using Atomic Memory Vertices (AMVs) as its core data unit. Bounded Local Generator Classes (BLGC) provide a formal model for how local state evolves in a bounded way, ensuring consistency. The Model Context Protocol (MCP) is an interface standard for connecting AI models to external tools and data.

A vision paper by Fekete, Wu, Moritz and colleagues surveys how foundation models like LLMs and VLMs are reshaping human-data interaction, exploration, and visualization systems. ## [PEARL: Contrastive Percentile Estimation Debias for Livestream Recs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.21752) ⭐️ 6.0/10

PEARL introduces a nonparametric contrastive percentile approximation framework that models relative preference signals instead of absolute engagement magnitudes to address behavioral intensity imbalance in recommender systems. The method provides theoretical justification for unbiased percentile estimates and is deployed on a production livestream platform with billions of users, yielding online A/B gains of +2.10% watch duration, +0.80% consumption amount, +1.49% interaction rate, and -6.91% report rate. Behavioral intensity imbalance causes models to over-amplify signals from highly active users, degrading recommendation quality and fairness at scale. PEARL's debiasing approach, validated in a large-scale production environment, offers a practical solution for industrial recommender systems and highlights the importance of relative preference modeling. PEARL leverages real contrastive interaction samples to approximate percentile relationships directly, avoiding auxiliary distribution estimation models. It also introduces a prediction-based bootstrapping mechanism for percentile smoothing to handle sparse and discrete feedback, along with a generalized value-weighted formulation and a co-training strategy.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Recommender systems trained on user interaction data often suffer from biases such as popularity bias and selection bias, where observed interactions do not faithfully reflect true preferences. Contrastive learning has emerged as a technique to reduce such biases by learning from augmented views or pairwise comparisons. Nonparametric methods avoid strong distributional assumptions, making them suitable for complex real-world data. Behavioral intensity imbalance specifically refers to heterogeneous engagement patterns across users, which skews feedback signals.

## [SFMambaNet uses frequency-aware Mamba for two-view correspondence pruning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.04493) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose SFMambaNet, a Mamba-based network that scans candidate matches cluster by cluster and applies learnable frequency-domain gates between forward and backward passes. It outperforms spatial-domain Mamba baselines with about two million parameters on camera pose estimation, outlier removal, and homography estimation. This work shows that frequency-domain filtering is a natural way to capture agreement among correspondences, potentially improving robustness in 3D vision pipelines. The findings may also inform other sequence models that must process unordered sets, such as point clouds and graphs. Contrary to expectations, a parameter-free low-pass filter outperforms point-wise layers as large as the learned gates, and the learned gates spontaneously become low-pass, making the scan less sensitive to match shuffling. The method is evaluated on standard benchmarks but remains an incremental architectural improvement in a specialized subfield.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Two-view correspondence learning, or correspondence pruning, aims to identify correct matches between two images, which is crucial for tasks like camera pose estimation and homography estimation. Mamba is a state space model that offers linear-time sequence modeling and has recently been applied to correspondence pruning, but it typically treats matches as a plain sequence. Frequency-domain analysis, which decomposes signals into slow and fast variations, provides a way to distinguish correct matches (which vary slowly) from wrong ones (which are erratic).

## [Localized LoRA-MoE combines spatial blocking with adaptive routing to reduce gradient interference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05114) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces Localized LoRA-MoE, a framework that merges localized spatial blocking with context-conditioned expert routing to mitigate gradient interference in parameter-efficient fine-tuning. It proposes two variants: Block-Wise LoRA-MoE with centralized routing and Cell-Wise LoRA-MoE with decentralized per-cell routing, and shows gains over LoRA and static localized adapters on structured transformation benchmarks, six interleaved GLUE tasks, and eight commonsense benchmarks with Qwen2.5-1.5B. Gradient interference is a known weakness of LoRA when training streams interleave multiple contexts, causing shared adapter weights to collapse into an unspecialized average. This work offers a routing-based remedy that maintains parameter efficiency and could improve multi-task and continual fine-tuning for practitioners using PEFT. Cell-wise routing is strictly better than block-wise routing when contexts are spatially heterogeneous, while both perform similarly under uniform context shifts. At decoder scale on Qwen2.5-1.5B, cell-wise routing achieves +3.9 points over LoRA at matched active capacity, and task-conditioned routing matches per-task adapters with 6x budgets on GLUE.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: LoRA is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning technique that freezes pre-trained model weights and injects small trainable low-rank matrices into layers, greatly reducing the number of trainable parameters. Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) uses multiple expert networks and a gating function to route inputs to a few experts, dividing the problem space into homogeneous regions. Gradient interference occurs when conflicting updates to shared adapter weights from different contexts degrade performance, and prior block-wise isolation methods use static topologies that cannot adapt to context switching.

## [Physics-informed dual-LTC network enables real-time fall detection on edge devices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12909) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2607.12909v2) proposes a physics-informed fall detection framework that models falling as a stability-loss event in a coupled dynamical system. The framework uses a dual-LTC architecture with Center-of-Mass and Base-of-Support subsystems, each implemented as a Liquid Time-Constant neural network, coupled via a learnable module and classified by a Stability Manifold classifier using Lyapunov-inspired metrics. This approach departs from conventional CNN-RNN pipelines by encoding continuous-time mechanical inertia, yielding a sub-50K-parameter network that can run in real time on resource-constrained edge devices. If validated, it could enable low-cost, physically interpretable fall detection for elderly care and smart surveillance. The architecture supports a three-state prediction paradigm (Normal, Falling, Fallen), but this preliminary study only validates two-class discrimination (Normal vs. Falling), leaving full temporal transition to future work. Additional features include counterfactual trajectory projection and Time-to-Collision estimation for irreversibility assessment and early warning.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Falls are a major health risk for the elderly, and vision-based detection is attractive because it requires no wearable sensors. Most existing methods treat fall detection as static pose classification or discrete temporal pattern matching, ignoring the underlying instability dynamics. Liquid Time-Constant (LTC) networks are a type of continuous-time recurrent neural network that can model dynamical systems with adaptive time constants, making them suitable for physics-informed modeling. The center of mass (CoM) and base of support (BoS) are biomechanical concepts: stability requires the CoM to remain within the BoS.

## [OptCar specializes generalist vehicle models for high-speed MPC across terrains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13319) ⭐️ 6.0/10

OptCar introduces a transformer-based forward kinodynamic (FKD) model that uses FiLM conditioning on a single dynamics context token to specialize a generalist vehicle model for high-speed model predictive control (MPC) across terrains. In closed-loop experiments, it reduces 6 m/s trajectory tracking error by roughly 55% on vegetation + dirt compared to a fine-tuned baseline, and with only 5 minutes of real data per terrain it matches a specialist trained on 30 minutes of road data. This work addresses a key challenge in off-road autonomy: adapting a generalist dynamics model to a specific vehicle with limited real-world data while preserving cross-terrain generalization. The significant error reduction at high speed, where slip dominates, could enable more reliable high-speed off-road navigation and reduce the data collection burden for deploying autonomous vehicles in new environments. OptCar uses FiLM to condition multi-step predictions on a single dynamics context token summarizing recent state-action history, and specializes the model with limited real-world data plus targeted synthetic rollouts from environment-specific system identification. It remains the most accurate even when an unseen cart payload changes the dynamics, and the largest gains appear at 6 m/s, the highest speed evaluated.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Forward kinodynamic (FKD) models predict how a vehicle will move given its state and control inputs, and they are essential for model predictive control (MPC), a control strategy that optimizes future actions by simulating the system forward. FiLM (Feature-wise Linear Modulation) is a general-purpose conditioning method that modulates neural network activations via a learned affine transformation based on external context, allowing a single model to adapt its behavior. Recent foundation models for FKD aim to provide a generalist starting point that can be specialized to specific vehicles and terrains, but specialization often requires large amounts of real-world data and can overfit to particular conditions.

## [RecHarness: Bandit-Routed Agentic Harness for Recommender Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29241) ⭐️ 6.0/10

RecHarness introduces a bandit-routed agentic harness that decouples modification-direction selection from LLM-generated hypotheses, using a multi-armed bandit router to pick the next optimization direction based on historical validation feedback while the LLM generates concrete hypotheses and code edits within that direction. A jump-basin mechanism activates a structural-jump arm when local edits stagnate, and a 7-day online A/B test on a large-scale short-video advertising platform showed ADVV +2.084%, Revenue +0.534%, and Exposure +0.559%. This work addresses the instability of LLM-directed search under limited trial budgets by combining bandit-based exploration with LLM code generation, offering a more sample-efficient approach to automating recommender model optimization. The positive online A/B results on a large-scale advertising platform suggest practical value for industrial recommender systems where manual iteration is costly. The bandit router selects among modification directions using historical validation feedback, while the LLM only formulates hypotheses and code edits within the chosen direction; the jump-basin mechanism triggers a structural-jump arm to escape local optima when edits stagnate. The approach is evaluated across multiple recommendation tasks, datasets, and model backbones, and code is available at https://github.com/6lyc/RecHarness.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Recommender models are typically optimized through manual iteration over architectures, objectives, and training strategies, which is labor-intensive and requires expert knowledge. LLM-based agents have been proposed to automate this trial-and-error process, but relying on LLMs for both direction selection and hypothesis formulation can lead to unstable search when trial budgets are limited. Multi-armed bandit algorithms provide a principled framework for balancing exploration and exploitation in sequential decision-making under uncertainty, making them a natural fit for routing optimization directions.

## [Arabic LLM Safety Alignment Should Be Measured as a Selective-Refusal Trade-off](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29378) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper evaluates refusal-only SFT, mixed SFT, DPO, and inference guards across five Arabic-capable models on the human-written AraSafe benchmark, running 130 experiments. It finds that refusal-only SFT collapses into blanket refusal, while selected mixed-SFT configurations achieve harmful-prompt refusal (H) of 90–93% at benign refusal (B) of 14–23%, with four configurations exceeding the 90% H target in all three runs. The work reframes Arabic safety alignment as a two-sided trade-off between refusing harmful prompts and over-refusing benign ones, rather than a single refusal rate. This matters for multilingual safety practitioners because it shows that DPO and inference guards are not uniform upgrades and that deployment operating points must be chosen per model. In a blinded audit of 300 responses, annotator binary-refusal agreement was 89.0% (kappa = 0.78), and Qwen3Guard and Aya Expanse 32B reached 88.7% and 91.0% accuracy respectively with no conclusive paired difference. Selected SFT raised H on Arabizi for all five models, but none reached 90%, indicating only partial transfer from Modern Standard Arabic.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: AraSafe is the first large-scale native Arabic safety benchmark, containing 12K human-written Arabic prompts across domains and dialects annotated into nine safety categories. Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) is a standard alignment method that trains models on pairs of preferred and dispreferred responses without a separate reward model. Qwen3Guard is a series of multilingual safety moderation models built on Qwen3, available in generative and streaming variants.

## [Frozen audio encoders compare vocal structure across four species](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.16612) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper uses frozen pretrained audio encoders with matched sampling to compare acoustic-event diversity and sequence dependence across sperm-whale codas, human speech phones, Bengalese-finch syllables, and common-marmoset calls, using 1,952 events and 1,700 adjacent pairs per comparison. It finds that whale codas show the fastest accumulation of encoder-defined acoustic groups, while finch syllables show the strongest repeated-sequence recurrence against within-block shuffles. This work demonstrates that frozen audio representations can serve as a common yardstick for comparing vocal organization across species with different native units and recording conditions, potentially enabling more rigorous cross-disciplinary bioacoustics and speech research. It also highlights how representation learning can reveal structural differences that raw waveform measurements alone may not capture. The comparison uses matched sampling to give each corpus equal opportunities to observe new acoustic forms without changing its native event definition, and a source-and-position control shows closely matched neighboring-event dependence. A second preceding event improves held-out prediction more favorably for whale than for other corpora, though whale's standalone improvement remains uncertain, and waveform measurements recover several differences while duration and acquisition-sensitive properties also contribute.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Sperm whale codas are patterned click sequences used in social communication and thought to be shaped by vocal learning within matrilineal clans. Bengalese finch syllables are learned song units arranged in sequences, while marmoset calls and human speech phones represent other forms of vocal organization. Frozen pretrained audio encoders are neural networks trained on large audio datasets whose weights are kept fixed, providing general-purpose high-dimensional representations that can be applied to new audio without task-specific training.

## [SkillPE evolves cinematic skills to enhance text-to-video prompts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34335) ⭐️ 6.0/10

SkillPE introduces a creativity-oriented prompt-enhancement framework that starts from 20 expert-provided seed skills and evolves reusable cinematic skills through reference-guided exploration using resonators, dissonants, and divergents. Experiments on StoryEval and VBench show improvements of up to 1.10 points over seed skills and 1.02 points over the strongest external baseline on a 7-point four-dimensional evaluation scale. This work addresses the gap between non-expert prompts and professional cinematic quality in text-to-video generation, offering a practical way to balance prompt fidelity and creativity. It could benefit AI/ML video-generation researchers and creators seeking more diverse, high-quality outputs without deep cinematic expertise. The framework categorizes movie references into resonators (good matches), dissonants (weak matches), and divergents (creatively inspiring near-misses), where resonators and dissonants refine skill applicability and execution while divergents inspire alternative realizations through controlled mutations that preserve user intent. Candidate skills are assessed on prompt fidelity, cinematic quality, narrative appeal, and creativity via generated videos to construct final skill libraries.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Text-to-video (T2V) generation models are sensitive to input prompts, and non-expert users often struggle to write prompts with professional narrative and creative design. Prompt enhancement (PE) methods aim to automatically improve prompts, but existing approaches and static cinematic skills offer limited ability to explore diverse creative realizations. SkillPE builds on the idea of evolving reusable skills from expert cinematic knowledge to guide prompt enhancement.

## [PILOT: Learning When to Retrain Time Series Forecasters from Forecast Errors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.39789) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose PILOT (Pseudo-label-Informed Learned Online Trigger), an online retraining framework that decides when to retrain a time series forecasting model by learning from forecast-error dynamics rather than from indirect drift alarms or staleness indicators. Since ground-truth retraining labels are unavailable, PILOT constructs a pseudo-label from future increases in forecast error and trains a lightweight scorer to predict it from observed error states. Evaluated on eight benchmarks with DLinear, iTransformer, and TimesNet backbones, PILOT achieves state-of-the-art average-rank performance among retraining policies while maintaining a favorable performance-efficiency trade-off. In real deployments, retraining is costly, so deciding when to retrain is as important as how to retrain; PILOT offers a direct, deployment-feedback-driven policy that plugs into arbitrary forecasting backbones without architectural changes. This could reduce unnecessary retraining costs and improve forecasting reliability under non-stationary data streams, benefiting practitioners who operate online forecasting systems with limited resources. PILOT uses only completed forecast errors at deployment and serves as a plug-in module for any forecasting backbone without architectural modification. The evaluation covers standard multivariate forecasting settings across eight benchmarks with three representative backbones (DLinear, iTransformer, TimesNet), but the approach relies on pseudo-labels derived from future error increases, which may introduce label noise or lag in detecting abrupt changes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Time series forecasting models are trained on historical data, but real-world data streams are non-stationary: the statistical properties of the target variable can change over time, a phenomenon known as concept drift, which degrades forecast accuracy. Retraining the model can recover performance but incurs computational and operational costs, so a key challenge is deciding when to retrain. Existing policies often rely on indirect signals such as drift detection alarms or model staleness, whereas PILOT uses realized forecast errors as direct feedback. Pseudo-labeling is a semi-supervised technique where automatically generated labels substitute for unavailable ground truth, and here it is used to create retraining decision labels from future error increases.

Researchers introduced SarcasTraps, a 479-entry Turkish test set annotated for both hate speech and sarcasm, and evaluated fine-tuned BERTurk and XLM-RoBERTa models as well as zero-shot GPT-4.1 and Llama-3.3-70B on it. Results show that sarcasm substantially affects hate speech detection: fine-tuned encoders lose recall on sarcastic hate, while zero-shot LLMs maintain higher recall but suffer substantial precision drops. This work highlights a critical blind spot in hate speech detection systems: indirect expressions like sarcasm can evade both traditional fine-tuned models and modern LLMs, potentially allowing harmful content to spread undetected. It underscores the need for more robust, context-aware moderation tools, especially for under-resourced languages like Turkish. SarcasTraps is a targeted external test set used strictly for evaluation, not training, and contains only 479 entries, which limits its statistical power. ## [STOPD: Random Rollout Truncation with IPW Correction Accelerates On-Policy Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21337) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces Stochastic Truncated On-Policy Distillation (STOPD), which randomly truncates student rollouts before generation and uses blockwise inverse-probability weighting (IPW) to correct the resulting sampling bias. It proves that this weighting preserves Vanilla OPD's gradient sum in expectation and derives one-step update-error bounds for SGD, Adam, and Muon. On-policy distillation is computationally expensive due to full rollout generation and per-token teacher feedback, so STOPD's ability to reduce generated tokens by 25-48% while maintaining accuracy and achieving 1.3-2.2x speedups could make LLM training more efficient. This is particularly relevant for specialists in efficient LLM training who need to scale distillation without changing the original objective. STOPD samples a stopping position for each rollout before generation and lets the student generate until that position or natural termination, obtaining teacher feedback on every generated token. The blockwise IPW compensates for the lower probability of retaining later tokens while controlling the weights, and the method is evaluated across multiple teacher-student model pairs, task domains, and optimizers.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: On-policy distillation (OPD) trains a student model using token-level feedback from a teacher on rollouts generated by the student itself, combining on-policy reinforcement learning with dense supervision. Inverse probability weighting (IPW) is a statistical technique that reweights samples to correct for selection bias when the sampling distribution differs from the target distribution. Existing truncation methods save computation by stopping rollouts early but do not compensate for truncated token gradients, biasing gradient estimates and altering the original training objective.

## [REGER: Modeling Inter-Item Relations with Semantic IDs for Generative Recommendation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37584) ⭐️ 6.0/10

REGER proposes a unified framework that explicitly incorporates inter-item semantic and collaborative relations into semantic pretraining, history encoding, and SID generation for generative recommendation. It pretrains the GR model via generative semantic retrieval, combines global and local attention aligned with SID boundaries, and formalizes item-level preference distillation on the SID prefix tree with an exact factorization of item preference distributions. This work addresses a key limitation in generative recommendation where randomly initialized SID token embeddings and models trained from scratch fail to inherit semantic relations, potentially improving recommendation accuracy and efficiency. It is relevant to researchers in recommendation systems and could influence future designs of semantic ID-based generative recommenders. REGER introduces generative semantic retrieval pretraining to inherit inter-item semantic similarity, combines global attention with local attention aligned to SID boundaries to capture historical context and directed transitions, and derives an exact factorization of item preference distributions into prefix conditionals with a weighted decomposition of item-level KL divergence into conditional KL terms. Experiments on real-world datasets show consistent outperformance over existing GR baselines while retaining efficient inference.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Generative recommendation (GR) is a paradigm that casts recommendation as a generative task, autoregressively generating discrete semantic IDs (SIDs) of items. SIDs are compact representations derived from item content, and GR models learn to generate them based on user history. Inter-item relations, both semantic (content similarity) and collaborative (co-occurrence patterns), are crucial for personalized recommendation but are often learned implicitly in GR models.

## [Cassette-Editor unifies human and AI video editing with shared timeline semantics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37690) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Cassette-Editor introduces an agent harness that combines manual user gestures and automated agent patches in a shared video editing environment, using a common action language, history, and validation rulebook. Evaluated with GPT-6 Luna, the full harness passed 92/93 scored request checks across 31 authored requests on two interview documentaries, compared to 48/93 for a basic-tools baseline. This work addresses the challenge of keeping automated video editing agents grounded and reliable as projects evolve, potentially enabling more seamless collaboration between humans and AI in creative workflows. The shared action language and validation rules could reduce errors in complex timeline edits and improve trust in agent-assisted editing. The system uses a state executor to automatically derive downstream consequences of edits (e.g., shifting later clips and titles after a trim) and a validation rulebook that enforces structural invariants and returns localized diagnostics for repair. Ablations show that disabling the executor reduced passed trials from 230/232 to 199/232 on edits with dependent timeline elements, and disabling rulebook validation and repair lowered passed trials from 46/48 to 10/48 on requests near structural validity boundaries.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: An agent harness is the software infrastructure around a large language model that manages tool use, memory, state, and feedback loops, effectively turning a model into an agent. Video editing timelines consist of interconnected tracks where a simple trim requires coordinated updates across later clips, attached titles, and transitions, making automated editing challenging. Structural invariants are rules that must always hold true in a system, and validation rulebooks enforce these constraints to prevent invalid states.

## [Multi-Estimate Elicitation Does Not Uniformly Debias Language Model Judgments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00597) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A large preregistered study (arXiv:2611.00597) with over 31,000 evaluation units finds that Multi-Candidate Verbalization (MCV) and weight elicitation do not uniformly reduce bias in language model judgments. The effects vary by task, model, and response scale, and in some cases, eliciting weights increases errors by 2-4 percentage points. This challenges the assumption that multi-estimate elicitation is a reliable debiasing method for LLM judgments, which is important for researchers and practitioners working on LLM evaluation, calibration, and decision-making. It suggests that debiasing interventions must be carefully validated across contexts rather than assumed to be universally effective. The study distinguishes format validity, numerical accuracy, and psychological susceptibility, and finds that within-generation weighting changes errors by only 0.05-0.07 percentage points, a 42x-58x smaller effect than eliciting weights. Smaller response scales lower string-match rates but increase Bayesian error, and unconstrained joint generation with post-hoc normalization offers only a local normalized-error improvement without confirmed accuracy gains.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Multi-Candidate Verbalization (MCV) is an output protocol where a language model generates multiple candidate answers or estimates, which are then aggregated to produce a final judgment. Weight elicitation asks the model to assign weights to these candidates, potentially improving calibration. Preregistered studies pre-specify hypotheses and analysis plans to reduce researcher degrees of freedom, and the smallest effect size of interest (SESOI) is the minimum effect size considered practically or theoretically meaningful.

<a id="item-339"></a>
## [Survey Organizes Malware Clustering Research into Seven Categories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02380) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv survey (2611.02380v2) taxonomizes existing malware clustering research into seven methodological categories: centroid-based, hierarchical-based, density-based, distribution-based, mixed-type, fuzzy, and AI-driven approaches. It also catalogs publicly available malware datasets commonly used in clustering studies and examines the feature representations derived from them. This survey addresses the fragmentation in malware clustering literature by providing a unified taxonomy and identifying gaps, which can help researchers new to the field navigate prior work and guide future research directions. It also highlights how AI-driven methods may shape large-scale and reproducible analysis, potentially influencing both academic research and industry malware analysis practices. The survey covers seven categories of clustering algorithms and summarizes public datasets and feature representations, but as a literature review it does not introduce novel methods or empirical results. Its impact is therefore primarily organizational and educational rather than groundbreaking.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Malware clustering is an unsupervised learning technique used to group similar malware samples based on their features, such as behavioral traces or static code properties, to discover families and variants without prior labels. It is critical for analysts facing thousands of new samples daily, as it helps prioritize analysis and identify emerging threats. The field has evolved from early behavior-based approaches using dynamic analysis and taint tracking to modern AI-driven methods, but research remains scattered across different algorithms and evaluation metrics.

<a id="item-340"></a>
## [TIBF: Brier Loss Supervision for Continuous Language Flow Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07721) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper proposes Token Identity Brier Flow (TIBF), which applies squared supervision directly to token identities via the Brier loss under a Gaussian observation model, while keeping pretrained token embeddings fixed. Under matched continuation budgets, TIBF achieves mean Gen.PPL of 34.87 on OWT and 80.08 on LM1B, outperforming continuous-MSE and cross-entropy controls. This addresses a real identifiability problem in continuous language flow models: different probability distributions can map to the same probability-weighted embedding vector, making them indistinguishable under MSE reconstruction. The method offers a principled way to improve generation quality with limited additional training while preserving pretrained representations. TIBF combines network scores with token-matching scores under a Gaussian observation model, supervises token probabilities with the Brier loss, and uses probability-weighted embeddings for continuous generation. The authors show that, for fixed token embeddings, the squared error in token posterior prediction controls the squared errors of both the continuous denoising prediction and the flow velocity, and a risk decomposition separates observation uncertainty from posterior fitting error.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Continuous language flow models generate text by starting from noise and iteratively denoising in a continuous token embedding space, rather than predicting discrete tokens directly as autoregressive models do. In these models, a continuous prediction is often formed by weighting token embeddings with predicted probabilities, and training typically uses mean squared error (MSE) between predicted and target embeddings. The Brier score is a proper scoring rule that measures the mean squared difference between predicted probabilities and actual outcomes, and it is commonly used for probability calibration.

<a id="item-341"></a>
## [RACE: Uncertainty-Aware Adaptive Compression for Sequential Recommendation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18244) ⭐️ 6.0/10

RACE introduces a framework that dynamically adjusts model structures and input sequences using uncertainty-aware compression ratios to address the Capacity-Complexity Mismatch in efficient sequential recommendation. It derives a list-wise ranking-uncertainty metric from top-K prediction stability and uses a self-supervised predictor to guide instance-specific slimming via channel sensitivity extraction and progressive sequence filtering. This work moves beyond uniform compression in recommender systems by adapting computation to heterogeneous user intent complexity, potentially improving both accuracy and efficiency in resource-constrained deployments. It could benefit real-time personalized services where computational resources must be allocated wisely across diverse instances. RACE uses a lightweight predictor trained via self-supervision to anticipate ranking uncertainty from early-layer features, enabling proactive resource allocation. Experiments on four popular datasets show it outperforms state-of-the-art baselines on accuracy, efficiency, and adaptability, though the paper does not specify the datasets or compression ratios used.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 18, 05:00

**Background**: Sequential recommendation models ordered user behavior to predict future preferences, but deploying these models in real-time requires model compression to meet latency and resource constraints. Traditional compression applies a uniform ratio to all instances, ignoring that some user intents are simple while others are complex, leading to wasted or insufficient computation. Uncertainty estimation in machine learning quantifies a model's confidence in its predictions and can serve as a proxy for instance difficulty.
